2. Invisible: it picks the straggler (`straggler`: the visible living hero furthest from the heroes' centre; one hero: that one; tie: nearest to it) and phases toward it through walls. With nobody in sight it waits, unseen, until Invisible ends.
3. Within Touch reach and where it may rest (`can_rest`, never inside a wall) it appears with a warning of `AMBUSH_WARNING` (0.5 s), then touches.
4. If it has not reached its target after `AMBUSH_MAX_INVISIBLE` (`defs/abilities.rs`, 5) seconds, or a hit reveals it early, Invisible ends and the ordinary AI fights with Touch; when Vanish is ready again the cycle repeats.
The spawns are the haunts: `dungeon/generate.rs` `place_haunts` (own random stream, salt `HAUNT_SALT`, so the map and the usual spawns of a seed do not move) gives each room or hall from depth `HAUNT_MIN_DEPTH` (0.35) a haunt with chance `HAUNT_CHANCE` (1 in 4). `enemies::haunt_for(boss)` says what appears: Ghost for the lich's dungeon, Shade for the demon's, nothing for the dragon's. All numbers are first guesses (label `balancing`): stats, HP, speed, weapon damage, the 20 s cooldown, the 5 s limit, the 0.5 s warning, XP 10 (Ghost) and 12 (Shade).

**Bosses (`bosses/`).**
- Interface: `trait BossBehaviour { spawn_extras, can_be_damaged, tick }` (no enrage: the demon fights the same all fight long), created by `bosses::create(BossId)` and held by the level's `BossEncounter`. During its tick the behaviour is taken out of the encounter (`behaviour: Option<…>`), so it can borrow `Run` mutably.
- A boss script chooses **which** ability, **when** and **where** (opening delays are script-local) through one path (`monster_use`, `bosses::use_in_pose` in code); it never sets damage, radius, push, speed, wind-up or cooldown: those come from the boss's `Kit` (`kit_for(KitSource::Boss(b), &attrs)`, re-resolved by `monster_numbers` from `Monster.base` on an Attribute change). Boss pushes use the boss's own STR.
- Boss HP is its base HP; it does not depend on the number of heroes (TODO 4b, 2026-10-19; before, `base × (0.5 + 0.5 × players)`, and enemies `× (0.75 + 0.25 × players)`).

| Boss | Base HP | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Demon | 1300 | `Strike` with *Demon Claws* (cleave, arc 2.1), `Swoop` (a long dash on foot, each foe hit once), `RingOfFire` (16 fire orbs). Each attack's animation (`Melee`, `Dash`, `Cast`) starts with its wind-up and runs on through a short recovery (the strike frames play out); the timings are `DEMON_*` in `defs/bosses.rs`, shared with the client. No enrage. |
| Lich | 900 | Spawns `LICH_DISCIPLES` (6) disciples in a ring. Immune while a disciple lives. `GloomBolts` (a fan of 5, `KIND.GloomBolt` 60). `RaiseDead` (3 per cast, 5 alive, `MINION_COOLDOWN` 12 s scaled by its INT 22, about 5.5 s, `Ev::Raise{k: 2}`): raised skeletons and `RaisedArcher`s (drawn as a skeleton archer, 20 HP, 2 XP), half and half; the disciples do not count towards the limit. It is the first in the order of choice, after its opening delay `LICH_RAISE_FIRST` (8 s). `Nova` on its kit cooldown (10 s base, about 4.5 s at INT 22) plus `LICH_NOVA_EXTRA_WAIT` (a random 0–2 s wait of the script, regardless of distance): `Anim::Slam` while it winds up (purple telegraph ring), then `Ev::Boom{k: 6}`, 24 damage and a push by the lich's own STR 10 (barely anything) for every hero within the Nova's radius. Order of choice: Raise Dead, nova, gloom bolts. No drain. |
| Dragon | 2000 | `FireBreath` (channelled cone, burning patches), `Bite` (*Dragon Bite*, narrow arc straight ahead, own cooldown; chosen before the claw when ready), `Strike` (*Dragon Claws*, wide arc), `TailWhip` (*Dragon Tail*, a swing behind it, physical), `Fireball` (the Wizard's def, one ball per use, by the dragon's INT, push 20 with its STR), and `Smash` on landing. After each strike a short recovery (the strike frames play out). Flight every 10–12 s (`DRAGON_FLIGHT_EVERY`): `Anim::TakeOff` 0.5 s standing still, then 3.5 s airborne (`Monster.airborne`, `FLAG.AIRBORNE`; melee and dashes skip it; only the breath; chases with `path_toward`), then `Anim::Land` 0.6 s and the Smash (`Ev::Boom{k: 5}`); take-off and landing flight are not abilities. Attack anims (`Melee`, `Bite`, `Tail`, `Breath`) start with the wind-up; the client shows the wind-up frame for `DRAGON_*_WINDUP` s. |

## 6. Dungeon generation (`dungeon/generate.rs`)

The map is 140×140 tiles of 16 px. Tile ids: Void 0, Floor 1, Wall 2, DoorOpen 3, DoorClosed 4, ShallowWater 5, DeepWater 6, Chasm 7, Lava 8, Trunk 9, Road 10, Thicket 11, Barrier 12.

**Tile rules (`dungeon/mod.rs`, mirrored in `client/src/sim/map.ts`).** There is no single "solid" rule any more:
- `opaque`: Void, Wall, DoorClosed, Trunk and Thicket (not Floor, Road, DoorOpen, Barrier or terrain). Blocks sight (FOV, `line_of_sight`) and projectiles. Terrain is never opaque. `hard_opaque` is `opaque` without Trunk and Thicket (the soft blockers: opaque but not hard-opaque): light passes the soft ones, see §7 "Soft blockers".
- `blocks(x, y, mover)`: opaque tiles and Barrier tiles block everyone. Deep water blocks every mover but `Dash`; chasms block `Enemy` and `Demon` (heroes may step in, and fall); lava blocks only `Enemy`.
- `speed_factor(pos, mover)`: `SHALLOW_SPEED` 0.7, `LAVA_SPEED` 0.4 (1 for demons), 1 for a dash. `step_move` takes it from the tile under the hero's centre at the start of the step.
- `Trunk` (#202, a tree's trunk: the cove's big tree, the woodland's trees): opaque but not hard (#203), so sight, line of sight and shots stop at it like at a column while light passes; it is not terrain. It blocks every mover but `Incorporeal` (walking and dashing; the user's decision), and an Incorporeal mover can pass it but not stop on it (`can_stop`). It is not `safe`; in the cove its 8 neighbours are always plain floor (the cove gets no terrain), checked by `cove_trunk_cuts_nothing_off`; the woodland drops a tree whose trunk would cut off walkable ground.
- `Road` (#203): a dirt road, open ground like Floor: not opaque, `safe`, speed factor 1.
- `Thicket` (#203): a hedge of undergrowth, a wall for every mover: opaque but not hard (blocks sight and shots, passes light), not `safe`.
- `Barrier` (#259): an invisible wall, the end of the playable part of an open map (the woodland). It blocks every mover, `Dash` and `Incorporeal` too (a phasing creature treats it like the map's edge); it is not opaque, so sight, light and shots pass (a shot flies on until its range ends); not `safe`. The client draws it as the ground of the tile west of it (`groundOf` in `woodland/ground.ts`, else the east one), so the wall column repeats its neighbour column and no seam shows. The parity fixture (`export.rs` `fixture_map`) holds a Barrier strip.
- `safe`: Floor, DoorOpen and ShallowWater, the ground a hero can stand on unharmed. Generator reachability (`bfs`) and `last_safe` use it.

The generator is seeded (ChaCha8) and deterministic:
1. **Placement:** built in a canonical orientation. The start room (8×8) is at the bottom center and the boss hall (26×20) at the top center. Right after the start room comes **the nymph's cove** (#202): a dedicated room of the boss hall's size (26×20, `BOSS_W`×`BOSS_H`), always one per dungeon (the user's decision). Its position is drawn from a clone of the generator rng on stream 14 (`cove_rng`), so the main rng makes no extra draws for it: any x from 3, y in the ordinary-room band (below the hall's band), at least 3 tiles from the start room, up to 100 tries (generation fails and retries if none fits). The same stream then gives the cove's seed (1 to 1 000 000) for the client's plants and pixies. It is pushed as `RoomKind::Cove` before the other rooms, so they keep clear of it. Because the cove is a room, the connections in step 2 reach it like any other. Existing seeds get new maps (the user's decision); the six recorded hash tests were re-recorded once on purpose. Rooms (6–10) and halls (12–18 × 11–15) are rejection-sampled below a band that keeps them away from the hall.
2. **Connections:**
   - a minimum spanning tree plus up to 6 extra loop edges, carved as 3-wide L corridors
   - the boss hall gets a single corridor with a 3-tile entrance (DoorOpen tiles, sealed later)
   - one-tile wall stubs left by overlapping carves (a cell with floor on three sides and around both corners between them) are filled with floor; they would draw as T junctions, and filling them opens no new path
   - walls are placed on every void cell next to floor
3. **Validation, retried on failure:** every room is reachable (BFS), and the boss hall is the farthest area by path distance, with a margin of 4 tiles.
4. **Population:** enemies scale with path depth. Necromancers appear only in halls. The cove gets no enemy (it is peaceful, the user's decision; its wild animals are spawned by `run/levels/dungeon.rs`, see §5 "Wildlife"), and no chest, barrel, terrain or ogre either (steps 4, 5 and the ogre skip `RoomKind::Cove`, as do the haunts).
   **Chests:** `place_chests` gives about one in three rooms and halls a chest on its top row (enemies never spawn there), against the wall, with floor on both sides and below so it never blocks a corridor mouth. A quarter are mimics. Chests use their own random stream (a clone of the generator rng on stream 7), so adding them did not change the maps of existing seeds.
5. **Terrain:** `place_terrain` runs on its own stream (8), like the chests. The theme (`Theme::for_boss`: lava for the demon, water otherwise) is passed into `generate(seed, theme)`; `DungeonLevel::build` picks the boss before generating. In ordinary rooms (40 %) and halls (two tries at 60 %) it places:
   - **pools:** ovals a tile away from the walls. Water pools are deep wherever all 8 neighbours are pool, so a shallow rim always surrounds the deep centre; lava pools are all lava
   - **chasm strips:** wall to wall across the room, 1 to `MAX_CHASM` (3) tiles wide so the Barbarian's dash clears them, with a 2–3 tile floor bridge

   A feature only covers floor, keeps two tiles from corridor mouths (open ground outside the room) and never covers a chest. It is undone if it cuts any safe ground off from the start or makes a room as far as the boss hall. Enemy spawns it covers move to the nearest free floor tile in the room. The start room stays clear. About 4 % of the floor becomes terrain.

   **Boss hall chasms** (`place_boss_chasms`, stream 9, every theme), so knockback matters in boss fights: one or two **wall strips** (4–7 tiles long, 1–2 deep, along any wall) and one or two **pits** (2x2 to 3x2, three tiles from the walls). Nothing goes within 3 tiles of the boss's start or near the entrance (the bottom centre, 4 tiles to each side, 5 deep). Pits keep 3 tiles from other chasms (the dragon is over two tiles wide), strips 2. A feature that cuts any hall floor off from the entrance is undone.
   **Lava pool in the demon's hall** (`place_boss_lava`, after the chasms on the same stream, lava theme only, asked for by the user): one oval lava pool (as in the rooms, 4×4 to 7×6), kept off the boss's start and the entrance like the chasms, with a tile of floor between it and any chasm, and undone if it cuts hall floor off from the entrance (up to 100 tries). The demon crosses it unharmed and at full speed (`Mover::Demon`); heroes knocked in are slowed and burn.
   **The ogre** (`place_ogre`, stream 10, after the terrain): one per dungeon, in a random ordinary room or hall at depth ≥ 0.35 (any ordinary room if none fits), on a tile with safe ground in all 8 neighbours and no chest. It is pushed to `spawns`, so it rotates with them. Stream 10 is a clone, so maps, spawns and chests of existing seeds are unchanged.
   **Barrels** (`place_barrels`, stream 11, last, a clone of the generator rng, so the maps, spawns, chests, terrain and ogre of existing seeds are unchanged): about one in four ordinary rooms and halls (at least 6×6) gets a group of 2–4 barrels in a 2×2 block, away from the walls, on tiles with plain floor all around (no wall, door, water, chasm or lava next to them), and not within a tile of an enemy spawn, a chest or another group. The boss hall gets 3–5 single barrels, each with a 7×7 area of plain floor around it (so a wide boss can always pass), outside `hall_keep_clear` (the boss's start and the entrance) and at least 4 tiles from the next barrel. A barrel or group is only placed if, with its tiles counted as walls, no safe tile is cut off from the start that was reachable before. With few valid spots a hall can get fewer than three barrels (best effort). They end up in `Layout.barrels`, and `Run::new` spawns them as objects.
6. **Rotation:** a random quarter turn, so the start can be on any side and the boss hall is on the opposite side.
7. **Torches** (`place_torches`, stream 12, **after** the rotation, so a "face" is a brick face on screen; a clone of the generator rng, so the maps, spawns, chests, terrain, ogre and barrels of existing seeds are unchanged; `level_setup_is_unchanged` leaves torches and their ids out):
   - **Candidates:** a wall cell with floor directly below and no floor left or right (`Face`), or with wall above and below and floor to one side (`West`/`East`, side sconces). Doorway jambs and corners never qualify (the "no floor left or right" test on `Face` is what rules out jambs).
   - **Spacing:** each straight run of candidate cells (per room or corridor stretch, side and line) gets a torch about every `TORCH_SPACING` (6) tiles from an unbiased random offset (`gen_range(0..TORCH_SPACING.min(len))`); runs under 3 cells get none; no two torches touch.
   - **Dark pockets:** each room and each corridor stretch (a connected piece of corridor floor between rooms, found by flood fill) is left without torches with probability `DARK_SHARE` (0.2).
   - **Never dark:** the start room and the boss hall are never dark, and always get at least one torch: if the normal rules give them none, a fallback puts one in the middle of their longest candidate run, whatever its length.
   - The result is `Dungeon.torches` → `Layout.torches` as `TorchSpawn { wall, side }`, and `Run::new` turns them into torch objects.
8. **Sunlit rooms** (`place_suns`, stream 13, after the torches, its own clone of the generator rng, so nothing else generated moves; #156). A level gets 0, 1 or 2 sunlit rooms with weights 25 / 50 / 25 (`SUN_ROOM_WEIGHTS`). A candidate is any room except the boss hall and the cove (the start room is allowed) whose floor `rect` is at least `SUN_MIN_ROOM_W` x `SUN_MIN_ROOM_H` (6 x 8) and has a floor tile that `sun_tile_ok` accepts: at least `SUN_MARGIN_LEFT` (2) tiles from the left wall (the light comes from the left), `SUN_MARGIN_FAR` (1) from the right and bottom walls, and `SUN_MARGIN_TOP` (4) below the top row of the rect (five rows below the top wall). The sun tile is a random qualifying tile. With probability `SUN_SECOND_CHANCE` (1/3) the room gets a second sun on a qualifying tile 3 to 5 tiles (`SUN_SECOND_DIST`, Euclidean) from the first, if there is one. If fewer fitting rooms exist than the roll asks for, it places what fits (possibly none); in practice about a quarter of the sunlit rooms end up with two suns. The result is `Dungeon.suns` → `Layout.suns`, a flat `Vec<(i32, i32)>` (0 to 4 per level). `Run::new` calls `Run::add_sun(x, y)` for each: a static `Daylight` light on that floor tile (`add_sun` is also the piece for hand-designed rooms). **Torches in a sunlit room start out** (`TorchSpawn.lit` false for every torch whose `front()` lies in the rect of a room that holds a sun; `spawn_torch` calls `start_light_off`, so they go out in `RunStartInfo.lights` as off); they can still be lit with E or fire, and the daylight stays on.
9. **The cove** (`dungeon/cove.rs` `finish_cove`, after `place_suns` and before the torches in sunlit rooms are put out; #202). The room is dressed after the rotation (it is 20×26 when turned):
   - **Trunk:** the tile at the room's centre (`rect.x + w / 2`, `rect.y + h / 2`) becomes `Tile::Trunk`. The room is plain floor, so nothing else in it needs checking; `cove_trunk_cuts_nothing_off` checks that all 8 neighbours are safe and that no safe tile is cut off from the start.
   - **Suns:** 5 (the user's decision), appended to `Dungeon.suns`. The first is on the trunk tile (the sun margins apply, the tile type does not, as it becomes the trunk; if the margins fail, the nearest `sun_tile_ok` tile). The other four are at the quarter centres (`rect.x + w * fx / 4`, `rect.y + h * fy / 4` for fx, fy in 1 and 3), each moved to the nearest `sun_tile_ok` tile, and dropped if within `COVE_SUN_GAP` (3) tiles of an earlier sun. So the room normally has 5 suns, and torches in it start out like in any sunlit room.
   - **Record:** `Dungeon.cove` / `Layout.cove` is `Cove { rect, tree, seed }`; `Run::new` sends it at run start as `RunStartInfo.cove` (`CoveInfo { x, y, w, h, tree_x, tree_y, seed }`, tiles). Other level types have none.

The test `guarantees_hold_over_many_seeds` checks 200 seeds (both themes), including chest placement (on floor against a wall, outside the start room and boss hall, not on an enemy), that chests and mimics actually appear, and the terrain guarantees: no safe tile cut off, spawns and chests on floor, no terrain in the start room, only chasms (and some) in the boss hall plus a lava pool in every lava-theme hall (none elsewhere), with its centre and entrance clear, only the theme's liquid, every terrain kind present, and a terrain share of 2–15 %.

### The woodland generator (`dungeon/woodland.rs`, #203)

`generate(seed)` is seeded (ChaCha8 on `seed ^ WOODLAND_SALT`) and builds a 188×46 map that starts as Thicket: the playable stretch of 140 columns (`WOODLAND_PLAY_W`) with a run-out of 24 columns (`WOODLAND_RUNOUT`) at each end (#259). In the run-out the forest and the road go on, so the party stands on a long road, not in a box; a column of `Tile::Barrier` stops it (`WALL_W` = column 26, `WALL_E` = column 161; `playable_x(x)` is true strictly between them). The follow camera stays inside the map (`followClamp` in `render3d/camera.ts`, for every level), so no screen sees where the run-out ends. Every number is a first guess (`balancing`). Columns below are given within the playable stretch (add 24 for the map column).
1. **Road:** a centre line `centre(x)` = the map's middle plus two sines, clamped to rows 12 to 33, and a 3-tile-wide Road along it, from end to end of the map.
2. **Walkable band:** Floor within 11 ± 3 tiles (smooth noise) of the line, inside a 3-tile Thicket border above and below; it runs to both ends of the map.
3. **Clearings:** six ellipses (radii 4 to 6 by 3 to 5) spread along the playable stretch, 7 to 10 tiles off the line, alternating above and below, carved as Floor. The start is a 10-column rect from column 3; the end is an ellipse (7×6) at column 131 whose playable part's last 14 columns are the `end` rect (the ellipse runs on past the eastern wall). Floor the road cannot reach turns back to Thicket.
4. **Roadside trees:** candidates are open Floor tiles with open neighbours, outside the start and outside every clearing's and the end's **ellipse** (not their bounding rects). A tree is dropped when its foot is within 3 tiles of the road's centre line, and half of those within 3 to 5 tiles. Kinds: round 28 %, conifer 24 %, tall 19 %, small round 14 %, wide 10 %, dead 5 % (`WOODLAND_FOLIAGE.dead_pct`, a first guess for the balancing pass; #279, #329: a dead, leafless tree, only by the road, never on the edge); spacing 2.0 to 3.2 tiles; three passes. Each has a fresh seed and a scale of 80 to 120 % (small rounds 50 to 65 %). **Leaf amount and dead mark (#329):** a `Plant` carries `leaf_pct` (0 to 100) and `dead`. `PlantKind::Bare` is gone: a dead tree is `dead: true` with `leaf_pct` 0, and its shape (round, wide or tall) comes from its seed (`seed % 3`), with no new random roll, so the rng order is as it was and no position or seed moved. Every other plant, the edge's and the ground bushes included, gets `WOODLAND_FOLIAGE.leaf_pct` (100, a first guess) and is alive. `WOODLAND_FOLIAGE` (`defs/foliage.rs`) is the level kind's one setting: `dead_pct` 5, `leaf_pct` 100, `palette` `LeafPalette::Summer` (a listed enum with one entry; `Woodland.palette` goes to the client as `WoodlandInfo.palette`). A dead-wood level, an autumn one or a winter one is a new `Foliage` value (and for autumn a new palette), nothing else.
5. **The edge:** trees and big bushes on the Thicket up to four tiles from walkable ground, denser near it. Edge plants take their (seed, scale) from `EDGE_SEEDS`, 12 fixed pairs per kind (indexed by the wire id, so it has a row for the ground bush as well: 5 rows, and the edge picks only round, wide, conifer and bush), so the client builds at most 48 distinct edge looks. No Thicket tile beside the ground is left without a plant within two tiles.
6. **Bushes on the ground:** one per about ten walkable tiles, 75 to 150 % (24 to 48 px wide: `scale_pct` is the drawn width, 32 px at 100 %), never on the road line.
7. **Trunks:** each tree's foot tile becomes `Tile::Trunk`; a tree whose trunk would cut off walkable ground is dropped, until none does. Then every `safe` tile of the two wall columns becomes `Tile::Barrier` (last, so the reachability passes before it see one band from end to end and keep the run-out's forest).
8. **Spots:** the party spawns on the road at the start. Nothing lives in the run-out (animals and their group members only on playable columns; the clearings are all inside). Up to four of the six clearings get a group of 2 to 4 enemies (goblin archers, orc warriors, orc archers), 21 tiles or more from the spawns. The animals are seven groups on the walkable ground, 6 tiles apart: three of 3 to 5 bunnies, two of 2 to 3 deer (one with a stag), two single boars. No spot is within 1.5 tiles of a trunk. The result is `Woodland { plants, start, end, clearings, animals, seed, sun }`; its `seed` (1 to 1 000 000) is for the client's grass, flowers and critters. `Layout.daylight` is true; there are no chests, barrels, torches or suns.
9. **The run's sun (#277):** `Sun { side, elevation_deg }`: `side` is -1 (left) or 1 (right), `elevation_deg` is rolled uniformly in `SUN_ELEVATION` 35..=70 (first guess, `balancing`). It rolls from its own `ChaCha8Rng` on `seed ^ SUN_SALT`, after everything else, so the map, plants and spawns of a seed do not move (`level_setup_is_unchanged` stays green). It travels as `WoodlandInfo.sun: SunInfo { side, elevation }` and only drives the client's light and shadows; the simulation never reads it.

The tests in `woodland.rs` run over many seeds: the same seed gives the same forest and the same sun, the sun is in range, a pinned fingerprint of the tiles, plants and spawns of five seeds (the sun is not in the hash, so a change that lets the sun move the map breaks it), the road reaches every playable floor tile and nothing beyond the walls, the border above and below is Thicket, the road runs on past the walls (road at both map ends, a barrier where the road crosses each wall column, no open tile left in it, start, end and every creature inside, a forest in the run-out), trunks are the roadside trees, nothing stands on the road, the counts are in range, the start is clear and creatures keep their distance from trunks. `dead_trees_grow_by_the_road_not_on_the_edge` (was `bare_trees_...`; also: a dead tree has `leaf_pct` 0 and is a round, wide or tall tree) and `plant_kind_ids_match_the_client` (the `PlantKind` ids against the client's `PLANT_KINDS` order) came with #279, which re-pinned the map fingerprint (the bare roll moved the plants). #329 added `a_dead_tree_takes_its_shape_from_its_seed`, `living_plants_are_full` and `the_woodland_names_its_palette`. The fingerprint was not re-recorded: `map_fingerprint` prints the plants through `legacy_plants`, which writes a dead tree as the old `Bare` kind and leaves out the two new fields, so `the_map_is_unchanged_by_the_sun` (and `level_setup_is_unchanged`) pass with their old values.

## 7. Field of vision and light

`fov.rs` is recursive shadowcasting with the viewer's vision in tiles (9 for every hero today); walls (`Map::opaque`) block sight but are themselves visible. Terrain does not block sight. Sight alone shows nothing any more: **lighting replaced the fog**. Heroes have no light of their own, so a tile is only seen when it is in sight **and lit** (see "Light sources", §5). Unlit tiles are fully black. Sight and light are used in three places:
- **The hero's view (`vispoly.rs`, client `sim/view.ts`, the same rule, pinned by the shared fixture):** what a hero sees is its `View` (since 2026-10-16), computed from the viewed hero's **pixel position**. Open tiles count when any of 16 sample points (a 4×4 grid, 4 px apart) lies inside the visibility polygon (see "The vision mask" below); walls count when the tile FOV sees them. So, from the same position, the server sends monsters on the floor the client shows, with no black wedges where the two drift apart behind a corner. `View::compute` runs the FOV and the polygon (one per player per snapshot) and tests only the open tiles in the polygon's bounding box. The point-in-polygon test uses a binary search over the ray angles (the polygon is star-shaped around the viewer). `Player.view` replaced `Player.fov`.
- **Server snapshots (`visibility.rs`), sight-only sending and the dark mark (#309):** `visible(pos, radius)` means on a tile of the viewer's view (large figures, `radius > 8`, also by the four corner points at `0.8 * radius`). `seen(pos)` means visible **and** (on a lit tile (static or moving layer, `Lights::lit`, the same rule as `is_lit`) **or** within `dark_reach`, the viewer's vision × darkvision, measured from the viewer), with the same corner rule. Both use the viewed hero's sight, so a spectator gets the teammate's view and darkvision. Monsters (non-Friendly), enemy projectiles, hazards, world objects and positional events are **sent when visible** (sight alone), and those that are not `seen` are **marked**: `Snapshot.dark` lists their entity ids, `Snapshot.dark_ev` the indices of the dark positional events in this snapshot's `ev` (the filtered list that goes out, not `run.events`). `snapshot_ents` returns `Sent { ents, dark }`; `ev_sent` / `ev_dark` decide events. Never marked: the party, Friendly creatures, the heroes' own shots, torches, party-wide events (no position) and events on a hero. Both lists are empty in a lit room. The 3D view uses the mark to leave out its overlays (§9 "Overlays, input and turning in the 3D run"). Before #309 the server simply did not send what was not `seen`, so a creature popped in at the edge of the light. Teammates are always sent, and so are the party's own projectiles, the `Dmg` of their hits and the `Boom` of their explosions (sent without a position), so a shot into the dark flies on and shows its hit instead of vanishing at the edge of the view. The lich drain beams keep their "boss fight on" rule.
  - **The rules log keeps the light filter:** `rules_log::send::sees` still decides by `seen` and `object_seen` (a torch by sight, every other object by `seen`), so no log line names a creature in the dark, and `snapshot_tags` leaves out every id in `Sent.dark` (a tag would give a dark creature away now that it is in `ents`).
  - **Torches** (world objects that own a light, `owns_light`) are sent by sight like every object and are never marked: a torch in the viewer's FOV is sent even if it is out or its tile is dark, one outside the FOV is not. The client's light state does not depend on the torch entities (it comes from `RunStartInfo.lights` and the light events), and a torch sprite outside the FOV sits under full black anyway.
  - **Hero events (`on_hero`):** `Run::event_on_hero` marks an event (hurt and heal numbers on a hero) by its index in the tick's event list. Those are sent where the hero is in sight, lit or not, and never marked; every other positional event is sent in sight and marked in `dark_ev` when it is not lit or within dark reach. The 3D view lays out the numbers on heroes (`Dmg.p`, `Heal.p`) in its overlay, which the scene's light never darkens, so they show even when the hero is unlit.
  - **The moving-light list (`Snapshot.lights`):** every lit moving light within the viewer's FOV radius plus one tile plus its own reach (`lights_for`: a distance test with no line-of-sight check), as `MovingLight(x, y, kind, colour?)`. It can include glows from behind walls (§13). The monster that carries a glow is sent when it stands in sight.
- **Client light and sight (`LightField`, `sim/view.ts`, `render3d/`):** see "Light and sight on the client" below.
- **Enemy perception:** `perception::sees` (above). **Behaviour change (2026-10-15):** humans (necromancer, summoner, lich disciples) do not see heroes in the dark; monsters (goblin and orc archers, orc warriors, mimic, ogre) only within a third of their vision (about 2.5 to 3 tiles); undead, demons and bosses see fully in the dark. A hero in light is seen as before. `simulation_is_unchanged_after_waking_the_boss` was re-recorded for this: in the lich hall the disciples stay idle in the dark and one orc loses the hero.

**Soft blockers and the soft shadow (#203).** Two rules, one per question.
- **Sight, line of sight and shots** use `Map::opaque`: walls, closed doors, trunks and thickets all stop them. `View::compute`, `fov.rs`, `vispoly.rs` and their client ports take a `Blocker`: `All` (every opaque tile) for what the hero sees and the server sends, `Hard` (`hard_opaque`) for light.
- **Light** uses `hard_opaque`: the static light's shadowcast (`run/light.rs`, `LightField`) passes trunks and thickets, so a tile behind a trunk stays lit, and the dungeon air flows over trunks. The sun reaches ground a hero cannot see.
- **The soft shadow (`render3d/sightmask.ts`; its geometry `shadeCovers` is in `game/sightField.ts`).** The client holds two views: the hard view (walls only; it drives the darkness and the mask's `hard` channel) and the sight view (all: what the server lets the hero see). Ground inside the hard polygon and outside the full one is the mask's `soft` channel: dark maps (the cove) dim it with black at `SHADE` (0.3), never blacking it out, daylight maps haze it (§9 "Sight in the 3D view (#305)"). A creature standing there is hidden because the server sends none (so the shade can show ground with no creature on it; that is by design).
- **Tests:** `hard_opaque_is_the_old_rule`, `shots_and_line_of_sight_stop_at_trunks`, `a_trunk_hides_a_monster_behind_it`, `a_creature_inside_a_trunk_tile_is_seen` (server); `sim.test.ts`, `view.test.ts`, `sightField.test.ts` (`fillPolygon`, `blurField`, `canvasRes`, the wedge geometry and `shadeCovers`), `sightmask.test.ts` (render3d) (client). The fingerprint tests passed unchanged (their scenarios never meet a trunk).
- **Tests of the woodland's depth (#277):** `the_sun_is_in_range_and_pinned_to_the_seed` and `the_map_is_unchanged_by_the_sun` (a pinned FNV-1a fingerprint of tiles, plants and spawns for seeds 1, 2, 3, 7 and 42) in `woodland.rs` (server); `vegetation.test.ts` (the shape does not change with the light, the cove tree's FNV pin guards against drift; it was re-recorded on purpose in #279) and `render3d/sun.test.ts` (the sun, the shadow map's fit) (client).

**Light and sight on the client.**
- **`LightField`** holds the static lights (from `RunStartInfo.lights` and the light events; `RunSession.onEvent` calls `set`, `add`, `remove` and, on `Ev::Tile`, `mapChanged`) and builds the same static light map as the server with the `sim/fov.ts` port. The shared fixture (`export.rs` → `fixtures.json`, checked by `light.test.ts`) pins the TypeScript map to the Rust one, so a tile the server counts as lit is never drawn fully black, and no light is drawn on a tile the server counts as dark. Colours mix additively: each source adds `colour × intensity × falloff(distance / radius)` per tile and channel, capped at 255 (torch plus lava gives orange, blue plus red purple). The falloff is a smoothstep from 1 at the centre down to `EDGE` (0.1) at the radius edge, never brighter further out, so a light fades out **inside** the tiles it lights. Moving lights from the snapshot are added each frame with the same falloff (`addMoving`): a radius-r shadowcast from the tile of the sent position (`Math.floor(px / TILE)`, the server's `light_tile`), the same tiles the server's moving layer marks; the shared fixture also pins these (moving lights in the open, beside a corner, against a wall and exactly on a tile corner).
- **The 3D view's light** is per pixel in the shader, not per tile on the CPU. `render3d/lights.ts` takes the `LightField` and the snapshot's moving lights and picks the lights that reach the screen; the falloff, the colour mixing, the wall and card shadows, the lava's glow, darkvision (the darkness a tile gets where only darkvision shows, never darker than `DV_DARK` 0.45) and the flicker of each kind (`lightFlicker`) are in §9 "The dungeon's light (#304)". `LightField.accumulate` is still used for the lava's texels (`lavaTexels`).
- **The edge of sight (`sim/vispoly.ts`, `sim/view.ts`):** `visibilityPolygon` (pure, `vispoly.test.ts`) builds the area seen from the viewer's **pixel** position, so the edge of sight is a straight shadow line and not the tile grid. Wall edges that face open tiles are merged into segments, and rays go to every segment end (± 1e-4 rad, so a ray grazing a corner continues behind it) and at 96 even angles. Each ray stops at the nearest segment or at the sight radius `sightRadius(vision)` = `(vision + 0.5) × TILE` px, so the outer edge is a circle covering the tiles the shadowcast reaches. It costs about 0.05 ms per polygon (measured in Node on the fixture map, vision 9). A ray tests only the segments of its *angular bucket* (#280, `bucketSegments`: 256 buckets around the viewer; a segment goes into every bucket its angle span touches plus one on each side, and into all of them if it passes within 1 px of the viewer), so the result is exactly that of testing every segment (pinned by a brute-force comparison in `vispoly.test.ts` and by the shared fixture). Without the buckets the cost grows with rays × segments: the woodland's full view (trunks and thickets block it) has about 200 segments and over 1000 rays and took about 2.2 ms per compute while walking; with them about 0.8 ms. The server's `vispoly.rs` still tests every segment (same result). `View.r` is the sight radius in world px of the last `compute`; `touches(v, x, y)` (`sim/view.ts`) is true when `View.isVisible` holds or any of 12 points 0.5 px outside the tile's border (at 2, 6, 10 and 14 px along each edge, corners left out so a diagonal neighbour's open ground cannot count) lies inside the full polygon: the hero sees ground against a side of the tile, not necessarily its face. The tile shadowcast starts at the hero's tile centre and the polygon at the exact pixel, so where a nearer trunk hides part of a tile, or at a grazing angle, the hero sees ground right against a trunk that the tile test calls hidden; `touches` is a client look only, `isVisible` is unchanged (server parity).
- **The unseen by map kind (#278, decided 2026-11-08).** What the hero cannot see looks different on the two kinds of map, so that dark always means shadow and pale always means "something could be hiding here". **Dark maps** (dungeons, the cove): beyond sight is black, and behind the cove tree's trunk the soft shadow dims with black at `SHADE`. **Daylight maps** (`RunStartInfo.daylight`, the woodland): the unseen is **haze**, never darker: the scenery is still drawn (#258), washed toward a pale grey-blue, desaturated, thinning with height (§9 "Sight in the 3D view (#305)"). No haze in dungeons: the fog look for lit tiles behind walls was rejected on 2026-10-16 and would reveal the layout; a dim map memory was demoed and rejected too. On daylight maps nothing is blacked out; the sight follows the real field of vision, so bees and butterflies stay hidden beyond it; creatures stay hidden because the server sends none from beyond sight (`run/visibility.rs`).
- **Sound text (the rule, 2026-10-16):** text that stands for a sound shows even where nothing else does, for example the sleeper's "Z". It is overlay text, which the scene's light never darkens, in `SOUND_TEXT` (`game/fxLooks.ts`): a dark fill `#1a1020` with a light outline `#d8d0e0` (a pale fill vanished inside the pale outline). Damage and heal numbers are not sounds and keep their rule (§9 "Overlays, input and turning in the 3D run"). A sound only shows for an entity the client has: heroes and the wolf are always sent, an enemy in the dark is not.
- **Daylight (#156).** The lit area of a `Daylight` light is the server's: a radius-7 shadowcast like any static light, and the client adds no lit tile. A cloud is a dip in the light's brightness (`sunLook.clouds` in `game/sunmath.ts`, up to 0.45 per sun, over 3 to 6 s every `gap` (8 to 25) seconds; the schedule is hashed from the light id, but each client times it on its own clock, like torch flicker, so clouds are not in sync between players); `render3d/lights.ts` dims a Daylight light's brightness by `cloudAt(id, t, lookFor(id, sunLook))` and the crack of the same light by the same cloud (§9 "The air in 3D (#307)"). Every sun has its own look from `sunVariant(id)` (a hash of the light id, the same for everyone): widening or straight beam, patch width 1.5 to 3 tiles, height, 2 or 3 inner rays laid out as a `fan` or as `parallel` shafts, a faint wide main ray under them, brightness (`glow`) and cloud depth. The beam, the floor patch and the dust in it are drawn by the air (§9 "The air in 3D (#307)").
- **Open-air daylight (#203, the woodland).** `Layout.daylight` becomes `Lights.daylight` on the server (`run/light.rs`): `lit(x, y)` is true for every tile in the map, whatever the light sources say, so what a hero sees depends on the field of vision alone and enemies notice a hero as they do in the light. `RunStartInfo.daylight` sets `LightField.daylight` on the client (`RunSession` constructor): `accumulate` fills every tile with white (`DAYLIGHT`, no tint). The dungeon's air is off in daylight. Beyond the sight edge the scenery is hazed, not blacked out (#258).
- The renderer knows nothing about torches, lava or projectiles: it draws whatever the registry sends.

## 8. Netcode and responsiveness

**Input (`RunSession.fixedStep`, #302).**
- The run session (`run/session.ts`, §9 "The run session and the view interface") owns the prediction (the `Predictor`, the own shots, `predictAbility`) and the fixed step; the host only hands it one input record per step. `runStage.ts` builds that `StepInput` (`run/input.ts`) from the held keys and the mouse latches: the movement `mx`, `my`, `primary`, `secondary` (held, or clicked since the last step: the click latches are consumed by every step, also while dead), `act` (one per E press) and `held`, true while any movement key is down, also when two cancel out (A and D), so the own figure still plays the walk animation. The own walk or idle animation reads `held` from the last fixed step, not the keys at frame time, so above 60 Hz it can switch up to one step (16.7 ms) later than the keys; that is when the prediction starts or stops moving.
- Inputs are sent at a fixed 60 Hz. Each step carries movement, the aim, and a shot id when an ability fires; the client fires when the button is held or was clicked and its local cooldown is ready. The aim comes from the only question the session asks the view, `RunView.pointerWorld()`; without an answer it follows the movement, else it stays.
- WebSocket `TCP_NODELAY` is enabled on the server listener.
- **Cache headers** (`cache_headers` in `main.rs`): hashed Vite bundles (`/assets/index-*`) are `immutable`; everything else (`index.html`, the atlas) is `no-cache`. Without this, a browser could keep running an old client against a new server, and the tile rules and prediction would disagree.

**Prediction (`world.ts` `Predictor`).**
- Local movement uses the TS collision port. It is bit-identical to Rust, which `sim.test.ts` checks against the Rust-written fixtures.
- On each snapshot the client replays unacknowledged inputs from the server state.
- Errors under 24 px are blended out over about 100 ms; larger errors snap.
- Terrain is part of the port: the speed factor and the walking rules.
- **Solid objects are part of the port** (`Predictor.solids`): the squares of resting barrels from the latest snapshot go to `stepMove`, like the server's `Run::solids`. A barrel moves only after a push or sinks, so prediction next to barrels should need no correction; that is part of the open live check.
- **Knockback is part of the port** (`stepMove`, `knocked()`). `me` carries `knock_vx/knock_vy`, and the replay starts from them, so after the snapshot that reports a push the prediction slides exactly like the server. The push itself is the enemy's action, so the client learns of it about RTT/2 late (a correction, blended or snapped as usual). While predicted knocked, `RunSession.fixedStep` fires nothing, and the replay drops dashes sent during a push the client learned of late, like the server. The predictor also applies the server's sinking rule (`sinking()` in `sim/collision.ts`): a hero not dashing whose centre is over a chasm or deep water stops moving and attacking, in prediction and in the replay, so a fall needs no round trip and causes no correction. The pending list keeps each input as it was sent, not as predicted: if the server sees no fall (or a debug climb-out already happened there), it applies the move, and the replay must too.
- **Prediction stops while charmed (#224).** `Predictor.charmed` comes from `me.charmed_by` (set only while the charm is on; paused: not charmed). While it is set, `reconcile` copies position, state and both cooldowns from the server's `me`, clears the pending inputs and blends nothing, and `step` numbers an empty input and moves and ticks nothing. `RunSession.fixedStep` keeps sending empty inputs (mx = my = 0, no ability, aim unchanged) only to keep the ack/seq clock going and, if the charm ends before the client knows, so the server sees what it expects (no flag on the wire); cooldowns come from `me`, not from these inputs. The own figure, the camera and the air mover are drawn from the interpolated own entity (`Predictor.charmedPos`). **Blending:** at the start the figure begins at its last drawn place and decays into the interpolated hero (offset times e^(-dt/0.1), like `renderPos`); when control returns the first reconcile sets `offX/offY` to drawn minus predicted, so the existing 0.1 s blend runs and there is no jump. The AI's shots (ids from `AI_SHOT_BASE`, 0xF000, owner = you) are never taken for predicted ones: `ownPredictedShot` is false for them, so they are drawn from the server and never hidden. The use hint is off while charmed; chat, the menu, the log and the stats overlay still work. Charmed hero entities: `meleeFoe` and `shotPassesThrough` (`game/shotTargets.ts`) treat a Hostile hero as a foe, and its bar is the enemy's red one. Its over-head name tag stays white on purpose (enemies have no name label to copy); the red bar, the heart and the pink party row mark it. While charmed the HUD root gets the class `charmed` (`CharmView.update`), which moves the messages below the CHARMED title block.
- Local cooldowns are corrected when they differ from the server by more than 0.15 s. The server value is first carried through the inputs it has not acknowledged yet, including the abilities they use (`Pending.used` for cooldowns; like the movement replay). Without that, a snapshot from before the server saw a shot showed cooldown 0, the client reset its own, and a held button fired a second, client-only shot (seen as two arrows when dragging the mouse while clicking).

**Own attacks.**
- `RunSession.predictAbility` starts the animation, cooldown or dash locally (the ranger's `SummonWolf` only the cooldown: the wolf itself is not predicted) and spawns cosmetic projectiles in `Shots` (`run/shots.ts`: their flight, wall and enemy hits, piercing and the hidden server copies are state in the session; the view only draws `RunFrame.shots` and reacts to `shotEnded`).
- The matching server projectile (same `shot_id`) is hidden, also for 2 s after the cosmetic one has ended (`endedShots`). The cosmetic one ends when the server one disappears, at a wall, or at the first enemy it touches (`sim/hitbox.ts`, the port of `Monster::shot_dist` with the exported `HITBOX` table; sampled every 4 px; `ResolvedAbility.projectile` is an `EntityKind`, sent as its id). Without the enemy check a shot that hit within the first ticks never appeared in a snapshot, and its cosmetic copy flew on through every enemy behind.

**Remote entities (`Interpolator`).**
- Snapshots are placed on a server-tick timeline using an estimated clock offset (a low envelope of arrival offsets).
- Remote entities are rendered behind that timeline by an adaptive delay of 1.5 snapshot intervals + 2.5× jitter, clamped to 50–300 ms.
- Brief extrapolation of at most 100 ms covers a missing snapshot.

**Measuring.**
- F3 overlay (§10 "The F3 panel"): FPS; the whole frame, its render part and `RunSession.frame`; the frame split per client system; scene objects, draw calls and the canvas size; the render size with the quality, the zoom and the backend (#314); then ping, jitter, interpolation delay, snapshot bytes, prediction correction, server ms per tick, entities, darkvision.
- F4 (debug runs only): cycles your own hero's darkvision 0 -> 50% -> 100% -> 0 via `DebugDarkvision` (the server honours it only in a debug run). The client ignores F4 unless the run is a debug run and your hero is alive. While darkvision > 0 the 3D light greys what only darkvision shows (`Lighting.setDarkvision`, §9 "The dungeon's light (#304)"). In darkvision reach a tile is never darker than `DV_DARK` (0.45), lit or not, and the grey fades out as a light gets brighter than darkvision: without this, the dim rim of every light was darker than the darkvision around it.
- F9 (debug runs only, alive): toggles immortality via `DebugImmortal` (the server echoes it in `DebugPath.immortal`; the badge shows MORTAL).
- Shift+F7 (debug runs only, alive): switches your own hero's anti-magic on and off via `DebugAntiMagic` (§5 "Magic and non-magic effects"): its magic effects, worn magic items and Radiant are paused while it is on. F7 alone is the incorporeal key.
- Shift+F8 (debug runs only, alive): a Dispel on your own hero via `DebugDispel` (`Want::Positive`, 3 picks, 5 s). Together with F8, which now applies its effect as magic, this shows the greyed icons and the grey chips.
- F8 (debug runs only, alive): cycles `DebugEffect` through every `EffectKind` (every kind, including Weakened, Clumsy and Frail) on your own hero, with the default duration (Charmed takes the nearest living enemy as its caster; at any distance; "No caster for Charmed" means no living enemy at all, and nothing happens); the server shows "name: Kind" as a message. Use it to see the HUD icons, the lying pose and the stone look without a lich.
- `?lag=&jitter=&loss=` simulates conditions in both directions, keeping TCP ordering (`DelayLine`: one FIFO queue and one timer, because browsers round timer delays down to whole milliseconds; a timer per message delivered inputs out of order, which the server then applied out of order, and that caused most of the corrections once seen in busy runs under lag).
- In a busy debug run under `?lag=150&jitter=40&loss=2` (bot walking to the demon and fighting it for 90 s), about 36 of 2,760 snapshots still correct the prediction: almost all are pushes, which the client learns of about RTT/2 late, plus the force field closing during a dash.

**Typical numbers (local):**
- server tick about 0.12–0.14 ms
- snapshot about 127 B with 2 players (the knock is `[vx, vy]` only while pushed, nil otherwise) (248 B before the compact form); a fight with 14 entities in view about 350 B (about 570 B before). Each extra entity costs about 19 B.
- correction 0.00 px

## 9. Rendering (client)

Every run is drawn by the 3D view (`client/src/render3d/`, three.js). This chapter starts with the art and the atlas, then what is data (figures, chests, texts, fonts), the DOM screens and the HUD, the run session with its view interface, and then the 3D view one subject at a time.

**Art source.**
- Art comes from the 0x72 pack; other CC0 tilesets that fit its style may be added (the user allowed this for terrain and enemies). Terrain tiles (water, lava, chasm) are generated at atlas build time by `client/scripts/terrain-tiles.ts` from pack colours and the pack's `floor_1`, because no CC0 set fit (the user allowed self-made art for terrain tiles only): seamless animated base textures (`terrain_<set>_f<frame>_q<piece>`) plus edge overlays (`terrain_<edge>_e<code>`, a 4-digit quarter code from the 8 neighbours, see `terrain-codes.ts`). `build-atlas.ts` turns `tile_list_v1.7` into a JSON-hash atlas, so frame names are the pack's own (`knight_m_run_anim_f2`, `weapon_axe`, `floor_1`, …).
- **Dragon frames** (`scripts/dragon-frames.ts`, poses and layout in `src/game/anim/dragonRig.ts`, shared with the client): AI-generated art made for this game at the user's request (2026-10-09 to 10-12), in `client/assets-src/dragon/`.
  - Sources: `dragon_base.png` (the standing dragon, 49×43, 9 colours) and one aligned strip per action (`dragon_walk`, `dragon_claw`, `dragon_bite`, `dragon_tail`, `dragon_hover`.png). `DRAGON_SHEETS` gives each strip's cell size, `shift` into base coordinates and the per-frame head offset.
  - Walk, claw, bite, tail whip and hover frames are copied from the strips. Idle, breath, take-off and landing are generated from the base: it is cut into parts (head, jaw, wings, tail, body; polygon masks), the parts are moved per pose, the outline is redrawn and joint cracks filled. On the hover frames only the jaw opens (fire from the air); that works because their head sits where the base head is.
  - The mouth and nostril points for the breath come from the same poses (`dragonPoints()`), so they follow the art.
  - All frames are 86×54 with the base at (19, 7); the feet are on row 50 (`footY`, the figure origin); legs hanging in flight reach below.
  - How the strips were imported (one-off scripts, not in the repo): the user's JPGs on a magenta background were converted with PowerShell `System.Drawing`; a rectangular pixel grid was fitted per strip (they were stretched unevenly), cell centres sampled (median) and snapped to the base palette; frames separated as connected shapes (specks dropped) and stray light-red pixels on the silhouette removed; then each frame placed in base coordinates, on the base head (walk, claw, hover) or on the planted hind claws (bite, tail), with the head offset found by matching the base head. The walk strip was drawn ~15% smaller and was resampled to base size. Redo the same for new strips.
- **Barrel frames** (`scripts/barrel-frames.ts`): AI-generated art made for this game with ChatGPT at the user's request (2026-10-15), source in `client/assets-src/barrel/barrel_source.png` (a 20×27 pixel grid at about 25 screen px per pixel). That grid was read once (cell medians, colours snapped to pack colours), then redrawn by hand at 16×22, about the crate's size, because 20×27 stands taller than a hero. The script holds the grid: `barrel` (plain wood, for blocking barrels that do not explode; not used yet) and `barrel_explosive` (the same with a red wood lid and a red band, the user's choice from three variants). `render3d/figures.ts` draws `barrel_explosive`; `?r3d=gallery` shows the fuse, fall and sink poses.
- **Torch frames** (`scripts/torch-frames.ts`): "Simple Torch Animation 16x16" by Natural_Privateer (OpenGameArt, CC0, "credit is appreciated"), the sheet and a licence note in `client/assets-src/torch/`. The sheet is a 3×2 grid of 16×16 frames, the last one empty; `buildTorches` copies the filled cells into the atlas as `torch_f0`… (the flame animation, 8 fps in `game/objectPose.ts`) and makes `torch_out`, frame 0 without its flame pixels (red ≥ 180 and green > blue), a bare stick, for an unlit torch. The side sconces reuse the same frames, on the side wall's inner edge facing into the room (`pushWall` in `render3d/cards.ts`). `?r3d=lights` shows lit and burnt-out torches. Credit: README Art.
- **Cove look** (#202; `game/covemath.ts`, `render3d/cove3d.ts`, `render3d/cover/cove.ts`, `scripts/cove-frames.ts`; try it in `?r3d=cove`; it is in every dungeon). The user approved the design on 2026-11-01.
  - Tree: the willow of `Cove3d` (§9 "Woodland light, shadows, wind and time of day in 3D", "The cove"), grown at run time; against the point lights it casts the flat stand-in card of the atlas frame `cove_tree` until #333. That frame is drawn for this game by code (`coveTree()` in `game/vegetation.ts`: the general round tree with the `old` option (#279: thicker, more bent trunk, four longer roots, a lower fork), seed 11, at `COVE_TREE_SCALE` 0.97 (the largest that fits), 96x100, trunk foot at (48, 80) = `COVE_TREE_FOOT`), pack-style palette lit from the top left (`DEFAULT_LIGHT`). The cove's surroundings (`ROOT_FRINGE`, `TRUNK_CLEAR`, `CANOPY_UP`) fit the older, thinner tree; the user left them for #299 (a willow replaces the cove tree). In the game only the trunk tile blocks movement (`Tile::Trunk`, §6); roots and canopy don't.
  - Grass is only a look, no tile: `paintCove` draws one rounded carpet 22 px in from the floor edge with a wobbling edge, base `3e8a43`, faint patches `3a8340`, edge shade `31743c`, the canopy shadow under the tree, and pointed 2-4 px blades along the border; the stone under the bottom edge is darkened. `coveGrass` says where grass is (flowers and plants only go there); the cove's cover source (`render3d/cover/cove.ts`) grows 3D grass over this carpet and keeps its colours. Flowers are single pixels or 5-pixel crosses in five colours, denser towards the tree.
  - Plants (`placeCovePlants`, 30 in all, seeded; seed 1 uses the approved prototype's seeds): bushes, tufts and ferns as atlas frames, kept apart (24 px for bushes, 15 px otherwise) and off the trunk and roots (`TRUNK_CLEAR`). Then 10 tufts and ferns among the roots (`ROOT_FRINGE`, own rng stream so the others keep their spots): around the root tips and in front of the roots, 9 px apart, never on the trunk base, so the roots blend into the grass. The bushes are 3D trees and the tufts and ferns bushels of the ground cover (`Cove3d.bushelSpots()`); heroes walk through them.
  - Light: several Daylight lights so the whole room is lit.
- **Woodland look** (#203; `game/vegetation.ts`, `game/woodland/ground.ts`, `render3d/`; try it in `?r3d=woodland`). The user approved the look on 2026-11-07 (conifers, forest, clouds, road).
  - Plants: `vegetation.ts` draws every tree, conifer, bare tree and bush at run time from shapes (`drawTree`, `drawBush`, `drawPlant(kind, seed, scalePct, light = DEFAULT_LIGHT)`; `PLANT_KINDS` round, wide, tall, conifer, bush = the wire ids of `PlantKind`; a dead tree is not a kind since #329: it is a plant with the `dead` mark, drawn as `'bare'`), in the pack-style palette; pure, so the atlas script imports it too (the cove tree). Each `Drawn` also carries a `Recipe` (#329: the trunk path, roots, limbs, lobes and skirts it was drawn from) that the 3D trees read for their size (§9 "The trees"; the painters stay until #333). A bush's `width` is its drawn width (32 px at 100 %). The drawing code is in `vegetation/*`. The user approved the look of #279 on 2026-11-10:
    - Leafy trees (`leafy.ts`, round/wide/tall): a bent, tapering trunk (`trunkPath`) with a foot flare, two or three limbs from the fork, two to four roots, wavy bark lines, root moss; a crown of overlapping lobes (`crown.ts`) with dark seams between them, light dabs, a few dark pockets with a twig and hanging vines, and the crown's shadow on the trunk. `TreeOpts.old` makes an older tree (trunk ×1.4, bend ×1.9, four roots ×1.25 long, fork 3 units lower); only the cove tree uses it so far.
    - Conifers (`conifer.ts`): a straight trunk, 1 to 24 design units of it bare below the lowest skirt, under stacked skirts with zigzag hems in the blue-green ramp (palette 5–9), narrowing to a spire.
    - Bare trees (`bare.ts`, the picture of a plant with the `dead` mark; it was wire kind 5 until #329): the leafy trunk and roots under recursive leafless limbs (depth 4, 3 below 70 %) ending in 1-px twigs; no crown shadow. Their bark is mapped to the dead-wood ramp e..h (greyer and darker than a..d) as the last pass, so every bark pass before it sees a..d.
    - Bushes (`bush.ts`): three to five lobes (by drawn width) on one thin stem in the middle (1 px, 2 px from 40 px wide) that forks low into two or three branches under the crown; a dark underside. `BUSH_SPREAD` 1.1 against the layout's extent sets the drawn width.
    - Light contract (#277, kept by #279): the light changes only colours, never which pixels are opaque, the size or the foot. Every random draw is made whatever the light; positions that depend on the sun only recolour; every write gives the picture's colour and the default light's reference colour (`Canvas.ref`), and colour decisions read the reference. A sweep test checks it (6 kinds × 12 seeds × 3 sizes × 3 suns). `Canvas.id` is a lobe-id map for seams and shadows between lobes and skirts; whole-picture passes loop over the tracked box, and `finish` crops to it.
    - Draw cost (#279, ms per plant at 60/100/120 %, before → after): round 2.47/4.13/5.80 → 0.58/1.06/1.49, wide 2.53/4.83/6.44 → 0.80/1.26/1.70, tall 1.91/2.98/3.79 → 0.45/0.79/1.02, conifer 4.38/5.76/6.61 → 0.30/0.40/0.45, bare 0.42/0.68/0.83 (budget: tall's), bush at 100/150 % 0.61/1.19 → 0.24/0.25; all the distinct looks of demo map seed 1: 1266 → 217 ms. `npx tsx scripts/plant-sheet.ts --bench` measures it; `npx tsx scripts/plant-sheet.ts <dir>` writes the judging sheets (every kind at 1× and 3×, small sizes, a right sun, the cove tree).
  - Light and sun (#277): the light is a parameter of every plant. `lightVector(sun)` gives a unit vector toward the sun in screen space (`DEFAULT_LIGHT`, a sun on the left at about 51°, is the one fixed light of the cove tree and the atlas script). The random stream is independent of the light (each random draw is made where the default light would make it), so a seed's plant shape never changes with the sun; only the shading does. Within a run the light is one constant, so the `PlantPages` frame key stays (kind, seed, size).
  - Ground (`woodland/ground.ts`): the whole map is painted once into RGBA, Floor and Trunk tiles as the cove's grass with flowers (about one per 40 px²), Road as packed dirt, Thicket as dark undergrowth; where a stronger ground meets a weaker one (road over thicket over grass) it creeps 3 px into it, dithered, with a faint darker rim where the road meets grass, so no tile edge shows. `GroundPainter` paints in row bands, the bands nearest the spawn first (`bandOrder` in `woodland/settle.ts`); the 3D view's cover job paints it and uploads the image once (§9 "The woodland's ground"), and until then the ground shows a flat grass colour. `DrawQueue` (`woodland/plantpages.ts`) runs jobs within a time budget per frame; the forest builds its trees with it (§9 "The forest").
  - What the 3D view does with it (the grown trees, the sun, the shadows, the cloud shadows, the critters, the haze) is in §9 "Woodland light, shadows, wind and time of day in 3D" and "Ground cover (#330)".
  - Daylight: no darkness or tint (§7). The map's wall and void tiles are drawn as in the dungeon; the ground tiles are not (`GROUND_TILES`, Barrier included). Sight is the mask's circle with the scenery hazy beyond it and behind trunks and thickets (#258, #278; §7, "Sight in the 3D view (#305)").
- **Ghost and Shade looks** (#169, #170; `game/anim/entityAlpha.ts`, `game/shadeHaze.ts`, `render3d/fx/status.ts`, `render3d/fx/booms.ts`; try them in `?r3d=gallery`).
  - Ghost: `ghostPulse` replaces `INCORPOREAL_ALPHA`, a sine between 0.55 and 0.85 once per 1.5 s, out of step per entity (`ghostPhase(id)`). During `ANIM.Ambush` it blinks between 0 and the pulse every 60 ms (`GHOST_FLICKER_SECS`, from `animT`). Death: `FX_KIND.DiedGhost` (`fx/booms.ts`).
  - Shade: drawn a little more solid than other incorporeal figures (`FigureDef.incorporealAlpha` 0.75 instead of `INCORPOREAL_ALPHA` 0.6), wrapped in a veil of pure black strands (#200). The maths is `VeilStrands` in `game/shadeHaze.ts` (pure and tested); `render3d/fx/status.ts` draws it and keeps the state per Shade id (`VEILS`). The strands are rooted inside the body, so they grow out of it. Each has two rest directions: standing, straight out from the body (together a cloud round it, segments 1.6 px, a slow billowing wave); moving, behind it (segments 3 px, a faster ripple). The blend follows the body's own speed. Each point eases toward where it would hang off the one before it, slower toward the tip, and a segment never stretches past `MAX_STRETCH` times its length, so the strands stay attached when it moves. In `ANIM.Ambush` the figure is a flat dark silhouette (`SILHOUETTE_FILL`, no eyes) whose alpha rises from 0.2 to 0.9 over `AMBUSH_WARNING` (`silhouetteAlpha`; the constant is `CONST.AMBUSH_WARNING`, exported from the server). Death (no skull): the veil, with nothing holding it together, drifts apart from the body's centre and fades over `VEIL_DEATH_LIFE` (1 s; `dissolveVeil`, `FX_KIND.ShadeVeil`; a settled `restingVeil` if the state is gone).
  - Frames (`scripts/spectre-frames.ts`, #200): 16x16 like the pack monsters, so they draw at ×1 with the hem on the bottom row. The design is an AI-generated ghost (ChatGPT, supplied by the user), redrawn by hand on a 16x16 grid with Claude Code; the Shade is the same body, all black but its glowing red eyes (the user's choice after the phone review; the veil gives it its shape). The script holds ASCII grids (head and body, two idle hems, four run hems) and the two palettes, checks them and draws `ghost_idle_anim_f0..3`, `ghost_run_anim_f0..3` and the same for the shade. Idle bobs 1 px up on f1 and 1 px down on f3 (the last wisp row is cut off there, as approved); run leans 1 px forward with a trailing wisp. The figure is 12 rows tall and 12 wide; with the bob that is the `body_height` 13 the server uses for shots, and `handY` 6 puts the touch at mid-body. The first version (#169, #170) cut 24x24 frames from two 1536x1024 AI sheets; they looked too big and chunky next to the pack and were dropped (#200). To change the art, edit the grids and run `npm run atlas`.
- **Demon frames** (`scripts/demon-frames.ts`, layout in `src/game/anim/demonFrames.ts`, shared with the client): AI-generated art made for this game at the user's request (2026-10-13), in `client/assets-src/demon/`.
  - The user supplied a front-view pixel demon (`images/demon.jpg`); an image generation AI first turned it to a side view facing right, then drew one sheet per action from that (prompts: one strip per action, flat magenta background, same size as the reference). The source images were deleted from the untracked `images/` inbox after the import (the user's request, 2026-10-13); redoing a strip needs a new sheet.
  - Strips: `demon_idle` (4), `demon_walk` (6: the sheet's images 1, 2, 3, 8 and back through 3, 2, the order the user chose, played at 9 fps; the legs cross in the middle of each stride, seen from the side; the strip holds them in play order; from the user's third walk sheet, `images/demon_walk_improved_cross_walk.png`: the first one, 4 frames, was no clean cycle and looked like a limp, and the second, 8 frames, kept the legs apart), `demon_cleave` (5: wind-up, swing, impact, follow-through, recovery; the user's sheet had 7, the two upright resting poses were dropped), `demon_charge` (4: bull crouch, two charging frames, the stop) and `demon_cast` (4: gathering power, rising, the burst, recovery). Every cell is 82×63 with the hips at x 33 (`hipX`, the body's horizontal origin, mirrored when facing left) and the feet on row 62 (`footY`). Drawn at ×1 (about 62 px tall with the horns).
  - How the sheets were imported (one-off scripts, not in the repo; redo the same for new sheets): the JPGs converted to PNG with PowerShell `System.Drawing`; figures found as connected shapes on the magenta; a pixel grid fitted per figure (pitch and phase from the colour edges; 8.6 px for idle, walk and cast, 5.7 px for the charge); the cleave sheet had been resampled by the AI and has no clean grid, so it was sampled at a fixed 5.45 px that matches the others' size; cell centres sampled (median of the middle half, empty where mostly magenta); colours snapped to one 16-colour palette (k-means over all frames, magenta fringe excluded and dropped on the silhouette); only the figure and pieces touching it kept; each frame placed with the loincloth's centre on `hipX` and its lowest pixel on `footY`. The 8-frame walk sheets (resampled by the AI too) was sampled at a fixed 6.25 px (its figures then match the idle height), snapped to the existing 16 colours, and placed on the average of the loincloth's centre and the eyes (minus their mean distance, 10.1 px for the cross walk), so the head stays within 2 px across the cycle; its tail needed 4 more pixels on the left, so every strip's cells were widened from 78 to 82 px (4 empty columns on the left).
  - `build-atlas.ts` checks each strip's size and copies the frames below the lich's.
- **Lich frames** (`scripts/lich-frames.ts`): pixel art drawn for this game with Claude Code at the user's request (2026-10-13), after two reference images the user supplied (a high-resolution crowned lich, a pixel-art lich). The user approved the look in the gallery page.
  - Pixel grids (one character per pixel) in pack colours: a gold spiked crown, a skull with glowing eyes in a crimson hood, gold crescent pauldrons, a crimson mantle with a jewel clasp, a lean robe with a gold trim strip, and a ragged hem it floats on (no feet).
  - `lich_idle_anim_f0-3` (float bob, hem sway, one eye flicker) and `lich_run_anim_f0-3` (the hem trails behind), 16×28 like the heroes, so it draws at ×1; plus `weapon_lich_staff` (8×30, a bone staff with a purple orb), held 7 px out (`handX`) so it does not cover the right pauldron.
  - The build checks every grid's size and colours; to change the art, edit the grids and run `npm run atlas`.
- **Title logo** (`scripts/build-logo.ts`, run by `npm run atlas`): made for this game with Google's image generation AI (2026-10-12), in `client/assets-src/logo/logo.png` (1408×768; the generator's watermark in the bottom-right corner was painted over once with wall from its left). The script writes three files into `public/assets/`:
  - `logo.png`: the whole image. The login screen shows it large (`.lobby.title`, `h1.logo.splash` from `ui/logo.ts`); every other `.lobby` screen uses it as a darkened background, darkest in the middle so the emblem behind the panels disappears and only the walls show (on portrait screens the left wall).
  - `logo-emblem.png`: the shield with the title (crop x 370, y 78, 680×626), the small header of the character select, lobby and error page.
  - `favicon.png`: the treasure chest on top, area-averaged to 64×64. The admin page has no favicon.
  - The `<h1>` around the images keeps the name for screen readers (`alt`); the edges fade out with `mask-image` (header from 70% to 85% of the radius, so the full width of ADVENTURERS stays opaque).
- **Recolors:** `RECOLORS` in `build-atlas.ts` swaps whole pack colors for other pack colors and writes the result as new frames in a strip below the sheet (32 px per entry). Today: `summoner_anim_f0-3`, the necromancer with its purple robe (`5f2d56`, trim `9f294e`) turned maroon and red (`62232f`, `da4e38`, the imp's and chort's reds). `scripts/png.ts` is a small PNG codec (8-bit RGB/RGBA, no interlace), so no image library is needed.
- No other self-drawn sprites: only the terrain tiles, the lich and the Ghost and Shade redraw, all at the user's request. See CLAUDE.md "Art".
- Runtime effect shapes (particles, glows, swooshes, rings; `render3d/fx/`) are generated geometry, and the ground shadow ellipses under figures are quads (`pushShadow`); no art files.

**Terrain, board and the boss hall.**
- The 3D view draws the map from `heights.ts` and `worldMesh.ts` (§9 "World geometry and the 3D run": walls are full blocks with faces and tops, floors and the woodland's ground are flat quads) and the terrain from `dips/`, `water/` and `lava/`. The atlas also holds the generated terrain tiles (`terrain_*`), which the 3D view does not draw.
- The boss hall entrance has no door: open and sealed entrance tiles both draw as floor. When it is sealed, a shimmering blue force field stands over it (§9 "World geometry and the 3D run", "Effects in 3D (#308)"). It is built from the map, so players who join or spectate later see it too.

**Figures (`anim/defs.ts` → `figurePose` → `Figures3d`).**
- `FigureDef` describes:
  - idle/run/hit frame prefixes (4-frame pack animations)
  - the weapon frame and how it is held (`blade` / `bow` / `staff`)
  - the hand height, an accent colour (`base`, used by spell particles and dash afterimages) and a footprint radius (`baseR`, the ground shadow size)
  - an optional scale or tint
  - an optional `swing` (reach and arc of the melee swoosh, matching the server's damage reach)
  - optional `mouth` / `nostrils` frame points
  - for figures with their own action frames (the dragon, the demon): `frames` (prefix, count, fps per action), `windups` (attack anim → wind-up seconds), `lift` (hover height), `footY` (the feet row when the frames leave room below), `hipX` (the hips' column when the figure is not centred in its frames), `bite`; `pickFrame.ts` chooses their frame
- Pack stand-ins:
  - the demon is not a pack figure: it has its own AI-generated frames (see Demon frames above), drawn at ×1
  - the lich is not a pack figure: it has its own frames (see Lich frames above), drawn at ×1
  - the summoner is the pack necromancer with a red robe (an atlas recolor, see above)
  - the dragon is not a pack figure: it has its own AI-generated frames (see Dragon frames above), drawn at ×1.2
- Weapons are drawn at 0.6× (`WEAPON_SCALE` in `game/anim/pose.ts`).
- The pack has no attack frames, so attacks are approximated:

| Attack | How it looks |
|---|---|
| Melee (blade) | Weapon fades out at the swing start angle; a crescent swoosh sweeps the damage arc at the damage reach; the weapon fades in at the end angle. Small lunge and tilt of the body. |
| Bow / crossbow | Recoil and spark. The assassin's dagger is a short thrust. |
| Staff / cast | Staff thrust and sparkles. |
| Wind-up | Body flashes red, shakes, weapon drawn back. |
| Dash | Stretched body and afterimages. |
| Dragon breath | Particles from the mouth (per-frame point from `dragonPoints()`, follows bob, flip, lift and scale) into the ground cone. During the wind-up, smoke comes from the nostrils. |
| Dragon flight | The body rises by `lift` over its ground shadow (which shrinks); the landing draws the slam ring at `DRAGON_SMASH_RADIUS` over `DRAGON_LAND`. |

- Facing is left/right only. The `Dir` type and a `TODO(4-dir)` mark where 4-direction sheets would plug in.

**Figures stand on a soft ground shadow** (`pushShadow` in `render3d/cards.ts`: a soft floor ellipse), not on a miniature base; the bases were removed at the user's request.

**Chests and mimics (`game/objectPose.ts`, drawn by `Figures3d`).** The pack has three 3-frame chest animations (`chest_full_open`, `chest_empty_open`, `chest_mimic_open`); frame 0 is the same closed chest in all three, so a mimic cannot be told apart by its look.
- The chest (`chestFrame`): closed, then the lid opens over the gold (0.3 s), then the empty chest.
- The mimic (`mimicPose`, `MimicTracker`): there are no walk or idle frames, so everything is the three opening frames plus squash and stretch. Hiding: closed chest that rattles now and then when the local hero is within 40 px. Reveal (the `HIDING` flag drops while in view): jump with the lid flying open, gold burst and a red "!". Hop (Move): crouch (lid ajar, squashed), flight (jaws open, stretched, a parabolic arc 7 px high with the shadow left on the floor), landing (squash, dust). Bite (Melee): lunge with the jaws snapping. Wind-up: crouched.
- In the 3D view the chest and the Mimic can be models instead of these cards (§9 "Object models: chest, Mimic, barrel (#317)"): `chestLid(opened)` is the lid's opening, 0 to 1, a smoothstep over the 0.3 s of the opening (`OPEN_T`); `chestLoot(opened)` says whether the loot is still in (until `EMPTY_AFTER`); `MimicPose` carries `lid`, `lunge` and `pitch` for the model, which the card path ignores.
- Coins: pack `coin_anim` frames fly out with gravity as cards; a gold "+N coins" floats up.

**Other visuals.**
- Projectiles: `weapon_arrow` for arrows and bolts, additive glows for magic.
- Deaths: a `skull` decal.
- Lich protection beams (a visual only; the lich no longer drains or heals): animated red beams from channelling disciples to the lich. Drawn from `Snapshot.drain` (the lich first, then each channelling disciple; on the wire a flat scaled array, the 8th `SnapW` field), which the server sends during the boss fight, or while the lich or a disciple is in view, regardless of the field of view; a beam is not drawn while the lich is hidden by the sight (§9 "Effects in 3D (#308)").
- The lich's magic is dark purple gloom (`GLOOM` in `game/fxLooks.ts`): gloom bolts flicker and trail dark smoke and pale sparks (`PROJECTILES[...].gloom`); the nova telegraph is a purple ring with wisps drawn in (`FigureDef.slam.gloom`), the lich flashes pale purple and holds its staff high during the wind-up, and the burst is the nova effect; its raised skeletons appear in a dark purple puff (`RAISE_LOOKS`).
- Debug paths: the server sends them to debug runs and the badge lists their colours, but the 3D view does not draw them (§13).

**Canvas and zoom.** The 3D canvas fills the window and renders at screen resolution (`devicePixelRatio`, capped at 2, in `render3d/host.ts`); the whole-number zoom comes from the window (`pickZoom`) and the quality decides how much of it the scene pass renders (§9 "Quality levels"). Text in the world is Inter and needs real pixels, so it is drawn by the overlay at the canvas's device pixels.

**World texts (`game/worldText.ts`, `render3d/overlay.ts`).**
- All text drawn in the world uses Inter (`FONT_TEXT`). `WORLD_TEXT` holds the sizes and styles in world pixels (tag 6.5 px at 600, number 7 px at 800, crit 9 px at 800 in gold `#ffd24a`, mark 7 px for the sleep Z and the "!", sound 6 px italic, small 6 px for "+N coins" and "IMMUNE"); at zoom 2 that is about 13 screen pixels for a name tag. The overlay draws them at these sizes times the zoom (§9 "Overlays, input and turning in the 3D run").
- A crit is its number alone in gold at the crit size, with a short pop (`popScale`: 1.4 easing to 1 in 0.12 s); the old "CRIT" word is gone.
- The use hint (`layoutUseHint`) is a gold key cap with a dark "E" and an optional white label ("Wake") to its right (null label: the bare cap, at a chest or door).
- One text resolution policy: every overlay text, the rules-log tags included, is drawn at the 3D canvas's device pixels.

**Fonts.** Self-hosted, no CDN: `client/public/fonts/` holds Inter (variable, weights 100 to 900, normal and italic, latin subset) and IM Fell English SC (400, headings only), both from the Fontsource npm packages (5.3.0) with their OFL licence texts. `client/src/fonts.css` has the `@font-face` rules and is imported by `style.css` and `admin/admin.css`. Family strings: text `'Inter', system-ui, sans-serif` (`--font-text`, `FONT_TEXT`), heading `'IM Fell English SC', Georgia, serif` (`--font-head`, `FONT_HEAD`). `loadFonts()` in `fonts.ts` loads every face the canvas draws with and is awaited before the run's screens start, so canvas text never renders in a fallback; it never rejects and gives up after 3 s. `fonts.test.ts` fails if the CSS, HTML or font code mentions `googleapis`, `gstatic`, Press Start or JetBrains. Sizes: body 15 px, small 13 px, smallest 11.5 px, HUD 13 px, nothing in the HTML UI below 11 px; digits that change in place use `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`.

**UI.** DOM overlay (`ui/`):
- Roster, character creation and the character hub with its four tabs (§11a "The character screens"), then the lobby with the run list and the waiting room.
- HUD (`ui/hud.ts`): HP, two ability slots, party list, boss bar with the IMMUNE tag, messages, spectate banner, Esc menu with Settings, run coin counter (pack coin icon via CSS from the atlas), end screen with stats, XP and coins, F3 debug panel, debug badge.
  - **Cooldown ring** (`ui/cooldown.ts`): the slot's gold rim fills clockwise from the top as the cooldown runs down, with the seconds left (`toFixed(1)`) in the slot. `slotView(cd, max)` gives fill, ready and label; `CooldownTracker` learns the slot's maximum from the values it sees (a new use, the Berserker speed-up) and reports `flash` on the update where the slot becomes ready (a short flash).
  - **Effect symbols** (`ui/effectIcons.ts`): one 16 x 16 SVG body per effect, a fill or a line style and a "weak" variant (outline or thinner line) for the lesser tier (Singed, Chilled, Tainted). `EFFECT_ICON` is a `Record` typed by the effect ids, so `tsc` fails on a missing symbol. `effectIconSvg(id, px)` feeds the HUD (own hero with the whole seconds left, party list without), `effectIconDataUri` feeds the overlay's name tags. The letters and `effectGlyph` are gone.
  - **Name tags** (`layoutHeroTags` in `render3d/overlayLayout.ts`): a row of symbol images to the right of a hero's name tag (`ICON_SIZE` 5.5 world px each, the first 2 px from the tag and 1 px between symbols); other heroes only, the own hero has no tag. No effect words are drawn in the world.
  - **Messages:** `message(text, 'game' | 'notice')`. The text is set with `textContent` (server messages carry player names). `game` is the heading face in the centre (force field, and everything that comes from the server as `Ev::Msg`, such as "{name} left the dungeon" and the debug effect line of F8); `notice` is the small client line (debug on or off, "You have fallen").
  - **Asleep panel:** `setAsleep(on)` shows a panel with a moon above the HP bar (`RunSession.frame` calls it from the own effects). The "E Wake" over a sleeping teammate is the overlay's use hint with the label "Wake".
  - **Help line and menu:** key caps (`helpHtml`, `<kbd>`), the Esc menu titled "Paused" with a hint.
  - **Settings** (`ui/settings.ts`): the Esc menu's Settings view, one checkbox row per `Toggle { label, key, get, set }` and a Back button. `RulesLog.settings()` supplies Rules log (L) and Calculation lines (Shift+L), `newRun` (`render3d/runStage.ts`) passes them to `Hud.setToggles` and the run's end clears them (`startRun3d().stop()`), and the Hud adds Network stats (F3). The keys and the checkboxes stay in sync because the view re-reads the toggles (`sync`) when it opens and on each frame while open. The Quality row is a `Choice` (a radio row of `[value, name]` options with `get` and `set`): `runStage.ts` hands the host's quality to `Hud.setChoices`, and a pick goes back through `host.setParams` (§10). The rules log adds one line to the rules-log boundary (§11d).

### The run session and the view interface (#302)
