A run's logic and its pictures are apart. `RunSession` (`client/src/run/session.ts`) holds everything that decides what is true in a run and imports nothing from `render3d/`; a view draws what the session hands it. Three pieces: the session, a view behind the `RunView` interface (`run/view.ts`), and a host that owns the frame loop and the raw keys and creates both. The game is `render3d/host.ts` (the frame loop and the `FrameTimer`) with `runStage.ts` (the keys, the click latches, the Esc, Q, E and F keys as calls on the session) and `runView3d.ts` (the view).

**What the session owns.** The `TileMap` and the `LightField`; the `Predictor` and the `Interpolator`; the entity map; the last snapshot; alive, spectating and own flags; the disabled slots; the debug data (`DebugDraw`). The network subscription and its messages (`onMessage`: `Snap`, `HeroChanged`, `RunEnded`, `DebugPath`, `DebugDodge`); the events that change state (`onEvent`: `Tile` changes the map and the light field, then tells the view; `Light`, `LightAdd`, `LightRemove`, `Msg`, the HUD part of `Coins`); the fixed step and ability prediction; the own predicted shots (`Shots`, `run/shots.ts`); and every call into the HUD, so any view has the whole HUD. The debug keys (F4, F7, F8, F9) are methods on the session (`debugDarkvision` and so on), which ignores them outside a debug run and while dead.

**One frame.** The host calls `RunSession.frame(deltaMs)` once per display frame. It runs the fixed steps (at most five), samples the interpolator, works out the own hero as drawn (`OwnHero`: predicted, or the interpolated hero while charmed) and the camera target (the own hero, or the watched teammate), advances the shots, picks the use hint, then calls `RunView.frame(RunFrame)` and passes the `ViewStats` it returns to `Hud.frame` with its own numbers.

**The interface.**
- `RunView.frame(f)` draws one frame and returns the numbers for the F3 panel (`ViewStats`; `NO_STATS` for a view that has none). `RunFrame` carries `dt` and `now`, the interpolated entities, the own hero, the spectated teammate, the camera target, the last snapshot (lights, looks, drain, `me.vision`, `me.darkvision`), the predicted shots and which server shots to hide, the use hint and the debug lines.
- `event(ev, own)` takes the events that only have a picture (`PictureEv`: `Dmg`, `Heal`, `Boom`, `Zap`, `Died`, `Sink`, `Raise`, `Immune`, `Effect`, `Coins`, `Cast`); camera shake is the view's reaction to them. `tileChanged(x, y, v)` comes after the session has updated the map and the light field. `shotEnded(shot, how)` ends a predicted shot's picture. `destroy()` frees the view.
- A view is made from `RunViewInit` (`session.viewInit()`: the run's start info, the session's map and light field, shared and read only for the view, and the own kind) and given to the session with `setView`; with no view the session still runs and the HUD still gets its frame.

**Rules.**
- The session asks the view one question only: `pointerWorld()`, the simulation px under the pointer (or `null`), for the aim. Everything else flows from the session to the view.
- Picture-only state stays in the view: entity views, wading and sinking, the vanish and reappear marks, skull decals, bar labels, the force field's age, the rules log's panel and tags. The session does not keep what only a picture needs.
- The interface grows by fields and events, never by back doors. A view that needs one more datum gets a field on `RunFrame` or `RunViewInit`, or a new event; it does not reach into the session, and the session does not reach into a view.
- A `RunFrame` and the objects in it (the entity map, `own`, `shots`, the debug arrays) are valid only during the call: the session reuses them, so a view copies what it keeps.
- Lights and sight reach a view as data it already has: the shared `LightField` and the snapshot's lights and vision numbers. The view makes its own picture of sight (the 3D view's `SightMask`).

**How the 3D view plugs in** (#302, "The 3D run" below). Its stage builds a `RunSession` with its own `input` function (a `StepInput` per fixed step, from its keys; `dir8` in `run/input.ts` rounds a direction to the eight the wire carries), creates its `RunView`, calls `session.setView(view)` and then `session.frame(deltaMs)` each frame, and calls `session.destroy()` and `view.destroy()` at the end. A view without a pointer (or while the pointer is paused, as in the 3D view during a middle-mouse drag) returns `null` from `pointerWorld()`; the aim then follows the movement direction. `RunSession.receive(m)` hands the session a message by hand, through the same handler as the net's (the 3D run replays the messages that arrived while its code loaded). `run/` has no bare import (no library), `render3d/` has only `three/webgpu` and `three/tsl`, and `main.ts` reaches `render3d/` only through its dynamic import of `./render3d/boot`, which `imports.test.ts` checks (§12); the session's HUD is DOM, so a run in the 3D view has the whole HUD without extra code. `run/testing.ts` has the fakes for a view's own tests.

### The 3D view (#301, #302)

The 3D view is the game's only view (#300, #310). Every run, for every account, is drawn here (#302, "The 3D run" below), and `?r3d=<page>` opens a dev page with no server and no login (§10); `?r3d=demo` is #301's scene (the reference demo's hall, plus a corridor 3 tiles wide seen across, one seen along, and a second room's near wall). Everything is in `client/src/render3d/`.

**Library and boundaries.**
- `three` is pinned to exactly `0.187.0` (`@types/three` the same), with no caret. The code imports it only as `three/webgpu` and `three/tsl`; a bare `three` import would load a second build.
- The folder's imports from outside are shared modules: `../ui/atlas` (the atlas loader), `../game/frameTimer`, `../run/` (the session), `../sim/map` (`TileMap`), `../game/terrain-layers` and `../game/terrain-codes` (terrain ids and codes), `../game/woodland/ground` (the ground painter), `../game/anim/` data (the class figures). `imports.test.ts` reads the files and checks that no file under `src` imports the old 2D engine package (and that `package.json` lists none), that the folder's bare specifiers are only `three/webgpu` and `three/tsl` and `run/` has none, and that `main.ts` has no static import of the folder (§12).
- `main.ts` reaches the folder only through `import('./render3d/boot')`: for `?r3d=<page>` at once; for a run on `Welcome` (preloaded for every account, so the chunk is usually there at `RunStarted`) and on `RunStarted`. If the chunk fails to load at `RunStarted`, the client sends `LeaveRun` (#316) and goes back to the lobby with the error "The 3D view could not load"; the next `RunStarted` retries the import. three.js is its own chunk, so the login and lobby pages stay light: the production build's `index` chunk is about 134 kB and the lazily loaded `boot` chunk (three.js and the 3D view) about 1,344 kB. `imports.test.ts` checks that `main.ts` has no static import of the folder.
- `WebGPURenderer` uses WebGPU where the browser offers it and falls back to WebGL2 by itself; `backend=webgl` in the URL forces the fallback, for every account. The panel's readout (dev pages and debug `?r3d` runs) and F3 name the backend in use.

**Structure.** Everything that can be a plain function is one and has a vitest test: `camera.ts`, `quality.ts`, `params.ts`, `heights.ts`, `demoMap.ts`, `worldMesh.ts`, `dips/` (the shape, the dip mesh and the descents), `lava/` (the lava's meshes, bubbles and pools), `fieldMesh.ts`, `cover/` (the ground cover's pure parts), `scene.ts`, `cards.ts`, `light.ts`, `recover.ts`, `stats.ts`; `urlWriter.ts`; `panel.ts` is plain DOM with a jsdom test. Three files use three.js: `view.ts` (the renderer, the chunk meshes, the render target; rebuilt whole after a lost device) and the TSL in `materials.ts` and `lightNodes.ts`, whose nodes and uniforms are made anew for every view.

**Host and stages** (`host.ts`). `startHost(el, search, mode, makeStage)` owns the canvas, the one frame loop, the held keys, the panel, the F3 readout, the parameters and the recovery; a **stage** (`Stage`) owns what is drawn: its `world` (a `WorldInput`: the `TileMap`, whether the ground is painted, the clear colour), `built(view)` after every (re)build, `frame(ctx)` per frame, `key(e)` for keys the host does not handle (when it returns true the host calls `preventDefault`), `stats()` for F3 and `dispose()`. `demoStage.ts` is #301's scene, `runStage.ts` the run. The frame context (`StageCtx`) carries two times: `dt`, clamped to 0.001..0.05 s (`frameDt`), for the stages' own animation and the camera, and `frameMs`, the raw time since the last animation frame (0 on the first). The run stage hands `frameMs` to `session.frame`, which clamps to 0.1 s and runs at most 5 fixed steps itself, (with the clamped `dt` a frame longer than 50 ms lost game time, #312). The stage's state and the parameters live outside the view and survive a rebuild.
**Host modes** (`HostMode`). `startHost(el, search, mode, makeStage)` runs in one of three modes, which decide what the player or developer gets:

| Mode | Used for | Parameters | Panel and keys | Address bar | Quality |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `demo` | the `?r3d=<page>` dev pages (`boot.ts`) | `parseParams(search)` | the panel (mode `demo`) and every dev key | rewritten by the panel | from the URL; never stored |
| `run` | a debug account's run with `?r3d` in the URL | `parseParams(search)` | the panel (mode `run`) and every dev key | rewritten (the yaw never) | the URL's `quality` when it has one for this run, else the stored one; every change is stored |
| `play` | every other run | `DEFAULTS` plus the URL's `backend` and the stored quality | no panel (`NO_PANEL`); the free camera is off | not touched | every change is stored |

`startRun3d` (`boot.ts`) passes `run` when `deps.dev` is true (`main.ts`: `debugAccount && ?r3d` in the URL) and `play` otherwise; `Host.dev` is true in `demo` and `run`. **One quality state:** `params.quality` is the host's; the Esc menu's Quality row and the panel both read and change it through the host's `apply`, which stores every change in `localStorage` (key `ta.quality`, `render3d/qualityPref.ts`; `loadQuality` gives `balanced` when nothing valid is stored or storage throws; every read and write is inside try/catch). A `run` host's `&quality=` is the start value of that run and is not written back until the player changes it. `lag`, `jitter` and `loss` are read by `net.ts` for every account and `backend=webgl` by every mode.
- `Host.stop()` can be called at any point, also while the host is still starting (then `startHost` returns `null`): it cancels the frame, removes its key and blur listeners, cancels a pending address-bar write, disposes the view, removes the canvas, the panel, the readout and its error boxes, calls `stage.dispose()` and deletes `window.__r3d` if that is still its own. A second host on the same element therefore starts clean (a second run in one page). `startHost` checks for a stop after each await; a throwing `makeStage` or first `built` stops the host before the error goes on. An error box a failed host leaves up stays for the player to read until the next host starts on the element or the run's `stop()` (`clearErrors`).
- Keys typed into an `input`, `textarea` or `select` are not the stage's. F3 asks `stage.stats()` first: the run's stage opens the HUD's panel instead of the host's readout.

**Coordinates and camera** (`camera.ts`).
- World units are tiles: `x` east, `z` south, `y` up; 16 art px are 1 tile. Gameplay stays 2D on the plane `y = 0`.
- The camera is orthographic. Pitch is the angle between the view direction and the ground; yaw turns it around `y`. With `c = cos(yaw)`, `s = sin(yaw)` and a point measured from the camera's target: `xr = dx*c + dz*s`, `zr = dz*c - dx*s`, screen `x' = xr`, `y' = -zr*sin(pitch) + y*cos(pitch)`. `project` is tested against a three.js `OrthographicCamera` built the way `view.ts` builds it (on the view ray behind the target, up `(0, 1, 0)`), and its inverse on the ground, `groundAt`, against `project`. The camera's distance is `50 + halfH / tan(pitch)` tiles, so the near plane never cuts the bottom of the screen at a low pitch; an orthographic picture does not depend on the distance.
- Zoom is a whole number of screen px per art px, picked from the window (`pickZoom`, 2 to 8) or forced with `zoom=N`. The dev pages and the panel's slider go closer: `zoom=1..48` (`ZOOM_MAX` in `params.ts`, #356; at 48 the 12 px Goblin fills most of a 768 px window), for close-ups of the figure models (`r3d-shot.mjs ... "&zoom=40"`). Past zoom 8 the dev pages' camera aim rises (`closeCam` in `camera.ts`, applied to the camera handed to `View.draw` in every `*Stage.ts`, none in a run), fully from zoom 16: the focused figure's middle (`HUMAN_HEIGHT / 2` above the floor) comes to mid-screen instead of its foot, so a whole figure stays in the picture (up to zoom 8 the camera is unchanged). `pickZoom` and a real game's automatic zoom stay at 2 to 8; there is no wheel zoom.
- `keysToWorld` turns WASD into world directions by the yaw; `facesLeft` decides a figure's facing on screen with a small dead zone, so it does not flip while walking straight away from the camera.

**Pixel handling.**
- **Screen-space snap** (`snapGround`): the camera's target and every card's foot are moved so that their screen position, measured from the world origin, is a whole number of art px at the current pitch and yaw. (Rounding to 1/16 tile in the world, as the reference demo does, is a whole pixel only without tilt.)
- **Cards** (figures, crates): a quad standing on its foot, turned to the camera's yaw. The vertex stage stretches its height by `1 / cos(pitch)`, so it shows its full sprite height on screen; its light uses the unstretched position. Torch flames lie in their wall's plane and are not stretched. A glow is an additive quad that faces the camera, so it is round on screen. `buildDynamic` moves its centre toward the camera along the view ray by its own size plus 0.05 tiles (`GLOW_LIFT`), which in an orthographic view changes only its depth: the whole disc then lies in front of the torch's wall, the wall top and the floor. (Left at the torch, the quad leans into the wall and loses half its halo; an upright quad stretched by the pitch dips below the floor instead.) The cost: a glow is added over a figure standing within about its size in front of a torch, and with the camera looking along a wall the part of a torch's disc that lies over the wall is still hidden. In the same way, and as in the reference demo, cards are moved 0.03 tiles and flames 0.05 tiles toward the camera (`materials.ts`), so they win against the surface they stand in front of.
- **Textures** are nearest-filtered with no mipmaps, `flipY = false` and `premultiplyAlpha = false`; uv coordinates are in atlas px with the frame's top row at the top (`uv / atlasSize`, no flip).
- **Smoothed ground** (a switch, on by default; the user's pick after the hardware check): floors read a second, linear-filtered copy of the atlas at a position sharpened to the texel borders (`seam + clamp((p - seam) / fwidth(p), -0.5, 0.5)`), clamped half a texel inside the frame's rect because the atlas has no padding. Apart from the off-screen target's texture when it is copied at a ratio that is not a whole number, this is the one texture that is not nearest. The switch is a uniform, so toggling it compiles nothing; walls, wall tops and cards are never smoothed.
- The wall top is generated in the shader: dark stone with a pale rim where the top borders something lower (from the height texture). The user kept this look for #302 (2026-11-11); it is tuned once the whole 3D view stands.

**Quality levels** (`quality.ts`; the player picks one in the Esc menu's Settings, §10). `effZoom` gives the render px per art px: High the screen's zoom; Fast `min(2, zoom)`; Balanced the largest divisor of the zoom up to half of it, at least 2 (else half the zoom, rounded up). `renderPlan` turns that into one frame's sizes: at the screen's zoom the scene is drawn straight to the canvas; below it, into an off-screen target that is copied to the canvas with one quad, nearest when the ratio is a whole number and linear otherwise. The copy is centred and is up to `zoom - 1` px larger than the canvas, so it may start up to 4 px outside it. The viewport is passed through unclamped: WebGL2 and Chromium's WebGPU crop it; other WebGPU implementations are not verified (if one drops the copy, offset the uv in the copy's node instead). There is no pixel grid mode (dropped by the user, 2026-11-11).

**Colour.** The reference demo's numbers are raw values, so the renderer converts nothing: `outputColorSpace = LinearSRGBColorSpace`, `NoToneMapping`, and every texture has `colorSpace = NoColorSpace`. A later package may revisit this when effects need linear light.

**Light** (`light.ts`, `lightNodes.ts`): the proof that TSL carries this renderer.
- `lightAt(P, N, wrap)` is ambient plus up to 8 point lights (two `uniformArray`s of 8 `Vector4`, updated in place every frame): a smooth falloff to the radius, the surface angle softened by `wrap` (floor 0.35, wall face 0.5, wall top 0.5 `TOP_WRAP`, cards 1 with no normal), and a soft shoulder on the result.
- **Wall tops (#342).** A top is drawn in `STONE` (`light.ts`: a cool stone, darker than the brick faces) and takes the point lights **without the wall and card test** (`lightAt`'s option `open`, a bool node; `worldMaterial` passes `isTop`). The lights hang below the tops, so the wall walk would leave only ragged wedges of light on them. With the sight mask this gives a one-tile stone band around a lit room that the hero sees, so a wall reads as a wall from every camera turn, also from behind, where no brick face points at the camera; the rock further in stays unseen and dark. The look is approved (the user, 2026-11-16). Darkvision does not show tops and reaches nothing beyond a wall (#328; the user confirmed it for this band too). Before #342 the tops were near black (`STONE` 0.115, 0.1, 0.145 with wrap 0.15) and melted into the clear colour.
- `wallVis(P, L)` is the wall shadow: a grid walk from the lit point to the light over a one-channel texture of wall heights (one texel per tile, read with `textureLoad`), at most 40 steps, started 0.03 tiles along the normal. It is a TSL `Loop(40)` with `Break()`, ported line by line from the demo's GLSL, and it compiles unchanged to GLSL (WebGL2) and WGSL (WebGPU).
- The scene has six wall torches with the demo's flicker and a moving orb; switches turn the wall shadows and the orb off.

**Lost device or context** (`recover.ts`, `view.ts`, `host.ts`). three.js 0.187 reports a loss (`renderer.onDeviceLost`) and then stops; it restores nothing. So the view is rebuilt whole on a new canvas: renderer, textures, target and meshes.
- `createRecovery` runs one rebuild at a time. A loss reported while one runs leads to one more rebuild after it; a rebuild that fails is logged (`console.warn`) and tried again after 1 s.
- Each view reports its loss through the generation it was made in, so a late report from a replaced view is ignored. A loss before boot has finished only sets a flag and starts the recovery at boot's end.
- `Renderer.dispose()` is async in three.js: the lost renderer's disposal is fire-and-forget with its rejection caught, and a `createView` that fails disposes its own renderer.
- While a rebuild runs, the scene keeps stepping, drawing is skipped and the readout says "Graphics device lost, restoring…". The frame loop asks for its next frame first and catches an exception from the frame's body (logged once per message), so one throw, for example from a context that is lost but not yet reported, does not stop the page. The panel's `Lose device (debug)` button forces a loss (WebGPU: `device.destroy()`, reported by hand because three ignores the reason `destroyed`; WebGL2: `WEBGL_lose_context`).

**Parameters and the address bar** (`params.ts`, `urlWriter.ts`, `host.ts`). `parseParams` gives the default or the clamped value for anything unreadable, so no NaN reaches the camera. `toSearch(p, current)` writes this page's keys and keeps every other key of the address as it is (`?lag=150`, a key without a value). Every change, from the panel, F3 or a stage's `host.setParams`, goes through one `apply` in `host.ts`, which keeps the parameters, the panel's copy, the F3 readout and the timer in step. The address bar is written by `trailingWriter` 200 ms after the last change, because browsers throttle `history.replaceState` and would drop the final value of a slider drag. A renderer that cannot be created or initialised throws `RendererInitError`, and only that shows the "needs WebGPU or WebGL2" message; any other start-up error shows its own text.

**Draw calls.** three's `info.render.drawCalls` adds up over a frame and is reset by three's own animation loop. `view.ts` switches `info.autoReset` off, resets the counter at the start of `draw` and reads it once after the last render, so the number is this frame's (the scene's calls plus one for the copy).

**What a headless run can show.** The headless WebGL2 path (SwiftShader) draws the scene, and `tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs` uses it. A WebGPU canvas cannot be captured headless on the development machine: with the SwiftShader adapter it reads back blank, with the real graphics card scrambled, and a raw WebGPU triangle without three.js does the same. A WebGPU run there shows only that the shaders compile and frames run without errors; the WebGPU picture is checked on a real screen. `r3d-shot.mjs` and `r3d-run.mjs` use a 1280x768 window (`R3D_W` / `R3D_H` override): on the development notebook WebGPU loses its device silently at heights such as 720, 900 and 1080 while the page keeps counting frames, so both scripts fail on a console warning that says "device lost" (except around a deliberate `forceLoss()`). `READBACK=1` saves `r3d-readback.png`, the scene read back by `window.__r3d.readback()` (`View.readback()` re-draws the last frame into an RGBA8 target and reads it, top row first): the way to look at a WebGPU frame, since a canvas capture is black (#306 used it to check that the sun's shadows fall the same way on both backends; it shows the scene, not the presented frame). `HEADED=1` with the `webgpu` mode runs a visible window on the real graphics card. `r3d-run.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [dungeon|woodland]` plays a run and, on the woodland, loses the device at the end, checks that frames resume and saves before/after pictures to compare. A headed Playwright window on the real graphics card (`headless: false`) draws WebGL2 correctly at the real frame rate, but its WebGPU capture is black too.

### World geometry and the 3D run (#302)

The 3D view draws any map the server generates, inside a real run. The design and the plan are on issue #302.

**One rule per tile kind** (`heights.ts`, `worldMesh.ts`). Every cell has a height and a surface; nothing depends on the level.
- Height (`cellHeight`, read only through `heightAt`): `Wall` and `Void` are `WALL_H` (2 tiles), every other tile 0; outside the map counts as `Void`. `Void` is solid rock: no wall shows a face toward the outside, and between `Wall` and `Void` there is no face. The height per cell is the hook for #167 (heights in levels) and #38 (pillars).
- A cell of height 2 gets a top quad (kind `top`) and, on each side whose neighbour is lower, one face quad per tile of height (kind `face`). Face frames: `wall_mid`; on a straight run (the cells beside it along the wall have a full face on the same side) the lower row now and then `wall_hole_1`, `wall_hole_2` or `wall_goo`, the upper row now and then a banner, by a hash of cell and side (`faceFrame`; shares 2.5 %, 2 %, 1.5 %, banners 1.5 %), on all four sides.
- A cell of height 0: nothing when the dip mesh draws it (`inDipMesh`: a terrain cell, or a floor cell with a terrain cell among its eight neighbours; "Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)" below); in a woodland run a ground quad for `GROUND_TILES` (`Floor`, `Road`, `Thicket`, `Trunk`, `Barrier`; kind `ground`, uv in the painted image's px); else a floor quad, `floor_1` mostly and `floor_2`–`floor_8` by hash (`floorFrame`, 7 %). No per-tile tint, grid lines or table frame.
- The wall-height texture (`heightBytes`, one byte per tile, `round(height * 100)`) feeds the top's rim and `wallVis`; a tile change rewrites its texel.

**Chunks.** The map is cut into 16 by 16 tiles (`CHUNK`; edge chunks are narrower, a map smaller than one chunk is one chunk). `buildChunk(map, painted, cx, cz, frames)` is a pure function returning the arrays of floor, ground, faces and tops; `view.ts` makes one mesh per chunk with a bounding box and three's frustum culling (`chunks.drawn` counts what three drew last frame; on a dungeon about 6 of 90 to 110). A tile change (`View.tileChanged`) rebuilds the chunks of the cell and its eight neighbours that lie in the grid (`chunksOfTile`, up to nine, because a face's straight-run test and the terrain edges read diagonal cells), the height texel and the force field. Today the only tile changes are the boss hall's entrance.

**Terrain** (`dips/`, `water/`, `lava/`). Terrain is sunk into the ground since #324: the ground of a terrain cell is the dip mesh ("Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)" below). Water has its own surface ("Water in 3D (#345)") and so has lava ("Lava in 3D (#344)"); a chasm has none. The atlas's generated terrain tiles are not drawn in 3D.

**The force field** (`fieldMesh.ts`). Over every `DoorClosed` cell a translucent blue box of wall height (unlit, depth-tested but not written, `renderOrder` 2), with sides only toward cells lower than a wall and not themselves sealed, plus its top. Rebuilt on every tile change. Its material, glitter and burst are described in §9 "Effects in 3D (#308)", "The force field". The message "A force field seals the hall behind you!" comes from the session, so the force field's message shows in every run.

**The woodland's ground** (`cover/woodland.ts`, `cover/coverJob.ts`; "Ground cover (#330)" below). The cover job paints the ground with `GroundPainter` (`game/woodland/ground.ts`) and the run's seed, in row bands nearest the spawn first (`bandOrder`), then blades, then flowers, at most `COVER_BUDGET_MS` (4 ms) per frame and at least one unit per step. It paints twice: the bare picture (every grass tile as road) is the ground, the cover picture (roads as grass) gives the cover its colours. Until the job is done the ground shows a flat grass colour (`GRASS_FLAT`, uniform `groundReady` 0); then `View.setGround` uploads the bare image once (`map.w * 16` by `map.h * 16` px, nearest plus a linear copy for the smoothed lookup; the two texture nodes are `mergeable = false`, or three merges them into one binding and the smoothing switch does nothing). An image larger than the device's texture limit throws `GroundTooLargeError`, shown in the host's error box; the run keeps the flat grass and goes on without cover. After a lost device the kept pixels are uploaded again, not painted again.

**Clear colour.** Dungeon: `stoneClear(ambient)`, the colour a wall top renders without point lights, so beyond the map's edge the rock goes on; woodland: `WOODLAND_CLEAR`, a dark green. three's clear does not reach the canvas where nothing is drawn (WebGL2 on the real graphics card; SwiftShader does not show it), so the canvas also gets the same colour as its CSS background (`cssColour`), written when it changes.

**Light in this package.** A run has two lights (`R3dParams.light`, `'game' | 'work'`, URL `light=work`; the default is `game`, and an old `light=lantern` reads as `game`): the **game's light** (#304, "The dungeon's light" below), the default in the dungeon, and the **work light** (always in the woodland, where light waits for its own issue): ambient `WORK_AMBIENT` 0.9, no point lights, the torch glow quad, so the whole map is visible. The ambient is a uniform (`Lighting.setAmbient`).

**The dungeon's light** (#304). The 3D dungeon is lit per pixel by the game's own lights. The pure parts are in `render3d/lights.ts`, the shader's in `render3d/lightNodes.ts` (`makeLighting(heightTex, map, atlas)`), the per-frame work in `RunView3d.frame`.
- **Parameters.** `light` (`game|work`), `dark` (`fade|shape`: how a figure in the dark looks), `amb` (0..1, a fraction of `AMBIENT`; default 0, the panel's Ambient slider; first guess until the play-test), `lcap` and `ccap` (0..16, the light and caster caps, default 16 = `LIGHT_CAP` / `CASTER_CAP`; panel sliders Lights and Casters), `dv` (0, 50 or 100: a darkvision override for the dev scenes, a dev-only slider; a run uses the session's own darkvision).
- **The light list** (`lights.ts`). `staticCands` takes the session's `LightField` (torches at their flame, in front of their wall by `TORCH_STAND`, `TorchPairs` pairing a light with its torch entity; `lightFlicker` the torch's three sines scaled by the kind's flicker), `movingCands` the snapshot's moving lights (a carried light sits at its drawn figure when one lies within `CARRY_PX`, else at its px with a height of `MOVING_Y`; a Blast's flash at `FLASH_Y`; a Daylight light is the bounce fill at `BOUNCE_Y` with `BOUNCE_GAIN` of its brightness, dimmed by its crack's cloud; the sunlight itself is the crack term of `lightAt`, §9 "The air in 3D"). Lava lights are not in the list: the pool glows by its own texture. Ids are stable: `s<lightId>` for static lights, `m<entityId>` for moving ones (`.1`, `.2` for more lights on one carrier) and `p<kind>:<x>:<y>` for uncarried ones; `carrierMap` derives the carrier entity from those ids. `Cand.base` is the brightness without flicker: `selectLights(cands, footprint, focus, cap)` ranks by it (and by distance to the followed figure), so the capped list does not flutter. Only lights whose reach (radius plus `LIGHT_MARGIN`) touches the screen's light view count; `inView` is how many do. The light view (`lightView(fp, cam)`, #326) is the ground footprint grown toward the camera by `WALL_H / tan(pitch)` (`raised`, shared with the sun map's `sunView`): a wall top on the screen's bottom edge stands that far below the edge on the ground, so a torch there stays in the list and the wall tops do not pop as the camera moves. Every caller of `selectLights` passes the light view; cracks, haze and the effects keep the plain footprint. `LIGHT_CAP` (16) slots go to the shader (`packLights`: position and radius, colour times `LIGHT_GAIN`, and in the shadow channel the light's flag with its skip slot as skip + 2, 1 none, 0 shadows off).
- **Light and wall shadows** in `lightNodes.ts`: the demo's `lightAt` and `wallVis` (grid walk over the height texture). A light's radius is the game's falloff radius; the falloff floors at `EDGE` (0.1) at the radius and the shader keeps that floor out to radius + `LIGHT_MARGIN`, where the light is cut to 0.
- **Figure shadows** (`ShadowCaster`: box in the world, y range, atlas rect; `selectCasters(casters, lights, ccap)` picks those near a light, the first `CASTER_CAP` go to the shader). `cardVis` tests the segment from a surface to a light against each caster: the card is turned to face the light, its atlas alpha is the occluder. A surface skips its own slot (`self`) and the light's carrier (`assignSkips`), so a torch-bearer's or a fireball's own sprite does not shadow it. A caster whose width is 0 or less ends the list in the shader, so `casterOf` returns null for zero width. Cards drawn in the ghost pass (alpha below 1) cast no shadow; a rotated card's shadow is the unrotated sprite over its bounding rect (an approximation). A 3D model's caster uses the same path with its own baked outline frame (§9 "Figure models (#338)", "Shadows"). Every TSL loop in `lightNodes.ts` names its own index (`li` the lights, `ws` the wall walk, `ci` the casters): three inlines `wallVis` and `cardVis` into the light loop and would name every index `i`, so an expression of the light's index pasted into an inner loop read the inner index instead (the shadows came from the wrong light, #304). `lightNodes.test.ts` builds the GLSL and WGSL without a GPU and checks that no loop reuses an enclosing loop's index name.
- **Lava.** `lavaTexels` writes one rgba texel per tile (the light the tile's lava lights give, alpha 1 on a lava tile); `Lighting.setLava(data, version)` converts and uploads only when the array object or `FrameInput.lavaVersion` changes (a caller that refills one buffer must bump the version). The self-light applies only to upward surfaces (the pool), with the cell clamped to the map.
- **Darkvision.** `Lighting.setDarkvision({ x, z, reach })` with the followed figure's centre and reach (`DV_EYE`, `DV_MARGIN`, `DV_FADE` of `render3d/light.ts`); `finish(albedo, lit)`: grey where only darkvision shows, colour back as real light grows, then the shoulder. The disc is blocked by `wallVis` from the point to the eye, and it leaves out the top of a block that rises above the eye (`dvOnTop` in `lightAt`: an upward face higher than `DV_EYE`, within `DV_TOP_SLACK` of its cell's height): `wallVis` skips the cell a point lies in, so alone it let the eye see wall tops from below, which showed as grey triangles on the rock beyond sight and made the see-through stipple visible on it (#328). `DV_DARK` lives in `game/light.ts`.
- **Figures in the dark.** `Card.dark` reaches the shader as `shade.y`: such a figure is lit by the ambient alone (`darkOnly`). By default (`dark=shape`, the play-test decision) it stays opaque, ambient-lit only: a silhouette; with `dark=fade` it goes to the ghost pass with its alpha times `darkAlpha(ambient)`. It has no ground shadow and is no caster. Torch flames: the glow quad is drawn only in `light=work` (and the woodland), because the game's light is the glow. Known: the west and east wall torches' flame cards are edge-on at yaw 0, so they are invisible now that the glow is gone.
- **Readout.** F3 on `?r3d=lights` and in a run shows `lights <list> of <inView> (cap <lcap>)` and `shadows <casters> cards (cap <ccap>)` (`stats.ts`); `window.__r3d.lights` and `.casters` hold the numbers.
- **`?r3d=lights`** (`lightsStage.ts`): the lights demo, no server and no login. It plays the script in `game/lightsDemoScript.ts` (the board, seven torches, a blue one that switches every 3 s, a lava pool, a fireball, a frost bolt, an explosion flash) through the run's own light-list functions. A knight walks a loop past the pillars; V cycles the viewer's darkvision 0 / 50 / 100 through the params (`dv`). With darkvision the wall tops around the rooms stay black. The side-wall torches of room 1 must leave the void behind their walls black.

**The 3D run.** Every run starts here, for every account; the host's mode is `run` for a debug account whose URL has `?r3d` and `play` for everyone else ("Host modes" above). Debug runs start immortal and debug accounts play only with each other.
- `main.ts` preloads `render3d/boot` on `Welcome`. On `RunStarted` it hides the lobby, stops an old 3D run, buffers every server message from that moment, awaits the module, and calls `startRun3d(gameEl, search, { info, net, hud, myClass, early, dev })`. `startRun3d` creates the `RunSession` and replays the buffered messages into it at once (`session.receive`), so nothing that arrives while three.js loads is lost (a sealed entrance, the debug message, `RunEnded`). A second `RunStarted` while the import is pending drops the stale one (a counter). A failed import returns to the lobby with an error. `backToLobby` stops the 3D run.
- Until the host's first view exists, a 16 ms interval steps the session, so prediction and the HUD run from the start; the host's loop takes over at its first frame. If the host cannot start (no WebGPU or WebGL2), its error box stays and the session and HUD keep running; the player leaves through the Esc menu.
- `runStage.ts`: the stage's `world` is the session's live `TileMap` (`painted` and `clear` by `info.woodland`), so a rebuild after a lost device draws the current map, a sealed entrance included. Keys: W A S D and the arrows move (`moveInput`: the screen direction turned by the yaw, rounded to the eight wire directions by `dir8`); Esc the menu; Q and E watch teammates after death; E uses; F toggles the free camera (`run` mode only; `Host.dev`); Z, X and C turn the camera; L the rules log; F3 the HUD's panel (`session.toggleStats`). Aiming, the attack buttons and turning are in "Overlays, input and turning in the 3D run" below. The debug keys (#315) call the session's methods: F4 `debugDarkvision`, F7 `debugIncorporeal` (Shift `debugAntiMagic`), F8 `debugEffect` (Shift `debugDispel`), F9 `debugImmortal`; a held key acts once, and the browser never gets them (F7 is caret browsing). F4 changes the server's darkvision (so the dark mark and the hidden bars follow it, and the 3D picture draws it, #304). `StepInput.held` is true while any movement key is down; under the free camera the hero gets no movement.
- `runView3d.ts` (`RunView3d`, the run's `RunView`): the **follow camera** eases toward the session's camera target (own hero, or the watched teammate) with `1 - exp(-dt * 6)`, clamped by `followClamp` so the screen's footprint on the ground stays inside the map at any turn (margins `|cos yaw| * halfW + |sin yaw| * hd` and `|sin yaw| * halfW + |cos yaw| * hd` with `hd = halfH / sin(pitch)`; centred on an axis the view exceeds; at turn 0 the margins are `halfW` and `hd`). The **free camera** pans with the movement keys in screen terms, 12 tiles per second (36 with Shift), kept inside the map (`freeClamp`); switching back eases from where it was. **Figures and shots** are `Figures3d` (`figures.ts`, #303): per entity one `PoseMemory`, `FigureStates.stateOf` for the drawn state and `figurePose` (`game/anim/pose.ts`) for the look, turned into cards (`cards.ts`): a body card, a weapon card (`WEAPON_SCALE`, pushed `±WEAPON_BIAS` toward or away from the camera when the weapon is in front of or behind the body), a floor shadow, a lying body in the floor decals when asleep, translucent figures (`pose.alpha < 1`, dash afterimages, the fading image of a figure that went invisible) in the sorted ghost pass, a skull decal after a death. Events are stamped with the clock of the last frame (`f.now`); before the first frame there is none, so `Figures3d.event` passes `null` and `FigureStates` starts the fall or drowning on the first frame that draws the figure, and `Marks.prune` stamps an Invisible mark on the first frame (#319). The pose's screen aim is `screenAim(yaw, aim) = aim - yaw` (the camera's right vector is `(cos yaw, sin yaw)`), so a figure faces the side it looks to on screen, with a small dead zone (`facesLeft`). `pose.sink` crops the body and lowers it, `pose.drop` lowers it; the aim step is in world tiles, `body` px in the card's plane (the body's constant 1 px below the shadow becomes a move of the foot 1/16 tile toward the camera on the ground: a card lowered into the floor would lose its foot row; `aimStep`'s y is halved for the melee step only, so it is used as it is). Ground shadows are ellipses along screen right and ground-down (`pushShadow` takes the camera); skulls lie flat and upright on screen (`pushFlat`), 2/16 tile up the screen from the death spot; the floor decal pass is translucent (a sleeping incorporeal figure keeps its alpha). Shots: frame shots are cards 0.5 tiles up turned by the screen aim, glow shots are glows of `scale * 7 / 16` tiles; server shots in `hiddenShots` are skipped, the own `f.shots` drawn. `atlasBounds.ts` reads the opaque bounds and the size of a frame from the atlas image (the lying layout needs them). `framePoint(id, points)` is a frame pixel (the dragon's mouth) as a world point. `window.__r3d.figures` is the number of figures drawn. **Objects** come from `game/objectPose.ts`: torches are wall cards (`pushWall`) with a flickering glow only in the work light (in the game light the torch is a point light, #304), chests and mimics cards with an ellipse shadow (13 x 5 px, strength 0.4), barrels cards with `barrelPose`, a mimic's wake, hop and bite from `mimicPose` (`MimicPose.flip` turns it); the coins of a chest or a fallen enemy fly as cards. Ground hazards (fire, holy ground, roots) are drawn by the effects (§9 "Effects in 3D (#308)"); props and plants are not drawn by the figures. `pointerWorld()` is the pointer on the ground at shot height (below). `shotEnded(shot, how)` hands a wall hit to the effects (`Effects3d.shotEnded`: a spark burst in the shot's colour) besides ending the figures' predicted shot.
- **Passes** (`view.ts`, by `renderOrder`): opaque cards (body, weapon, chest, barrel, mimic) with depth, then shadows (1), floor decals (2: skulls, then lying bodies, translucent; no depth write and no sorting, so the buffer order is the draw order: `Figures3d.frame` pushes the skulls first (`drawSkulls`) and a figure sleeping on a skull's spot covers it, #318), ghosts (3: translucent figures and afterimages, sorted back to front by `sortBackToFront`), glows (4), and the wall cards (torches; `emissiveMaterial`). A burning torch is self-lit; one that is out is lit by the light model (`lightAt`, as a figure's card) and so is as dark as the wall it hangs on (#325): `pushWall`'s `byLight` flag travels as the fourth foot value and reaches the shader as the third `sight` value. `materials.ts` has the card modes tint, fill and grey, a translucent variant and a flat one.
- **Decisions** (#303): the facing uses the screen aim; shadows follow the camera's yaw (depth 0.45 times the radius); skulls lie flat and upright on screen; the standing foot sits 1/16 tile toward the camera (a 1 px baseline); wading or lava does not stop a figure opening walls, falling or drowning does. The Shade's veil is left to #308.
- **The see-through** (`openers.ts`, `seeThrough.ts`, the mask pass in `view.ts`, `worldMaterial`): walls open softly around what stands behind them. `opensWalls` says which things open: heroes, Hostile and Friendly creatures (a hiding mimic too, a revealed one as an enemy), chests; Neutral creatures, barrels, torches, hazards, shots, the dead and the sinking do not. `seenOpeners` keeps only those on tiles the hero's sight shows (`runView3d.ts` builds a sight `View`) and drops every id the snapshot marks as in the dark (`RunFrame.dark`, #309): a cut-out in a wall with nothing visible in it would give the creature away. Each opener draws a camera-facing disc (`discQuads`) of `radius` tiles into a quarter-size HalfFloat mask, MAX-blended: red is the strength (`discStrength`: 1 inside `(1 - edge) * radius`, a smoothstep to 0 at the radius), green is `SEE_DEPTH_BIAS (48) - near` with `near` the depth of the foot measured horizontally along the ground (`nearOf`, clamped to +-`SEE_NEAR_MAX` 40, so the height of a wall behind never counts), alpha the coverage. The wall shader (`wallOpens`) opens a pixel where the strength is above 0 and the wall is more than `SEE_DEPTH_MARGIN` (0.05) tiles nearer than the farthest thing under the disc. Modes: `soft` (a Bayer 4x4 dither on `screenCoordinate`, at scene-pixel size so coarser at Fast quality: a pixel opens where its threshold is at least `keepAt = 1 - strength * (1 - keep)`, so the alpha is binary, never partial), and `off` (opens nothing; a dev switch, the player has no setting for it). Parameters in `SEE_DEFAULTS`: radius 0.8 tiles, keep 0.15, edge 0.4. The remaining checks of the see-through (the dither at quality Fast, overlapping openings, whether neutral creatures open walls and whether a closed mimic opens them like a chest) wait for #347, which decides the camera-side walls as a whole.
- **`?r3d=gallery`** (`galleryStage.ts`, `galleryMap.ts`): every figure kind cycling through its states, the status-effect looks, the board objects and shots, and a corridor behind a full-height wall where things walk, on the real path (a synthetic `RunFrame` through `Figures3d`, its openers through `seenOpeners`, into `FrameInput.see`). No server, no login.
- `window.__r3d` in a run also has `hero` and `cam` (tiles), `chunks` (`total`, `drawn`), `yaw` (whole degrees, -180 to 180, the camera as drawn) and `overlay` (the items the overlay painted last frame), for `r3d-run.mjs`. That script also presses Z and X, clicks, and decodes the Input arrays it sees on the WebSocket (a small MessagePack reader in the script) to check a primary went out.

### Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)

Water, lava and chasms are holes in the ground in the 3D view: the bank goes down under the floor, figures stand on the bed, and a figure pushed into a chasm falls out of sight. Drawing only: gameplay stays on the plane `y = 0` and the server and the protocol are untouched (`FALL_TIME` 0.7 s, `DROWN_TIME` 0.9 s, speeds, damage and `Ev::Sink` are as before). The design and the plan are on issue #324; the water's own surface (#345) and the lava's (#344) come after it. Code: `render3d/dips/` and the files named below.

**The shape** (`dips/shape.ts`, pure). `bedAt(map, x, z)` is the height of the ground at a point in tiles: 0 on plain floor, a little above it on a rim, negative in a dip. It is a function of the point alone (no per-cell data), so the mesh, the figures and the effects read the same ground.
- **Membership mask.** `maskAt(map, x, z, member)` is the bilinear interpolation of "this cell is of the kind" between cell centres with smoothstep weights, for water (`isWater`: shallow and deep), deep water, lava and chasm. Its `OUTLINE_EDGE` (0.5) contour lies on a straight cell edge and rounds the corners: a convex corner of a pool is cut inward, a concave one reaches a little into the floor cell. The rules still use the cell squares; the rounding is a look. Outside the map is no member.
- **Depth.** With `ss` the smoothstep: `bed = -(SHALLOW_BED * ss(0.5, STEEP.water, water) + (DEEP_BED - SHALLOW_BED) * ss(0.5, STEEP.deep, deep)) - LAVA_BED * ss(0.5, STEEP.lava, lava) - CHASM_BED * ss(0.5, STEEP.chasm, chasm)`. Then the roughness (value noise `vnoise` on an integer-lattice hash, plus or minus `ROUGH`, from mask 0.5 to 0.7 and not on a chasm's wall) and the lip, a continuous bump on the outside of the outline, `LIP * ss(0.25, 0.4, any) * (1 - ss(0.42, 0.5, any)) * (0.4 + 0.6 * vnoise)`, where `any` is the largest of the water, lava and chasm masks. The bump is 0 at the cell edge (mask 0.5) and beyond mask 0.25, so a dip meets the floor without a step.
- **Numbers** (first guesses for the user's look, all in `shape.ts`):

  | | surface level | bed below the floor | `STEEP` (mask where the bed is full) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | shallow water | `WATER_LEVEL` -0.08 | `SHALLOW_BED` 0.55 | 0.85 |
  | deep water (added by the deep cells' own mask) | same | `DEEP_BED` 2.3 | 0.95 |
  | lava | `LAVA_LEVEL` -0.15 | `LAVA_BED` 1.7 | 0.8 |
  | chasm | none | `CHASM_BED` 12 | 0.56 (nearly a wall) |

  Also `ROUGH` 0.06, `LIP` 0.05.
- **The other readers.** `walkBedAt` is `bedAt` without the chasm's term: where a grounded foot stands (below). `kindOf(water, lava, chasm)` is the kind whose mask is largest, none below 0.15 (the bank material's choice); callers pass the three `maskAt` values of a point. `surfaceAt` is the liquid's level where the water or lava mask is above `OUTLINE_EDGE`, else `-Infinity`. `inDipMesh` lists the cells the dip mesh draws. `depthFade(y)` is `0.03 + 0.97 * exp(0.55 * min(y, 0))`. `dipOf(tile)` is the kind of a tile id (`DIP`: none, water, lava, chasm).
- **The bed as a texture** (`BED_RES` 4 texels per tile). The water's surface reads the bed's height and its own outline from a texture of `bedAt` and the water mask (`water/waterField.ts`; "Water in 3D (#345)" below). The wall-height bytes stay as they are (`wallVis` reads them).

**The dip mesh** (`dips/dipMesh.ts`, pure). `buildDipChunk(map, painted, cx, cz, frames)` returns one indexed mesh for a chunk's dip cells (`inDipMesh`: the terrain cells and the floor cells with a terrain cell among their eight neighbours, because the rounded outline and the lip reach into those), or null. Each cell is `DIP_RES` (12) quads on a side with its vertices on `bedAt`, the normal from central differences of `bedAt` on a per-cell grid of `(DIP_RES + 3)` squared values (one point of border, one grid step `1 / DIP_RES` either way, so `bedAt` runs once per grid point; a shared edge gets the same values from either cell because `bedAt` is a pure function of the point), and the attributes of the world material (`kind`, `rect`, uv) from `flatSurface` (`worldMesh.ts`: the cell's floor frame by `floorFrame`, or the painted ground on a woodland map), so where the bed is at floor height the pack's floor texel shows unchanged. Each vertex also carries `dip`, the `kindOf` of the three masks at its point, and `marks` (below).
- **Who draws the floor next to a dip.** The world mesh skips every cell `inDipMesh` says (`buildChunk` calls it before the floor quad), the dip mesh draws exactly those; a floor cell is drawn once, also after a tile change (`View.tileChanged` rebuilds the chunks of the cell and its eight neighbours; the plate, dip and water surface meshes of them, and the water's field texture, only when `dipClassChanged(old, new)` says the dip kind, deep or shallow water, the height or the painted-ground kind of the cell changed, `old` from the view's copy of the tiles as built. A door opening or closing rebuilds the world chunks alone: the boss hall's door must not cost a dip rebuild when the boss wakes).
- **Skirts.** Where a dip cell's side borders a wall or void cell (`cellHeight` above 0) and the bed along that side is below 0 anywhere, a vertical strip runs from `y = 0` down to the bed along the edge, facing into the dip, with the bank material. It closes the slit under the wall face at a concave terrain corner, where the bed goes below the foot of the wall's face at `y = 0`. The shape function is unchanged: pools and chasm strips meet walls straight.
- **Chunk bounds** are the real extent of the arrays (`min`, `max`; they reach down to the chasm's bed), not the 0 to `WALL_H` box of the world chunks. A plate chunk's box is `LAVA_LEVEL` to 0. `chunks.total` and `chunks.drawn` count the dip meshes too.

**The dip material** (`dips/dipNodes.ts`, `dipMaterial`, TSL). `mix(floor texel, rock, bank)` with `bank = smoothstep(ROCK_START, ROCK_FULL, depth below the floor)` (0.02 to 0.12). The floor texel is `flatTexel` of `materials.ts` (the code the world material shares: the atlas frame or the painted ground, nearest or the smoothed linear read; each material has its own `smooth` uniform, which the view sets to the world material's value, and `setGround`). The rock is picked by the vertex's `dip` and reads the world position only, in a plane that slides with depth (`pp`), so it looks the same on the sloped ground and on the skirts. All noise is evaluated at the top level of the shader and the kinds only choose between the results.
- **The floor along a rim** is marked by what lies beside it, so the tiles do not end on a clean curve (the user, 2026-11-16). The dip mesh carries one more vertex attribute for it, the vec3 `marks`: x is the chasm's outline mask (`maskAt`: 0.5 on the outline, 1 inside), y is near lava and z is near a chasm (`nearAt`: 1 minus the distance in tiles to the nearest cell of that kind, so 1 on such a cell and 0 one tile out, which is where the dip mesh ends and the world mesh's plain floor begins). `dipMaterial` changes the floor texel before the bank mix. By a **chasm**: the floor is broken into pieces (`shards`, a Voronoi cell per piece with straight edges, `SHARD_FREQ` 1.35 per tile); cracks run along the pieces' edges up to `CRACK_REACH` 0.9 tile out, each piece its own distance (`CRACK_RAGGED`), widening toward the rim (`CRACK_WIDTH`), and the pieces on the outline have broken off and show dark rock (`CHIP`), which makes the break line jagged. By **lava**: the floor is scorched to soot (`SCORCH_REACH`, `SCORCH_DARK`, `SOOT`), and the tile joints and the pieces' cracks glow (`EMBER`, `EMBER_REACH` 0.5 tile; an emission added after the light, flickering with the band's noise). By **water** the rim is the game's own moss, not a shader mark (the user, 2026-11-16): the ground cover (#330) grows moss along every pool (`cover/moss.ts`, "waterside" below), and `dipMaterial` takes the cover's shared uniforms and draws the floor under it the way `worldMaterial` does (darkened under standing cover, the cover's colour flat while the layer is off). A low-frequency noise makes the lava's and the chasm's reach uneven; the cracks' reach is also multiplied by `smoothstep(0, CRACK_BORDER_FADE, marks.z)` (0.1), so they fade to nothing at the mesh's border however the reach is tuned. The two costly patterns sit behind real branches in the shader (`Fn` with `If`; plain arithmetic, so a branch is allowed): the chasm's slabs only below the floor inside a chasm's outline (one evaluation on a straight wall, two on a rounded corner), the floor's pieces only on floor that lava or a chasm marks. The thresholds of the gates and the other rim numbers are named constants at the top of the rim section of `dipNodes.ts` (`ROCK_START`, `SLAB_MASK_MIN`, `PIECE_MARK_MIN`, ...); `ROCK_START` is both the slab gate's depth and the start of `bank`, so no slabs are computed where no rock shows; the slab gate's mask threshold (`SLAB_MASK_MIN`) lies below `OUTLINE_EDGE`, so the gate is open wherever the bank has started; the piece gate ends where `bank` is nearly full (`PIECE_BANK_MAX`). The rock and the cover's floor texel are picked by float weights (`weight` in `materials.ts`: exactly 0 or 1), not by `select`, so every atlas or ground sample and its `fwidth` sit at the top level of the shader, outside any branch, and the slabs are emitted once; `readsInBranches` in `shaderCode.testutil.ts` checks the generated code for a sample or derivative in a branch (loop bodies are out of scope). All numbers are first guesses for the user to tune; the lava and water rims are judged again with the new surfaces of #344 and #345.
- **Three banks.** Water: dark mud with stones (`vnoise2` thresholded, not grid-aligned) going green-black toward the deep. Lava: very dark basalt, with a glowing band that follows the lava's level (`exp(-7 |y - LAVA_LEVEL|)` times a drifting `fbm2`, uniform `time`). Chasm: overlapping jagged slabs of layered dark rock (the user's reference: fractured slate, a rugged charcoal face with jagged slabs, diagonal ridges and deep crevices). `slabs(s, y)` shades the face in the wall's own frame, `s` along the wall and `y` up it. The face is a stack of layers tilted about 25 degrees (`LEDGE_COS`, `LEDGE_SIN`), 2.3 per world unit (`LEDGE_ACROSS`: a chasm shows only its top two or three tiles), bent by a low-frequency warp (`LEDGE_WARP`). Every layer has its own angular lower edge (`ledgeEdge`: two swings of `lin`, a 1D noise joined by straight lines so the edge has corners, plus stepped notches; `EDGE_*`); the ranges sum to under one layer, so layers never cross and a layer's thickness runs from a sliver to nearly two layers. The layer over a point is the highest one whose edge lies below it, picked from four candidates with `step` and `mix` (unrolled, no shader loop). A layer is broken along its length into slabs (`SLAB_FREQ`, slanted breaks, most hairline and a few wide, `BREAK_*`); a slab is a flat plate with its own tone (`SLAB_TONE_LOW` to 1) and its own turn to or from the light (`SLAB_FACET`), a lit lip at its top (`LIP`) and a dark undercut at its foot, narrow or wide per slab (`UNDERCUT*`), ending in a black line. Mottling, a broad variation and fine grain lie over it (`ROCK_*`). About 30 `hash21` and 4 `vnoise2` calls per evaluation; no derivative op, no sine. A wall along x takes `s = x`, one along z takes `s = z` (signed so the pattern runs to the right seen from inside the chasm), and a rounded corner blends the two evaluations by the mesh normal, so the pattern does not mirror there. The `ridges` value shapes both the lit albedo (`CHASM_ROCK` (0.07, 0.069, 0.068)) and the cool self-light that works with no torch near (`CHASM_SELF` (0.155, 0.162, 0.178)); all constants sit at the top of `dipNodes.ts`. The fade `depthFade` takes it to black by about 6 to 8 tiles. The pattern was designed as a flat image first (a throwaway script, not in the repo), because the engine shows too little of a wall to judge it. Every colour number is a first guess from the mockup.
- **Light and sight.** The colour goes through `lighting.lightAt` (wrap 0.35, like a floor) and `finish`, so torches and the lava's lights reach into the dips and wall shadows work (a dip is not a wall: `wallVis` reads the height bytes, which a dip does not touch), then `sight.apply` at the surface point. `lightAt` has no glow block of its own for lava cells (it had one until #344): the lava surface is self-lit and the dip ground draws the basalt's band itself, at the lava's level. The material is opaque and listed in `sightMaterials.test.ts`.
- **Noise** (`noiseNodes.ts`): `hash21` (Dave Hoskins' hash without sine), `vnoise2` and `fbm2` (four octaves). No new shader uses a sine hash.
- **Sunlight in a dip** (`air/crackNodes.ts`, `air/cracks.ts`). The crack's patch is lit below the floor too: the term accepts `y` down to `-(CHASM_BED + 1)`, follows the beam's direction down (`k = y / B.y` keeps the sign), and keeps its floor-level size and ray pattern (`yh = max(y, 0)`), so a shaft falls into a pool; the CPU twin `crackMask` has the same rule.

**Where a foot stands** (`figures.ts`, `Figures3d.foot`). The pose's wading crop, drop, fall shrink and fade are not used in 3D (`figOf` zeroes `sink`, `fall` and `drown` of the state; `pose.ts` still computes them). A figure's drawn foot has a world height `y`:
- A grounded figure or barrel stands on `walkBedAt`: the bed of a dip (the shallow bed, the lip), but never down a chasm's wall. A rounded chasm outline reaches into floor cells at inner corners, where the server keeps figures legally; they stand on the floor there, and only a `Sink` (or a barrel's anim 2) makes a figure fall.
- In lava the figure floats: `standY(bed, sink)` is `max(bed, LAVA_LEVEL - sink)` with `sink` easing toward `lavaSinkOf(height)` (`LAVA_SHARE` 0.6 of the body's height in tiles, from the figure's own anchor height, no camera stretch; `figFoot` places the foot after the pose) at `LAVA_IN` 0.55 per second on a lava tile and back to 0 at `LAVA_OUT` 2.5 per second off it (`stepLavaSink`, `1 - exp(-rate * dt)`), so it sinks slowly and rises fast. 0.6 of a hero's 1.75 tiles is the 1.05 tiles #324 judged fine; a big figure sinks to the same share.
- A figure that skims the terrain (`skimsTerrain`: dashing or incorporeal) or flies (`FLAG.AIRBORNE`) is at 0.
- `Foot { x, z, y, d, started, gone }` is what `foot()` returns; `Motion` keeps each thing's last drawn spot, its eased speed (rate 20 per second) and last height.
- **A world height as a lift.** The camera passes stretch a card or model by `1 / cos(pitch)`, lift included, so a world height is handed on as `lifted(y) = y * cos(pitch)` and the foot lands at the true `y` (as the overlays project it). Art-pixel lifts (a hop) are not passed through `lifted`. Wading depth on a card therefore shows `depth * cos(pitch)` of the sprite under the surface (the same stretch every card has against a wall).
- `DrawnFig.foot` is the foot height; effects that used `top` as a world height add it (status flames and light, boom flashes, the gaze, the channel swirl: `fx/status.ts`, `fx/booms.ts`, `fx/figureFx.ts`). `FigureAnchor.y` is the foot height for the overlay (`overlayLayout.ts`), so a bar and a name hang from the sunk figure; `FigureAnchor.descending` (a fall, a drowning or a pending `Sink`) makes `layoutBars` draw no bar or name for it. A coin rests on the bed where it was born (`Coin.bed`); chests, mimics, barrels and the ground shadows of objects use the bed under them. The skull and the lying body lift by `groundLift`, the cover's top plus the bed.
- **Ground shadows** (`ovalOk`) test against the real `bedAt` (the drawn ground, a chasm's wall included): the oval is drawn only when the foot stands on it (`y - bedAt <= 0.05`, so none for a dasher over a chasm or a figure on the walk bed above a rendered wall) and not under a liquid (`y >= surfaceAt`). A figure in a descent has none and casts no shadow, afterimage or opener; a drowned or falling figure stops opening walls as before.

**Descents** (`dips/descent.ts`, `dips/descents.ts`; the server sends `Ev::Sink` and `Died`, the view draws the rest). All numbers are first guesses.

| constant | value | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `FALL_G` | 14 tiles/s squared | gravity of a fall |
| `SLIDE_MAX` | 3 tiles/s | the cap of the slide a figure carries over the edge |
| `RIM_PULL` | 2 tiles/s | the pull to the sink cell's centre while the bed still carries it |
| `FALL_GONE` | 11.5 (`CHASM_BED - 0.5`) | the depth where a faller is out of sight |
| `FALL_RADIUS` | 0.3 tiles | the faller's body radius: its sideways step stops where the bed that far ahead is above the foot |
| `DROWN_SINK` | 0.4 s | the drawn drop of a drowning figure to the bed (a look; `DROWN_TIME` stays the server's rule) |
| `FALL_MAX_AGE` | 3 s | a faller older than this is dropped |
| `STEP_MAX` | 0.05 s | the longest step of a fall (a longer frame is stepped in pieces) |
| `FALLEN_CAP` | 32 | detached fallers kept (the oldest go first) |

- **A fall** (`startFall`, `stepFall`) starts only from a `Sink` that `FigureStates` holds (`sinking(id)` and not `drowning`: `how` 0; `how` 1 is a drowning) or a barrel's anim 2, never from the bed alone. It starts at the foot's last height with the figure's drawn speed (the server stops the creature and sends no speed), capped at `SLIDE_MAX`. The sink cell (`sinkCell`): where the drawn spot is not on a chasm cell (a pushed barrel's drawn position lags its anim, so its fall often starts on the floor before the rim), the chasm cell of the 3x3 around the spot that is nearest, with the one the drawn velocity points into preferred (`distance - 0.6 * cos(angle to the velocity)`), gives the centre the faller is pulled to; with no chasm cell near, or a start on a chasm cell (a figure's `Sink` spot), the centre of the spot's own cell as before. While the bed under it carries it (`bed >= y`, the rounded rim) it is pulled to the centre of the cell it sank in and follows the bed; when the bed drops away gravity takes over and it slides on. A sideways step is checked per axis (a slide along a wall goes on): it is dropped, and that axis' speed zeroed, when the bed at the new place or `FALL_RADIUS` ahead of it (in the direction of travel) is above the foot, so a hard push into a narrow chasm leaves the body (about 0.6 tiles wide) off the far wall instead of clipping into it (at any frame time, `advance` steps in pieces of `STEP_MAX`). A body that overlaps a wall sideways while it falls (a narrow chasm, a sink spot near a bank: the bed `FALL_RADIUS` to either side on an axis is above the foot) eases toward its cell's centre line on that axis at `RIM_PULL`, never into rock; the carried phase on the rim is as before; the final height is clamped to the bed at the final place. It is not stepped on the frame it starts. The faller is drawn on `bedAt` including the chasm's wall, in the depth's dark (`depthFade(y)` on each tint channel) and without ground shadow, afterimage, caster or opener; at `FALL_GONE` nothing is drawn any more (`gone`).
- **Detaching.** The server's `Died` arrives after `FALL_TIME`, while the figure is still falling, and the entity leaves the snapshot. `Descents.detach` keeps a falling one with the pieces it was last drawn from (`FallParts`: its cards and model instances as offsets from the faller, the pose copied with `copyPose` because the pose memory mutates it, and `dark`, whether the entity was dark when last drawn, because its id leaves the snapshot's `dark` set with it) and `drawFallen` draws it until it is out of sight, so the fall outlives the entity. A drowning one is dropped. `Sink` how 2 (the debug climb-out) ends the descent and any detached faller of the id (`Descents.end`). A second `Sink` for a falling id does not restart it; `Died` without a `Sink` draws no fall.
- **Drowning** (`drownY`) drops the foot from its height to the bed over `DROWN_SINK` (0.4 s, ease-in: `u` squared, so it accelerates) and it rests there, seen through the clear water (darkened by the depth of the water over the bed), until the entity is removed at `Died` as before. A barrel's anim 3 drowns the same way. The rings, the plunge and the bubbles above it are the water's ("Water in 3D (#345)").
- **Barrels.** The server sends a barrel no `Sink` and no `Died`: anim 2 is its fall and anim 3 its drowning, so `drawBarrel` starts the descent from the anim (the shake and flash of `barrelPose` use anim 0).
- **Things the hero cannot see.** A figure the sight hides or the dark marks falls without dust, bar or name: `FigureOut.falls` omits ids in `dark`, and the effects skip ids in `hidden`.
- **The dust.** `FigureOut.falls` lists the falls that began this frame at the rim they began on; `Effects3d` emits `FX_KIND.RimDust` (life 0.6 s, `FX_LIFE`), whose burst (`RIM` in `fx/figureFx.ts`) is a splash made large and light so it reads in the dim light: 12 motes of 4 art px (fixed size, the burst has no shrink), light stone colours `0xd9d0c4` and `0xb8a898`, up to 40 px/s, thrown 0.5 to 1.4 rad (up into the light above the rim), from 4 px above the rim spot. Like all dust (§9 "Lit or glowing") it is lit (`out.lit`), so in the dim light beside a black chasm it stays a dim, small puff; its albedo, size and toss are what make it read.

**The page `?r3d=liquids`** (`liquidsStage.ts`, `liquidsScript.ts`, `liquidsMap.ts`; `r3dPage` in `r3dFlag.ts`, `bootLiquids` in `boot.ts`). One room (18 by 12 tiles) with a water pool in the west (shallow rim, deep centre), a chasm two cells wide in the middle and a lava pool against the east wall; an elf wading up and down the shallow U round the deep, a knight walking into the lava, standing 5 s and out, and three things to push in: an imp into the chasm (key 1), an imp into the deep water (2) and a barrel into the chasm (3), or the panel's buttons. The script (`liquidsScript`, pure) feeds the figures what a run feeds them: entity states and the events (`Sink`, `Died`) at the server's times; the pushed imp slides 0.35 s (`SLIDE_TIME`) to 0.1 tile inside the target cell so the `Sink` comes as it crosses the rim, and returns `BACK_TIME` 3.2 s after the push. The page uses the run's own light list (the wall torches and a light per lava tile), the sight off, the game light; WASD pans a free camera. Zoom is 2 unless the URL picks one. `&push=chasm|water|barrel` asks that push at t = 1 s, `&t=<s>` pins the clock (the first frame replays the 6 s before it in steps of 1/60 so a fall in progress shows; the figures then get no time), `&slow=<n>` slows it. `window.__r3d.liquids = { t, fallers }`. Shots: `node r3d-shot.mjs http://localhost:5173/ shots "&push=chasm&t=1.6" webgl "" liquids`.

**Decisions of #324.**
1. All four terrain kinds sink here. Water and lava got the base (dips, banks, figures on the bed) with #324 and a plate as their surface; water's own surface came with #345, lava's with #344. One terrain system in `view.ts`, no seam between a sunken chasm and a flat lava pool next to it.
2. `bedAt` is the one source for the mesh and the figures (the water's field texture, #345, is made from it).
3. A faller never moves sideways into rock; a figure whose sink spot lies on a rounded rim is pulled to its cell's centre first, then drops.
4. Ground cover (#330) is not applied to dips (the woodland has no terrain); the painted woodland ground is supported through the shared texel code (`flatSurface`, `flatTexel`), so a later terrain cell on a woodland map draws.
5. Every new material ends its colour with `sight.apply` and is listed in `sightMaterials.test.ts`; every TSL loop names its own index; no sine hash.

**Tests.** `dips/shape.test.ts` (flat away from terrain; full depth in a wide pool; 0 on a straight edge and half a tile outside it; never above the lip and finite; rounded corners; purity; `kindOf`, `surfaceAt`, `inDipMesh`, `depthFade`; `walkBedAt`), `dips/dipMesh.test.ts` (no chunk without terrain, the cells covered, vertices on `bedAt`, shared edges agree also across chunks, normals, the floor texel, the marks and the kind of every vertex, the bank kind, bounds, a partition test that every flat cell is drawn once over a random map and after `map.set`, skirts along walls and at a concave corner), `dips/dipNodes.test.ts` (builds in GLSL and WGSL for each backend, no sine hash, lighting built once, the chasm slabs emitted once, no texture read or derivative in a branch, loops apart, the sight step, the handles), `dips/descent.test.ts` (gravity, gone before the bottom, `FALL_MAX_AGE`, the slide cap, never into rock, the rim, any start in a 3 by 3 chasm, drowning, the lava sink, `standY`), `liquidsScript.test.ts` and `liquidsStage.test.ts` (the map, the script's actors and events once each, the stage's keys and pinned clock), and cases added to `figures.test.ts` (the foot, descents, detached fallers, barrels, hidden ids, the shadow rule), `materials.test.ts` (no texture read or derivative in a branch of the world material), `worldMesh.test.ts`, `overlayLayout.test.ts`, `fx/figureFx.test.ts`, `fx/status.test.ts`, `air/crackNodes.test.ts`, `air/cracks.test.ts`, `air/drips.test.ts`, `lightNodes.test.ts`, `sightMaterials.test.ts` and `r3dFlag.test.ts`.

**Known limits.** The card stretch against the waterline (above), a flat shadow oval on a slope (the oval is one flat ellipse at the foot), no self-shadow of a dip's rim (the sun's shadow map and `wallVis` do not see the lip). All numbers above are first guesses for the user's look on real hardware.

### Water in 3D (#345)

In the 3D view water is a clear, moving surface in its dip: the bed and the submerged part of a figure show through it, the lights glint on it, the room is mirrored in it, and wading, drips and drowning stir it. Drawing only: the server, the protocol and the rules (`DROWN_TIME` 0.9 s, speeds, `Ev::Sink`) are untouched. The design and the plan are on issue #345 (the shared design on #324). It builds on "Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)" above. Code: `render3d/water/` and the files named below. Every number is a first guess for the user's look, a named constant in the file named.

**The field** (`water/waterField.ts`, pure). `waterField(map)` holds two floats per texel, the bed's height (`bedAt`) and the water's outline mask (`maskAt(isWater)`), on a grid of `BED_RES` (4) texels per tile plus one each way, so texel (i, j) lies at tile (i / 4, j / 4). A texel none of whose touching cells is in the dip mesh is (0, 0) without evaluating (such a point is under a wall or far from terrain; the mask there is below the outline anyway). `refreshWaterField(f, map, x, z)` recomputes the texels a change of cell (x, z) can reach (from x - 1 to x + 2, the same in z); `sampleWaterField` is the CPU twin of the shader's filtered, clamp-to-edge read (on the Float32 values; the texture holds half floats, about 0.008 coarse at a chasm's bed). `view.ts` uploads it as an RG half-float texture, filtered linearly (as the lava light texture is), and `tileChanged` refreshes it and re-uploads it when the cell's dip class changed (`dipClassChanged`: the dip kind, deep or shallow water, or the height, so a wall turning into floor beside a pool counts and a door opening does not).

**The surface mesh** (`water/waterMesh.ts`, pure). `buildWaterChunk(map, cx, cz)` lays two triangles at `WATER_LEVEL` on every `waterCell` of a chunk: a cell of height 0 that is water or has a water tile among its eight neighbours (the rounded outline reaches into those; a closed door has height 0 and counts, a wall does not). The mesh does not decide where the water ends: the material discards the surface where the field's mask is below `OUTLINE_EDGE`, so it never lies over a neighbouring chasm's or lava pool's bulge, and inside the outline the bank rises through it. The chunks are a fourth list in `view.ts` (`waterChunks`), built and dropped with the dip chunks, on their own layer (`WATER_LAYER`), counted in `chunks`.

**The material** (`water/waterNodes.ts`, `waterMaterial`, TSL). With `p` the surface point's x and z, `t` the water's clock and `V` the unit vector toward the eye (constant in the orthographic view):
- **Waves** (`WAVES`, `WAVE_SHAPE`): the height is two slow sines (0.007 and 0.006 tiles, wavelengths of 2 to 3 tiles) plus one low-frequency noise (0.008, `vnoise2`, no sine hash), plus the ripples. Calm and large on purpose: busy high-frequency waves gave "squiggly worms" of light in the mockup. The normal comes from finite differences of the height (`eps` 0.015 tile) with the slope doubled (`gain`).
- **Ripples**: a uniform array of `RIPPLE_CAP` (16) sources (x, z, age, strength), a slot with age below 0 adds nothing. The shader's loop (`rp`) is the twin of `rippleHeight` (`water/ripples.ts`, `RIPPLE`): a ring that travels at `RIPPLE_SPEED` 0.75 tiles per second and lives `RIPPLE_LIFE` 3.5 s, `height * strength * exp(-decay * age) * smoothstep(0, rise, age) * exp(-((d - age * speed) * width)^2) * sin((d - age * speed) * waves)`.
- **Depth** is the bed's height from the field (one filtered sample at the top level of the shader), not the depth buffer: `depth = max(WATER_LEVEL - bed, 0)` and the way through the water is `depth / max(V.y, VIEW_MIN)`. A shader that sampled a depth texture lost the context on the NVIDIA start line in the mockup; the bed's height works everywhere.
- **Seeing through** (`ABSORB`, `SCATTER`, `REFRACT`): what lies behind the surface is read from the scene copy (below) at the screen position, offset by the normal's tilt times `REFRACT` (0.012 of the screen, scaled by the depth up to 1 tile). It is absorbed per tile of way by (0.55, 0.22, 0.16) for red, green and blue (red goes first), and the water adds a very dark colour of its own (`SCATTER`) lit by the light at the surface (`lightAt`, wrap 0.5, `shadow: 'walls'`). The deep middle reads darker because the way is longer, not because of a tint.
- **The mirror** (`MIRROR_BASE` 0.45, `FRESNEL_GAIN`, `MIRROR_MAX`, `MIRROR_WOBBLE`, `MIRROR_GAIN`): the mirror picture (below) is read at the surface point through the mirror camera's projection times view, nudged by the normal, and mixed in by `clamp(MIRROR_BASE + F * 6 * (1 - MIRROR_BASE), 0, 0.95)` with `F` the Fresnel term `0.02 + 0.98 * (1 - N.V)^5`. Without a mirror picture the share is 0 (`setMirror(null)`). v runs down in a picture on both backends (three turns a render target's lookup itself), so the uv is `(ndc.x / 2 + 0.5, 0.5 - ndc.y / 2)`.
- **Glints** (`lighting.glintAt`, `GLINT` in `lightNodes.ts`): a specular term from the light list (torches, glowing shots, the moving light) and from the cracks' sunlight: each light's colour times the falloff and the wall test of `lightAt`, times a sharp lobe (power 700, gain 5) plus a faint broad one (power 90, gain 0.1) of the half vector. Its loops are `gl` and `gc`. No card test, no ambient, no lava. Only the water has a specular term.
- Last comes `sight.apply` at the surface point, so nothing moves, glints or is mirrored where the hero cannot see. The material is opaque and writes depth; its alpha is `step(OUTLINE_EDGE, mask)` with an alpha test (three writes the discard). It carries four uniform arrays (the lights' two, the cracks, the ripples): `shadow: 'walls'` leaves the three caster arrays out, which keeps it within WebGPU's 12 uniform buffers per fragment stage.

**The picture's passes** (`view.ts`, `water/passes.ts`). `poolInView` tests a list of chunk boxes (the ground their quads cover; the water chunks' or the lava chunks') against the ground the screen shows, with a margin of 1 tile; `picturePasses({ water, lava, reflect, heat }, w, h)` says what the frame needs: `{ copy, mirror, shimmer }`, where `copy` is `water || (lava && heat)`, `mirror` only with water and reflections, and `shimmer` is `lava && heat`. **Without a pool in view nothing extra runs** and the picture is drawn as before.
- **The scene copy** (`splitPicture`). With a pool in view the picture is one scene drawn in two parts: the opaque scene (layer 0) into the picture's target, a copy of its colour into a second target of the same size (nearest), then the water (`WATER_LAYER`) and everything translucent (`AIR_SKIP_LAYER`: shadows, decals, ghosts, glows, effects, the air) into the first target with its depth kept. The water reads the copy. The picture therefore goes through a target while a pool is in view, on High too (a canvas-size target and one more blit; the present quad then covers the whole canvas). The shafts' composite and the present step follow as before. `readback` draws through the same routine when the last frame used the copy (and the picture's target still has that frame's size); otherwise it is one plain render as before.
- **The mirror pass** (only with reflections on): the scene above the water drawn once more from a camera mirrored in the plane `y = WATER_LEVEL` (`mirrorPose`; the same frustum, up (0, -1, 0)), into a target of half the picture's size (never 0 px), before the picture. For this pass only, `sight.clipBelow` is `WATER_LEVEL` (the sight step, which every material ends with, discards a fragment below it: one clip for all materials, no second shader variant; -1e6 at all other times), the walls' see-through mode is 0 (the openings read the picture's screen) and the chunk counters rest; all three are restored in a `finally`. The mirror camera sees layer 0 and `CEILING_LAYER`.
- **The ceiling** (`ceilingMaterial`, `CEILING`). The 3D view has no ceiling, and a mirror of empty space read as a slate sheen in the mockup. Only the mirror pass draws one: a quad over the whole map at `WALL_H`, facing down, dark rough rock (`fbm2` over x and z, thin dark cracks), lit by the game's lights (`lightAt` with the normal pointing down), so a torch leaves a warm patch on it. Not made on a painted map (the woodland has no water).
- **The targets** (the scene copy, the mirror picture) are made once at 1 by 1 and resized in place, so the water's texture nodes keep their objects; a lost device rebuilds the view and with it all of this.

**Reflections switch and quality.** The parameter `refl` (`params.ts`; URL `refl=0`; on by default) is the panel's switch "Reflections" on the demo pages and in runs. The frame's `FrameInput.water` is `{ ripples, now, reflect }` with `reflect = reflects(params)` (`params.ts`: `refl && quality !== 'fast'`): quality Fast leaves the mirror pass out whatever the switch says; the scene copy stays (one blit). The F3 readout books the mirror pass as its own system, `mirror` (`R3D_SYSTEMS` in `stats.ts`).

**What stirs the water** (`water/water.ts`, `Water3d`, pure; `WADE`). The run owns one (`RunView3d`, like its `Air3d`, so the ripples outlive a rebuilt view); `step({ dt, now, figs, sinks, landed, hidden })` returns the packed ripples and the cues for the droplets. It reads the map every frame, so water that appears or goes needs no call. The rules, in tiles:
- A figure **stands in water** when the tile under it is water, it does not dash and its foot is below `WATER_LEVEL` (a ghost or a flier at height 0 does not).
