- **Entering** (it stood out of the water in the last frame it was listed): a ripple of strength 1.6 and an `enters` cue. A figure listed for the first time gets none, and neither does a dash that ends in water (a dash over water counts as in it: the figure keeps its tile during a dash).
- **Wading** (faster than 0.3 tiles per second): a ripple of strength 1 every 0.3 s.
- **Dashing over water**: `spray` cues at 30 per second, no ripple.
- **A drowning** (`FigureOut.sinks`: a `Sink` how 1, or a barrel's anim 3, on the frame it begins; ids in the dark are left out, as in `falls`): three ripples of strength 1.6 within 0.15 tile and a `plunges` cue; then for `DROWN_TIME`, ripples of strength 0.6 within 0.2 tile at 8 per second and `bubbles` cues at 12 per second.
- **A drip that landed** (`AirParts.landed`, the air's report; in the run the air steps after the water, so it is last frame's): a ripple of strength 0.55. The drips' own drawn rings are gone (two ring systems on one surface would double up); the drop and its two splash pixels are as before.
- An id in `hidden` makes no ripple and no cue, but its state is tracked (so it makes no entering splash when it comes into sight standing in water). Ids no longer listed are forgotten. The rates use accumulators, so the counts do not depend on the frame time. `Ripples.add` reuses a free or expired slot (or one born in the future, after a clock went back) before it replaces the oldest live one: never more than 16.
- `DrawnFig.dashing` says a figure dashes (the own hero: from prediction); an incorporeal figure that only skims the terrain does not.

**The droplets** (`fx/waterFx.ts`, `registerWaterFx`; `FxFrame.water`). `emitWaterCues` is the one place that turns cues into records: `FX_KIND.WaterSplash` (24; a 0 an entering: 6 motes of `0x72d6ce`, up to 30 px/s, 0.35 s; a 1 a plunge: 14 motes of `0xcae6f5`, up to 45 px/s, 0.45 s) and `FX_KIND.WaterDrop` (25; a 0 a spray droplet, a 1 a bubble rising at 10 px/s; 0.35 s). They are lit, like all dust and smoke, and are born at `WATER_Y` (`WATER_LEVEL + 0.01`): `thrown` and `BurstSpec` of `fx/shapes.ts` take a `floor` height for it (default `MOTE_FLOOR` 0.03, the height a mote rests at above the ground), so a droplet falls back onto the water, not onto the floor above it. A cue of a hidden id makes no record.

**The pages.** `?r3d=liquids`: the elf's wading rings, the pushed imp's plunge, rings and bubbles, the mirror; `window.__r3d.liquids.ripples` is the live count; a pinned clock (`&t=`) holds the waves still and replays the water (rings and droplets) of the 6 s before it. `?r3d=mood`: the drips ring the pool (`window.__r3d.ripples`; no effects pass on that page, so no droplets). `?r3d=gallery`: rings under the wading group. The other dev pages have no water.

**Decisions of #345** (beyond the design).
1. The scene copy splits one picture; the scene is not drawn twice.
2. Depth for the absorption is the bed's height. Accepted: a submerged figure is tinted as if it were at the bed; the refraction offset can pull in a pixel of something that stands above the surface.
3. One texture for the bed and the outline; the surface is discarded outside the outline.
4. The mirror pass is clipped by the sight step (`clipBelow`), not by a second set of shaders.
5. The ceiling exists only in the mirror pass, on dungeon maps.
6. A drowned figure is removed at `Died` as before: it reaches the bed in `DROWN_SINK` 0.4 s and lies there, seen through the water, until `DROWN_TIME`.
7. The water's effects are lit droplets, the dash's spray included.

**Tests.** `water/waterField.test.ts` (the texels against `bedAt` and the mask, sampling, no mask over a chasm or lava beside the pool, a tile change equals a fresh build), `water/waterMesh.test.ts` (no chunk without water, the cells covered, facing up, walls and the map edge, a chunk border, a chasm or lava cell beside the pool), `water/ripples.test.ts` (flat outside its life, the speed, the fade, packing, expiry, the oldest replaced, a slot born in the future), `water/waterNodes.test.ts` (both materials build in GLSL and WGSL without a TSL error, no sine hash, no texture read or derivative in a branch, the loops apart, the discard, at most 12 uniform buffers, the mirror switch), `water/passes.test.ts` (nothing without a pool, half size, no mirror when asked, never 0 px, a pool at the screen edge, the mirror pose, the layers, and since #344 the lava's copy only with the shimmer), `water/water.test.ts` (every rule above, the hidden ids, the cap, water that appears under a figure, the four dash cases), `fx/waterFx.test.ts` (the records per cue, finite vertices at every age, born at `WATER_Y` and never below it), and cases added to `lightNodes.test.ts` (the glint builds, its loops, no caster), `sightNodes.test.ts` (the clip), `sightMaterials.test.ts`, `figures.test.ts` (`dashing`, `sinks`), `air/drips.test.ts` (`landed`, no ring), `fx/shapes.test.ts` (the floor of a thrown mote), `params.test.ts` and `panel.test.ts` (`refl`), `runView3d.test.ts`, `liquidsStage.test.ts` and `moodStage.test.ts` (the wiring). `view.ts` has no unit test (it needs a GPU): the shots of `r3d-shot.mjs` on both backends check it.

**Known limits.** A submerged figure is tinted by the bed's depth, not its own. The refraction offset can pull in a pixel from above the surface. Translucent things under the surface (a decal, a ground shadow, a glow on the bed) are hidden: the water is opaque and writes depth, and they are drawn after it and are not in the copy. The mirror shows no translucent thing either, and no figure that stands outside the picture (the cards and models are built for the picture's view); a card's depth push uses the picture's view ray in the mirror pass, so a reflection right beside a wall can be cut a little early. A wading figure shows `depth * cos(pitch)` of a card under the surface (the card stretch, as in #324). Every material holds the clip's `discard`, which may cost early depth rejection on some devices. On High the canvas-size target stays allocated after a pool leaves the view. A drip falls on any water in view, seen or not, so a ring born in unseen water can travel into the seen part of the same pool. The ripple loop runs over all 16 slots three times per pixel whatever the live count, and the water's and ceiling's shaders are compiled when a pool first comes into view (a possible hitch): both are cost left for later. A light shaft over a pool is composited against the bed's depth, not the surface's. The colour and light numbers come from a mockup tuned under other lights and tone mapping: they are first guesses for the user's look.

### Lava in 3D (#344)

In the 3D view lava is a molten surface in its dip: dark crust plates drift over glowing rock, cracks and openings glow from deep red to yellow-white, bubbles swell and pop with a burst of sparks, a wading figure sinks to its waist in an open ring, a hero in lava throws embers, and the hot air over a pool wobbles the picture behind it. Drawing only: the server, the protocol and the rules (lava burns, slows and sets burning; demons walk through) are untouched. The design and the plan are on issue #344 (the shared design on #324). It builds on "Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)" and takes the place of the lava plates that section's first version had. Code: `render3d/lava/` and the files named below. Every number is a first guess for the user's look, a named constant in the file named.

**The surface mesh** (`lava/lavaMesh.ts`, pure). `buildLavaChunk(map, cx, cz)` lays a subdivided grid of `LAVA_RES` (12) quads per tile side at `LAVA_LEVEL` over every `lavaCell` of a chunk (`liquidCell`, as for water: a cell of height 0 that is lava or has a lava tile among its eight neighbours). Each vertex carries two attributes besides its position: `shore`, the lava mask there (`maskAt(isLava)`) and the lava's depth over the bed there (`LAVA_LEVEL - bedAt`, at least 0). The finer grid is for the bubbles' domes, which the vertex stage raises. The mesh does not decide where the lava ends: the material discards the surface where the mask is below `OUTLINE_EDGE`, and the bank rises through it at the visible shoreline. The chunks (`lavaChunks` in `view.ts`) are built and dropped with the dip and water chunks, counted in `chunks`, on layer 0 (the opaque scene: lava reads nothing from the scene copy). Their box reaches `LAVA_DOME_ROOM` (0.1) above `LAVA_LEVEL`. The mesh has no transform: the material reads `positionLocal.xz` as the world's x and z.

**The material** (`lava/lavaNodes.ts`, `lavaMaterial`, TSL). Opaque, depth write on, alpha test 0.5 (alpha is `step(OUTLINE_EDGE, mask)`), self-lit: it does not go through `lightAt`, so crust, cracks and molten rock are the shader's own colours, and it carries no light arrays. With `p` the surface point's x and z and `t` the clock:
- **Crust plates** (`CRUST`): the noise coordinate `q = p * freq + warp * CRUST.warp + t * flow`, with the warp two `fbm2` reads (`CRUST.warpFreq` 0.7, `FLOW.warp`, `FLOW.warpSeed`). A 3 by 3 Voronoi over `q` (the loops `vj` and `vi`) gives the nearest and second nearest point, each cell's point drifting on a sine of the clock (`drift` 0.3, `driftRate` 0.12, `hash22`, no sine hash), so plates move slowly and show no squares. `edge` is the difference of the two distances; `crack = 1 - smoothstep(0, CRUST.crack 0.11, edge)`.
- **Openings** (`MOLTEN.open` [0.6, 0.74]): `smoothstep` of a slow `fbm2` at `MOLTEN.openFreq` 0.55 drifting by `FLOW.open`; where it is 1 the crust is gone. `fbm2` has mean about 0.47 and deviation about 0.11, so the upper edge must stay below 0.8 for an opening to open fully: about one per 3 by 3 pool.
- **Molten colour** (`LAVA_COLOUR`): `mix(mix(deep, hot, heat), white, smoothstep(whiteAt) * whiteShare)` with `heat` an `fbm2` at `MOLTEN.heatFreq` 2.2 drifting by `FLOW.heat`. **Crust colour**: `crust` times a grain and a per-plate tone (the nearest cell's hash), plus a dull red `rim` at a plate's edge (`CRUST.rim` 0.2 wide) scaled by the heat. `plate = (1 - crack) * (1 - open) * (1 - bank)` and the colour is `mix(molten, crust, plate)`.
- **The shore** (`SHORE`): over the first `width` 0.15 tile of lava depth there is no crust, and the molten rock is up to `gain` 1.3 times as bright (`bank = 1 - smoothstep(0, width, shore.y)`).
- **Rings** (loop `st`, `STIR`, `MOLTEN.ring` [0.75, 0.3]): a uniform array of `STIR_CAP` (8) sources (x, z, strength); the colour mixes toward `molten * STIR.gain` by `STIR.share * strength` inside 0.3 to 0.75 tile of a source.
- **Bubbles** (loops `bv` in the vertex stage and `bb` in the fragment stage, `BUBBLE_LOOK`): a uniform array of `BUBBLE_CAP` (6) slots (x, z, age, size), a free slot has age -1. Swelling (`age < BUBBLE.swell` 1.25 s): the vertex stage raises a dome (`bubbleRise`, `BUBBLE.rise` 0.22 of the size), which is the twin of `bubbles.ts`; the fragment draws it as molten rock times `gain` with a glint toward -x and -z. Popping (`BUBBLE.pop` 0.65 s): a ring of colour `ring` travels out to `BUBBLE.ring` 1.6 radii and a molten patch fades.
- **The glow** (`GLOW`, `glowShown`): the colours above are mixed as light (values above 1) and pressed into 0..1 at the end, before the sight step: exposure 1.15, the two ACES matrices and the fit of three.js's `ACESFilmicToneMapping`, then the sRGB encoding. This is a tone curve inside the lava material only; the view itself maps no tones. The approved mockup was tuned under that curve, and constants alone cannot copy it (the mixes happen before the curve), so every colour number keeps the mockup's value and means the same as there. Without it the plates are black and the cracks thin and pure red.
- Last comes `sight.apply` at the surface point, so nothing glows or bubbles where the hero cannot see.

**Bubbles** (`lava/bubbles.ts`, `lava/lava.ts`, pure). `Bubbles` is the buffer of live bubbles (`add`, `pack`, `prune`), at most `BUBBLE_CAP` 6; `bubbleRadius` and `bubbleRise` are the shader's twins; sizes are `BUBBLE.size` [0.2, 0.36] tiles (a tile is 32 px at zoom 2, so smaller domes were not seen). `Lava3d` finds pools (a pool is a 4-connected group of lava tiles; `mapChanged` finds them again and drops bubbles over a cell that is no longer lava, so lava that appears or goes in a run needs one call). Each pool in view (a tile within 1 tile of the ground the screen shows) and seen (a tile centre not `hiddenAt` the sight mask) counts its own timer down, `LAVA.every` 0.6 to 2 s, and starts one bubble when it runs out (a long frame starts one, not a burst); a pool out of view or unseen stands still. A spot is found in up to `LAVA.tries` 8 tries: a random point of the pool where the lava mask is at least `LAVA.mask` 0.95 **and** at the four points one dome's size away (x +- size, z +- size) too, so a dome never reaches a bank, and a pool without such a spot never bubbles. That rule is why a lava channel one tile wide, or a lone tile, never bubbles (the plan's plain rule would let a lone tile bubble, as its mask is 1 at the centre). A bubble that pops gives a `pops` cue at its spot unless the sight mask hides it.

**What stirs the lava** (`Lava3d.step({ dt, now, figs, hidden, fp, mask, focus })`, in tiles). The run owns one (`RunView3d`, so bubbles outlive a rebuilt view); it steps after the water and returns the packed bubbles and rings (the same arrays every call) and the cues. A figure **stands in lava** when the tile under it is lava, it does not dash and its foot is below `LAVA_LEVEL`.
- **Entering**: an `enters` cue. A figure listed for the first time gets none, and neither does a dash that ends in lava (a dash over lava counts as in it).
- **Dashing over lava**: `spray` cues at `LAVA.sprayRate` 30 per second.
- **The ring** needs no state: its strength is `clamp((LAVA_LEVEL - foot) / LAVA.ringDepth 0.3, 0, 1)`, so it opens as the figure sinks and closes as it rises. At most `STIR_CAP` 8 rings; with more figures in lava those nearest the camera target (`focus`) win.
- **Burning**: a hero (`isPlayerKind`; heroes only) standing in lava is in `burning`.
- An id in `hidden` makes no cue and no ring, but its state is tracked. Ids no longer listed are forgotten.

**The sparks and embers** (`fx/lavaFx.ts`, `registerLavaFx`; `FxFrame.lava`). `emitLavaCues` turns cues into records: `FX_KIND.LavaBurst` (26; a 0 an entering: 6 orange motes, up to 30 px/s, 0.2 to 0.35 s; a 1 a pop: 14 motes of `0xffcc68` and `0xee8e2e`, up to 65 px/s, 0.7 to 1.2 s, gravity 5, so they fall back onto the surface) and `FX_KIND.LavaSpray` (27; one mote per cue). They glow (the tris list), they are not lit, and are born at `LAVA_Y` (`LAVA_LEVEL + 0.02`). A cue of a hidden id makes no record; a pop has no id (-1), so only the mask decides. The sparks are records, not the embers of `air/puffs.ts`, because a burst needs an arc and the air's embers only rise and sway. The slow sparks and smoke of the air stay as they are. The embers of a burning hero are not records: `emberAt(id, slot, now)` is a pure function of the clock, `EMBER_SLOTS` 6 per hero looping every `EMBER_LIFE` 0.6 s, starting within `EMBER_SPREAD` 6 px of the hero, `EMBER_LIFT` 10 px up and rising `EMBER_RISE` 20 to 32 px/s, size 2 px, fading by `1 - k^3` (a straight fade left dull olive specks on the dark crust). Records would overrun `FX_KIND_CAP` 12 (14 a second for four heroes).

**The heat shimmer** (`lava/lavaNodes.ts`, `shimmerMaterial`; sheets from `buildShimmerChunk`). Three flat sheets lie over each pool at `SHIMMER_SHEETS` (0.02, 0.8 and `HEAT.height` 1.6 tiles above `LAVA_LEVEL`) on `WATER_LAYER`, drawn first among the translucent things (`renderOrder` 0). The material is transparent, depth tested, no depth write, no discard, no `sight.apply`.
- **The ray rule.** A pixel's strength depends only on its view ray: with `toward` the unit vector to the eye, `per = toward.xz / max(toward.y, SHIMMER.viewMin 0.1)` and `G` the ray's point at `LAVA_LEVEL`. The strength is the mean of three normalised reads (`SHIMMER.reads` 3) of the lava mask (the alpha of the lava light texture, already filtered) at the heights 0, H / 3 and 2H / 3 along the ray (H is `HEAT.height`), weights 1, 2/3 and 1/3 over their sum 2 (before a fourth read of weight 0 halved the strength: it peaked at 0.5 and now reaches 1 over a wide pool), times the seen share at `G`. So overlapping sheets agree, the depth test hides the shimmer behind a wall or a figure in front of the pool, and a sheet over a hidden pool draws nothing.
- **The seen share** is `sight.shown(hard, 0)`, not `1 - hard`: with the sight off the mask is the pending all-unseen texel, so the plain form would be 0 and the shimmer would vanish on pages without sight. The mask is read at the top level of the shader (`toVar`) so the select holds no sample.
- **The wobble:** two `vnoise2` reads in the ground's frame (`ahead` is the horizontal direction away from the camera, `HEAT.freq` [2.5, 2.2]) moving away from the camera at `HEAT.rise` [1.6, 1.9] noise cells per second, which is up on the screen. The colour is the scene copy at `screenUV + wobble * HEAT.offset 0.006 * strength`, alpha `smoothstep(0, SHIMMER.fade 0.05, strength)`. The scene copy already went through the sight step, so the shimmer's colour does not pass it again. `HEAT.offset` is a share of the screen: twice as wide in tiles at zoom 2 as at zoom 4.
- **When the copy is made** (`picturePasses`, `poolInView`; `water/passes.ts`): when a lava pool is in view (the lava chunks' boxes, margin 1 tile) and the shimmer is on, the picture is split as for water (`splitPicture` in `view.ts`: opaque scene, a copy of its colour, then the translucent things) and the shimmer sheets are visible that frame; the copy also serves any water in view. Without a lava pool in view or with the shimmer off a lava pool costs no extra pass; `readback` uses the last frame's choice.

**The heat switch and quality.** The parameter `heat` (`params.ts`; URL `heat=0`; on by default) is the panel's switch "Heat shimmer" (after "Reflections") on the demo pages and in runs. `shimmers(params)` is `heat && quality !== 'fast'`: quality Fast leaves the shimmer out whatever the switch says, and then a lava pool needs no copy. Measured on the RTX 3060 (uncapped, zoom 2, Balanced): WebGL2 about 1000 fps and WebGPU about 480 with the shimmer on or off, the difference inside the noise.

**The sink by share** (`dips/descent.ts`, `figures.ts`). The fixed `LAVA_SINK` 1.05 is replaced by `LAVA_SHARE` 0.6 of the figure's height (`lavaSinkOf(height)`), so a big figure sinks to the same share and a hero sinks as before. `stepLavaSink(sink, goal, dt)` eases with `LAVA_IN` 0.55 per second toward the goal and with `LAVA_OUT` 2.5 per second back to 0. `foot` takes the figure's height (tiles, no camera stretch); `figFoot` places the foot after the pose, from the standing anchor's height (3 px when lying; a barrel passes `CHEST_HEIGHT`).

**The pages.** `?r3d=liquids`: the molten surface with its bubbles, the knight sinking to the waist in an open ring, the heat shimmer; `&heat=0` turns the shimmer off; `window.__r3d.liquids.bubbles` is the live count; the page's `Lava3d` has a seeded random source (344), so a pinned clock (`&t=`) shows the same bubbles every time and replays the lava's cues of the 6 s before it. `?r3d=mood` and `?r3d=lights` show the surface with bubbles and rings from arrays only (no effects pass there, so no burst; seeded). The run feeds all of it from its own `Lava3d`. The knight on the liquids page is not a hero, so it shows no embers; they appear only in a run.

**Decisions of #344** (beyond the design).
1. The lava surface is on layer 0, not on the water's layer: it reads nothing from the scene copy, and being in the copy is what lets the shimmer wobble it. The water's mirror pass clips it: water does not mirror lava (no dungeon has both).
2. Self-lit only: the surface does not go through `lightAt`. The "lava cell's own glow" block of `lightAt` and its option `selfLit` are gone; so are the plates, `terrainMesh.ts` and `stepTerrainUv`.
3. The outline and the shore come from vertex attributes, not a texture.
4. The shimmer is three flat sheets with a strength that depends only on the view ray.
5. The scene copy is made when a lava pool is in view and the shimmer is on; quality Fast turns the shimmer off.
6. Accepted limits of the shimmer (below).
7. At most 6 bubbles; a spot needs the mask at 0.95 at its centre and at four points a dome's size away (the implementer's rule, accepted: the plain centre rule let a lone tile bubble); a pool without a spot never bubbles.
8. The pops' sparks are effect records that fall back to the surface; the air's embers stay as they are. Pages without an effects pass show bubbles without the burst.
9. The sink depth is a share of the figure's height, `LAVA_SHARE` 0.6, which keeps what the user judged fine in #324 for a hero (the design's "about 0.45" was written before that look).
10. The ring round a figure is stateless (the foot's depth below the level); at most 8 rings.
11. The lava's effects glow: the entering burst, the dash's spray, the embers on a hero in lava (heroes only, from the clock and the id).
12. Dev pages: `liquids` gets everything, `mood` and `lights` bubbles and rings; each `Lava3d` there has a seeded random source.
13. (During the build) The lava's colours go through the `GLOW` curve inside the material (the plan said raw colours; the approved look was tuned under ACES). The shimmer's reads are three, normalised. The embers got the numbers listed above after a look in a run.

**Tests.** `lava/lavaMesh.test.ts` (nothing without lava, the cells covered, walls and the map's outside left out, a closed door counted and a chasm or water cell skipped, the mask and depth per vertex, a chunk border, facing up, a tile change, three sheets per cell), `lava/bubbles.test.ts` (flat before birth and after the pop, the dome's height and radius, packing and freeing a slot, each pop reported once, the cap, `prune`), `lava/lava.test.ts` (no lava, a bubble every 0.6 to 2 s, none at the banks or in a pool too small, none out of view or unseen, two pools with their own timers and the shared cap, at most one start in a long frame, a bubble dropped when its cell stops being lava, a pool that appears, the pop cue and the mask, the ring by depth, none for a dasher, a ghost or a hidden id, the `STIR_CAP` nearest rings, the entering and spray rules, a hero burning and a monster not, hidden ids tracked, the same arrays every call), `lava/lavaNodes.test.ts` (both materials build in GLSL and WGSL without a TSL error, the uniform buffers within WebGPU's limit, opaque lava and blending shimmer, the slots of bubbles and rings, the scene copy, three reads of the lava light), `fx/lavaFx.test.ts` (the records per cue, hidden ids and the id-less pop, finite vertices at every age, born at `LAVA_Y`, `emberAt` looping and fading, the embers per hero), cases added to `water/passes.test.ts` (the copy for lava only with the shimmer), `dips/descent.test.ts` (`lavaSinkOf`, `stepLavaSink`), `figures.test.ts` (the sink by share for a knight and an ogre, no change with the pitch), `lightNodes.test.ts` (the light's code reads no lava texture), `sightMaterials.test.ts` (both materials), `params.test.ts` and `panel.test.ts` (`heat`), `runView3d.test.ts`, `liquidsStage.test.ts`, `moodStage.test.ts` and `lightsStage.test.ts` (the wiring). `view.ts` has no unit test (it needs a GPU): the shots of `r3d-shot.mjs` on both backends check it.

### Overlays, input and turning in the 3D run (#311)

Package 3b of the 3D renderer: bars, names, numbers, hints, the mouse, aiming and turning. The design and plan are on issue #311. Bars hang from the drawn figure's anchor, and the own hero's numbers are born at the predicted spot (#281); the overlay is its own layer, never darkened or greyed by the scene (#113); all overlay text is drawn at one resolution, the 3D canvas's device px (#63).

**One aim origin** (`run/aim.ts`). The aim is measured from the hero's foot to the aimed point on the foot plane (`aimAt`), not from the body centre. The view places its camera first and then calls `settleOwnAim`, so the drawn weapon uses this frame's camera (`RunView3d.frame` stores the camera, plan, zoom and canvas before it settles). The session reads `pointerWorld()` between frames, so the aim it sends uses the last drawn frame.

**Canvas ↔ world** (`render3d/screen.ts`). `toCanvas(cam, plan, zoom, x, y, z)` projects a world point to canvas device px with the camera as `View.draw` places it (target snapped to whole art px, `drawnCam`) and the plan's destination rectangle; `aimGround` is the inverse for the ground. `pointerWorld()` reads the pointer at shot height: the ray is moved `SHOT_LIFT_PX` (8 art px = `SHOT_LIFT` 0.5 tiles in `camera.ts`) down the screen before it is put on the floor, so a shot aimed at a monster's chest hits the chest at any pitch. Both functions read the current canvas size and plan every frame, never a cached one, so a resize or a zoom change mid-run needs nothing.

**Mouse and keys** (`render3d/input.ts`, `RunMouse`). The listeners sit on the host element (it survives a canvas rebuild after a lost device) and on the window (a release or a blur outside the page ends a drag and clears the buttons). Only a press on the 3D canvas counts: clicks on the dev panel, the HUD or a menu never attack or turn, and keys typed into a text field are ignored (`isTextTarget`, exported from `host.ts`). Left and right buttons latch (`StepInput`), E uses, and the pointer is read against the canvas's client rect, read once per host frame (`RunView3d.frame`) so a resize shows in the next frame. Z, X, C and L do nothing with Ctrl, Cmd or Alt held (copy, the address bar and the like stay the browser's). `preventDefault` is called only for what the game uses.

**Turning** (`render3d/turn.ts`, `CamTurn`). Keys by `KeyboardEvent.code`, so they sit in the same place on any layout (`turnKeyLabels` asks `navigator.keyboard` for the letters shown in the help line: Y on a QWERTZ keyboard is Z's place): `KeyZ` +45°, `KeyC` -45°, `KeyX` north (yaw 0). A turn lasts 0.2 s, eased out (`TURN_SECS`); a press during a turn retargets from the current target, so two presses make 90°. Positive yaw moves the camera to the west of a north-facing view, so Z makes the scene turn with the camera "walking around to the left". A middle-mouse drag turns the view `DRAG_DEG_PER_PX` (0.4°) per CSS px, drag right = raise the yaw (the same way as Z, the common orbit-camera feel; flip one sign if the play-test says otherwise), and snaps to the nearest 45° step on release, the short way across ±180°. The angle is unwrapped inside `CamTurn`, `shown` is wrapped, so eight presses of Z come back to north without a long spin. The stage feeds `shown` to the view each frame and reports the whole degrees to the host (`Host.setRunYaw`: the panel's slider follows; the URL's `yaw` is not written during a run, so a reload starts north). The panel's turn slider still works (`turn.set`). Every run starts facing north whatever the URL says; spectating keeps the angle. While a drag runs the pointer is paused, `pointerWorld()` returns `null`, and the session's no-pointer rule aims along the movement keys (a shot fired mid-drag while walking goes the walking way; on the play-test list). The help line gets the turn keys and "MMB drag to turn" (`Hud.setTurnHelp`, removed when the run ends).

**The overlay layer** (`render3d/overlay.ts`, `overlayLayout.ts`). One Canvas 2D element over the 3D canvas (`.r3d-overlay`), created by the stage so it survives a rebuild after a lost device (the 3D canvas is replaced, the overlay is not). Its backing store is the 3D canvas's size in device px (the host caps the device pixel ratio at 2): overlay text stays sharp at every quality level and zoom, and is never darkened by the scene's light. It is cleared and redrawn every frame. While the device is being restored it still paints from the last anchors, so names do not blink.
- **Layout is plain data.** `overlayLayout.ts` turns the frame into `OverlayItem`s in canvas px with no DOM: `layoutBars` (the HP bars), `layoutHeroTags` (hero names and their status symbols), `layoutTags` (the rules log's creature tags, from `RulesLog.tagOf`), `layoutUseHint` (the gold key cap and its label), plus the `Marks` and `Floats` below. `Overlay3d.paint` only draws the items. Text sizes, weights and colours come from `WORLD_TEXT`, `SOUND_TEXT`, `barName.ts` and `damage.ts`. Text is drawn from the em box bottom.
- **Anchors.** Each item hangs from a `FigureAnchor` that `Figures3d` records for every figure it draws (foot and drawn height, in world units, from the pose of this frame), projected with `toCanvas`, then offset upward by fixed art-px amounts times `zoom`. Because the anchor is the drawn figure's, a bar does not trail its figure, the own hero included.
- **One bar rule.** `barOf` (`game/barName.ts`) decides whether a creature has a bar and how it looks (width, fill, the name over it); `layoutBars` calls it.
- **Floating numbers** (`Floats`, `game/damage.ts` `floatOf`). `floatOf(ev)` turns a `Dmg`, `Heal`, `Immune` or `Coins` event into its text, colour and options. `Floats` keeps each number at the ground spot it was born at and lays it out with its rise, pop and fade (`FLOAT`, `popScale`, rising in screen space); the spot is in the world, so turning the camera moves it with the scene. The server sends `Dmg` and `Heal` at the body, `BODY_RISE` (10) px above the foot, which is a screen-up offset; `Floats.addPicture` stores the foot and lifts the number by `BODY_RISE` art px on the screen instead, so it starts the same height above the foot at every yaw. **Own-hero numbers:** `onOwnHero` judges an event to be on the own hero when it lies within 4 px of the hero's server position in the snapshot that carried it (the server emits at the server position, so only that comparison holds under lag); `floatSpot` then shifts it by predicted position minus that server position, so the number is born where the hero is drawn (#281). A number on another figure within 4 px of the hero's server spot is moved the same way (at most a few px).
- **Marks** (`Marks`): the sleep Z's, the fear "!" and the charm heart over figures, from the effect bits of their anchors laid out from the same anchors.
- **Things in the dark (#309).** The server sends what is in sight and marks what is in the dark (§7). `RunFrame.dark` holds the `dark` ids of the snapshot the frame's entities are drawn from (`Interpolator.drawnDark`, set by `sample`; the interpolation delay makes that snapshot older than the newest, which may no longer send or mark a thing still drawn; the set belongs to that snapshot and is valid during the frame) and passes a `dark` flag per picture event from `dark_ev` (`RunView.event(ev, own, server, dark)`). A marked thing is drawn as a figure, but the 3D view adds nothing over it that would give it away: no bar or name (`layoutBars`), no use hint (`layoutUseHint`), no status marks (`Marks.update` drops its state and starts none), no floats from a marked event (`Floats.addPicture`), no wake cue (`figures.ts`), no wall opening (`seenOpeners`); the server leaves out its rules-log tag. A thing that leaves the mark gets its overlays back on the next frame. Since #305 the same holds for things the picture hides: `hiddenByPicture` (`sightAttrs.ts`, the card's own rule: `thingHard` of `footSight` > 0.5, daylight scenery never) is unioned with `dark` into `RunView3d.hidden`, which openers, bars, hero and rules-log tags, the use hint and marks skip. Known gap: this also hides teammates' and allies' bars beyond sight. Effect visuals of marked events (`figures.event`) are #308's business. **Known gaps:** the mark is per tile while the 3D light will be per pixel, so near a glow's edge a creature can look faintly lit while its bar is still hidden, or the other way round, for up to about half a tile. Since #304 the dungeon's marked figures are drawn dark (§9 "The dungeon's light"): they fade in with the ambient's brightness and take no shadow. The woodland by day marks nothing.
- **Rules log.** `RulesLog` (`rulesLog/index.ts`) holds the opt-in, the keys, the buffer, the panel and `tagOf`; the creature tags are overlay items. The run session owns the log (`SessionDeps.log`), so it hears every snapshot; the stage binds its key (L, Shift+L) and `runView.tagOf`.

**Tests.** `screen.test.ts` (both ways, two canvas sizes and both plan kinds), `turn.test.ts` (steps, retarget, wrap, drag and snap), `input.test.ts` (buttons, drag, a click on the panel, a release outside the window), `overlayLayout.test.ts` (also: a marked creature has no bar, a marked object no use hint, `Marks` drop a marked creature, a dark picture event adds no float), `openers.test.ts` (a marked opener is dropped even on a seen tile), `figures.test.ts` (no wake cue for a marked mimic), `session.test.ts` (the frame carries the `dark` ids of the snapshot its entities are drawn from, so a marked creature that has left the newest snapshot stays marked while it is still drawn; a picture event in `dark_ev` reaches the view marked), `runStage.test.ts` (keys, the overlay's life, the turn help), `imports.test.ts` (the import rules, §12). `tools/e2e/r3d-run.mjs` plays a debug account's run: Z gives yaw 45, X gives 0, the overlay has items, a left click sends a primary Input.

**The panel in a run** (`panel.ts`, mode `run`). It sits at the bottom left so it does not cover the HUD. Rows: pitch, turn, quality, zoom (Auto, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 and a slider to 48; the URL takes 1 to 48, Auto picks 2 to 8), and the switches Wall shadows, Smoothed ground, Game light (on by default; off is `light=work`, the work light), Dark fade (`dark=fade`; off by default, figures in the dark keep a silhouette), Free camera and Sight (#305), the light sliders Ambient, Lights and Casters (the dev pages add Darkvision), and on daylight maps the mist sliders Mist height, Mist top, Mist drift and Mist day. At zoom 1 every quality draws at the screen's zoom. The demo's panel has the zoom row too, and "Game light" instead of Lantern and Free camera.

### Sight in the 3D view (#305)

The 3D view hides what the hero cannot see by the field of vision and its edge: on dark maps the unseen is the clear colour (black rock, one surface with the wall tops in the rock and the space beyond the map); on daylight maps it is mist. Nothing in the server, `sim/vispoly.ts` or `sim/view.ts` changed.

- **The pure parts** (`fillPolygon`, `blurField`, `extendPoints`, `extendRim`, `rampLayers`, `canvasRes`, `SHADE`, `shadeCovers`, `HazeLook`, `DEFAULT_HAZE`) live in `game/sightField.ts`. The 3D view ignores `blurPx` (no blur of the unseen).
- **The mask** (`render3d/sightmask.ts`, `SightMask`). A square in world space centred on the viewer, snapped to its texel grid so the edge does not swim: half side `sightRadius(vision)` plus `SIGHT_MARGIN` (2 tiles), plus `ceil(3 * feather / 16)` tiles on daylight maps so the blurred field is exactly 1 at the border. Outside the square is unseen, so the mask does not depend on the camera (every yaw, pitch, zoom, the free camera). Resolution `canvasRes` (8 texels per tile dark, 4 daylight); the texture covers `n` texels = `n / res` tiles a side (one texel more than `size`). Two channels, RG8: `hard` (beyond sight: the hard polygon by `fillPolygon`, visible hard walls painted whole, on daylight maps `blurField` by the feather) and `soft` (the share behind a trunk: the `shadeCovers` geometry with `rampLayers`; on dark maps `hard coverage * (1 - all coverage)`). Filled on the CPU into float fields with reused scratch, refilled only when the hard view's version, the `all` view's version or the map version changes. `sample(x, z)` is the CPU twin of the GPU's bilinear, clamp-to-edge read. `footSight(m, x, z, reach)` gives one value per thing: the least of its foot and, for wide figures (`reach` > 0.5 tile, the server's `radius > 8` px), the four diagonal corners at ±0.8·reach (`run/visibility.rs` `visible`).
- **The shader side** (`render3d/sightNodes.ts`, `makeSight`; maths in `render3d/mist.ts`). One `Sight` per `View` (made in `buildView`, disposed with it; a new one uploads on its first frame, so a rebuilt view after a lost device gets the mask at once). `view.ts` calls `sight.set(f.sight ?? SIGHT_OFF)` once per frame; a `SightFrame` carries the mode (`off`, `dark`, `mist`, a uniform, so switching needs no rebuild), the mask, the clear colour, the haze and mist looks, `mistDay`, `mistLight`, the wind and the time. `unseenAt(xz)` samples the mask once, at the top level of the shader (uniform control flow, no `select`); a material calls it once and passes the result to `apply` and `shown`. `apply(colour, P, u)` is the last step after `lighting.finish`: dark `mix(c * (1 - SHADE * soft), clear, hard)`; mist `amount = mistAmount(unseenDay(hard, soft), P.y, n)` (strength `max(outside * hard, soft * softShare)`, times the height curve `mistHeight`: full up to `MIST.low` 0.5 tiles, smoothly down to `MIST.top` 0.4 at `MIST.high` 3 tiles; times `1 - drift * n` with one `mx_noise_float` drifting with the wind, so the drift only thins the mist and never moves the edge), then desaturate by `amount * desat` and mix toward `mistColour(day) * mistLight` by `amount * mix`. `mistColour` blends `DEFAULT_HAZE.colour` toward `NIGHT_MIST` (0.1, 0.12, 0.17) as `mistDay` goes to 0; #306 drives `mistDay`, `mistLight` and the wind.
- **Where each surface reads.** World (`worldMaterial`): its point pushed out along the normal by 1.5 texels (`1.5 / res`), so a wall face reads the floor in front of it (the shadow line behind a pillar runs up the wall; a face bordering an unseen room is black whatever lights it); floors and tops read their own point. Cards, flames, glows and shadows: one value per thing, from `footSight` on the CPU, sent as a vertex attribute (`foot` in `CardBuf`/`ShadowBuf`/`GlowBuf`: x, z, reach, scenery; filled per frame by `render3d/sightAttrs.ts`, one `footSight` per quad). Wall torches and their glows read 0.25 tile in front of the face (`WALL_SIGHT_PUSH`), never the wall's own texel. On daylight masks the per-thing value is `thingHard` = `clamp((hard - 0.5) * 2, 0, 1)` (the feather blur is centred on the edge, so without it a seen figure near the edge would already be stippled and misted); the ground keeps the feathered value. `shown = cardShown(hard, scenery, daylight)`: 1 - hard, except scenery on daylight maps (torches, chests, barrels, mimics, skulls, coins; plants from #306), which is misted, never hidden. Opaque cards and flames drop pixels by a 4×4 Bayer dither (`bayerAt`, shared with the world material) where `bayer ≥ shown`, so a creature leaving sight on the woodland fades over the feather; translucent passes multiply alpha by `shown`; glows multiply their colour and shadows their alpha by `shown(hard, 0)`. Flames are self-lit: no `apply`, but dimmed by `SHADE * soft` on dark maps. The force field is a node material ending with `apply` at its own point. A test (`sightMaterials.test.ts`, "every world material ends with the sight step") builds each material in GLSL and WGSL and checks for the sight step; #307 added the air's three materials (and its test builds them the same way); #308 extends the list.
- **The run** (`runView3d.ts`). The viewer is `RunFrame.camera` (the own hero or the spectated teammate; the free camera moves only the 3D camera, never the mask). The hard view comes from `sightFor(f)` (vision fallback `CONST.FOV_RADIUS` before the first snapshot); the `all` view (blocker `'all'`) is computed only while the map has a soft tile (recounted after `tileChanged`, which also bumps the map version). The two `View` objects live for the whole run, so their versions only grow and the mask's keys cannot collide. One `SightFrame` is reused and updated in place; the wind is a constant (`MIST.speed` turned 20°) until #306. F3 books the mask refill under `sight` (after `lights`); the polygon itself stays under `cards`. `?r3d=lights` takes the viewer from `&at=x,y` (tiles) or `VIEWER` and passes a dark frame.
- **Parameters** (`params.ts`, `panel.ts`): `sight` (switch Sight, `sight=0` shows everything), and on daylight maps the sliders Mist height (`mistHigh`, 0.5..6), Mist top (`mistTop`), Mist drift (`mistDrift`) and Mist day (`mistDay`, 0 night .. 1 day). `Panel.setDaylight` shows the mist rows; the run calls it through `Host.setDaylight` once `info.daylight` is known.
- **Tests.** `sightField.test.ts` (the polygon fill, the blur, the ramp layers and the wedge geometry), `sightmask.test.ts` (square, snap, resize, outside, pillar, crisp and feathered edges, soft, refill keys, wide figures), `mist.test.ts`, `sightNodes.test.ts` (both builders; one mask sample at the top level; uploads; a new texture on a size change), `sightAttrs.test.ts` (per-quad values, wall torches on either face), `sightMaterials.test.ts`, and the run, figures, lights-stage, params and panel tests.
- **Open seams for #306: resolved.** A seen trunk or thicket tile has `soft` 0 in the mask (the full view's `touches`, §9 "Woodland light"), plants read their foot once in the shader, and a material that calls `shown` on an `unseenAt` result without `apply` still reads `u.x`/`u.y` into variables first (bees, butterflies, beams). Ground shadows of scenery (chests, barrels) still fade in the daylight feather while the thing stays: the shadow attribute carries no scenery flag yet.
- **Older issues.** #116's wall-tile point is settled for 3D: a wall is no longer shown whole when part of it lies in a pillar's shadow. Remembered tiles (#106) would be a second mask next to this one.

**What a run does not draw:** the server's debug paths (the yellow, blue and green lines of a debug run; §13), point-light shadows of woodland plants (only if wanted) and real ones of the cove's 3D plants (#333; today they cast flat stand-in cards).

### Woodland light, shadows, wind and time of day in 3D (#306)

Package 6 of the 3D renderer (#300). The design and the plan are on issue #306. The woodland run and `?r3d=woodland` get a sun and moon on a clock, shadows from a shadow map, cloud shadows, wind, grown 3D trees (#329, "The trees" below), critters, fireflies and the cove's pixies. The server is unchanged by #306 (the hour is a client-side look; the fingerprint tests did not move); #329 added the leaf amount, the dead mark and the palette to the plant list (§4, §6). #330 then added the ground cover (grass, moss; "Ground cover (#330)" in §9); nothing in the cove is interim any more except the flat stand-in shadows of its 3D plants (#333).

**The sky** (`render3d/sun.ts`, pure maths, x east, y up, z south). `sky(hour)` gives a `Sky { dir, colour, ambient, night, day, haze, elevation, moon }`. The sun's elevation is `62 sin(pi s)` for `s = (hour - 6) / 14` in 0..1 (06:00 to 20:00) and `s * 40` before, `(1 - s) * 40` after; it rises in the west of the screen, passes behind the forest (north) at noon and sets in the east. The sun colour goes from deep orange `(1.05, 0.48, 0.24)` to `(1.0, 0.93, 0.8)` by elevation (2 to 32 degrees) and fades out between -1.5 and 6 degrees; the ambient goes from `(0.035, 0.045, 0.09)` to `(0.3, 0.34, 0.44)` by `day` (-7 to 18 degrees) with a warm dusk term; below -1.5 degrees the light is the moon (direction `(0.35, 0.85, -0.45)`, blue, fading in to -9 degrees); `night` is `1 - smooth(-9, 0, el)`. `Sky.haze` is not used: the daylight mist darkens through `mistDay` (`day`) and `mistLight` (the luminance of ambient plus sun over its value at 13:00, as grey), fed by `mistInputs(sky, wind)`. `cardSunGain(dir)` is how strongly the sun lights an upright card (`CARD_GAIN`, the shader builds the same expression).

**The hour.** A run's start hour is `startHour(info.woodland.sun)`, the hour at which the sun stands at the level's elevation on its side. Time runs at `HOUR_RATE` = 1 game hour per 720 real seconds (first guess) times the panel's or URL's `hourRate` (0 stops time), counted in run seconds (`snapshot.tick / TICK_HZ`), so it stands still until the first snapshot and pauses with the run. `HourClock` is the anchored clock; `SkyClock` adds the panel's inputs: a new hour re-anchors there, a new pace continues from the hour reached (no jump), and the first call takes the URL's hour and pace so a pace of 0 does not run an hour first. The run (`runView3d.ts`) and the dev page (`woodlandStage.ts`) share `SkyClock`, `sunFrame`, `mistInputs` and `ovalOf`, so they cannot drift apart. One clock (`c.now`, seconds) drives mist, cloud shadows and sway. The ground ovals under figures have the strength `0.5 + 0.5 * night` of the dungeon's value on the woodland (`ovalOf`; `FigureLook.oval`); the sun's shadows carry the grounding by day.

**The shadow map's fit** (`sun.ts`). `fitSunMap(dir, fp, map, size)` fits a square orthographic map to what the camera shows: `sunView(footprint, cam)` first grows the screen's ground footprint by `SUN_TOP / tan(pitch)` toward the camera (a tall thing whose crown is at the bottom of the screen stands that far nearer than the edge), then the box is placed in light space (`sunBasis`: F along the light, R level, U = R x F; R = (1, 0, 0) for a vertical light) and its centre snapped to whole texels so shadows do not shimmer. `SUN_TOP` = 10 tiles (above the tallest plant the generator draws). Size by quality: High 4096, Balanced 2048, Fast 1024 (`SUN_MAP`, clamped to the device limit before the fit: `sunFrame(..., cap)` takes `View.maxTexture`, so `fit.size` and the texel snap are the real ones). A low sun (5 degrees) or the moon makes the box long along the light but finite; the fit has two texels of edge margin. `sunCoord(fit, P)` is the CPU twin of the shader's lookup (u, v across the map, depth 0..1 from the near plane), pinned against `sunCamera` in `sunNodes.test.ts`.

**The sun pass and the receivers** (`view.ts`, `sunNodes.ts`, `lightNodes.ts`, `materials.ts`). In a world with a sun (`world.painted`, known in `buildView`; a dungeon builds none of this) the view renders the casters into a depth-only `RenderTarget` with a `DepthTexture` (compare `LessEqual`, linear) after the 'upload' lap, booked as the F3 system `sun`, and remakes it when the size changes; a lost device brings it back with the rest of the view. `FrameInput.sun?: SunFrame | null` carries `dir`, `colour`, `fit`, `shadows`, `clouds`, `wind` and `t` (absent is null). `makeSunLight().term(P, Ps, ndl)` is `colour * ndl * sunVis(Ps) * cloud(P)`, added to the light in `lightAt` (before any `If`, so the depth sample sits at the top level; arithmetic, no branch, outside the map is lit). The depth bias is 0.0003. **The v flip is `SUN_FLIP_V = true` on both backends**: three's TSL `texture()` flips the uv of a render target on the GLSL backend itself, so TSL sees v = 0 at the top on both; checked on WebGL2 pictures and on WebGPU by `View.readback()` (§10). Receivers: the world (`Ps = P + N * 0.03`, `ndl = mix(max(dot(N, dir), 0), 1, wrap)`), cards (turned to face the light around the new `cardFoot` attribute, nudged 0.14 tiles toward the light, `ndl` the card gain), flat decals (their own point, `ndl = max(dir.y, 0)`). Casters: figures and objects through the opaque cards' twin mesh (`DynMesh.twin`, shares the geometry; the caster material alpha-tests the atlas texel and **is stippled by sight** like the opaque card, so a hidden monster casts nothing), and the trees' own caster meshes (`TreeLayer`, "The trees" below; the cove's atlas cards cast nothing into the sun map, and the cove has no sun). Walls cast no sun shadow (the woodland has none). `lightAt`'s `opts.selfFoot` lets a plant skip its own caster card (the caster whose foot is within 0.02 tiles), so a plant is not self-shadowed by the point lights' card casters; the trees pass it too. The panel's "Wall shadows" switch also switches the sun's shadows (`SunFrame.shadows`): with it off the view skips the sun pass altogether (no target made or drawn into, the F3 `sun` lap about 0), the shader's shadow uniform is 0 and the shaders bind the last map or a 1x1 stand-in depth texture. `lightAt` builds the own-foot test only for a material that passes `selfFoot` (a JS-time choice; the dungeon's and the figures' shaders are as before). The woodland page sends no sun frame under `light=work`, as a run does.

**Cloud shadows** (`sunNodes.ts`, `CLOUD`). A layer 14 tiles up; value noise `n = vnoise(q) * 0.65 + vnoise(q * 2.3 + 7.1) * 0.35` with scale 0.065 per tile, shade `1 - 0.62 * smoothstep(0.47, 0.66, n)`, drifting at 2.4 times the wind's speed (`CLOUD.drift`) along `WIND`. They multiply the sun term only (not the ambient), so at night they do nothing. `clouds=0` switches them off.

**Wind** (`wind.ts`, `windNodes.ts`). `sway(x, z, y, h, amp, t) = amp * (y/h)^2 * (0.5 sin(1.3t + 0.9x + 0.4z) + 0.25 sin(2.9t + z) + 1.4 gust)` with `gust = max(0, sin(0.33(x + 0.3z) - 1.5t))^6`, the offset along the wind's direction; `swayNode` is the same in TSL (the CPU function is the spec; there is no numeric parity test, the shader is checked to build). `WIND` (the mist's wind, moved here from `runView3d.ts`) and `WIND_DIR` give the direction. Amplitudes by kind (`SWAY`): leafy 0.11, conifer 0.07, bush / tuft / fern 0.05, bare 0, cove tree 0.11. `gust(x, z, t)` and `gustNode` are the travelling gust alone (0..1), split out of `sway` for the leaf patches' flutter. The grown trees take their sway from `swayK` per vertex (below). `wind=0` stops the sway, the flutter, the falling leaves' drift, the cloud drift and the mist drift.

**Seen trunks are not misted** (`sightmask.ts`). The fill zeroes `soft` on every soft-blocker tile (opaque, not hard-opaque: trunks, thickets) that the full view `touches` (`touches(all, x, z)` of `sim/view.ts`: the first trunk on a line of sight and grazing faces, not the stricter `hard.isVisible`), after the soft field is built. A tree behind it keeps its mist. `hiddenAt(mask, x, z)` (`thingHard(footSight(...)) > 0.5`; `hard` 1 means unseen) is the cull every woodland thing uses (pixies, fireflies, critters, cove casters): it is the rule of `hiddenByPicture` (`sightAttrs.ts`).

**Critters** (`critters3d.ts`; the seeds, order and maths are in `game/woodland/critterMath.ts`). Bees loop around the flower patches at 0.35 tiles up (`BEE_LIFT`), butterflies wander over the grass at their flap lift plus 0.25 tiles (landed: 0.02 and sunk to the ground). Each is a few solid pixel rects, drawn as opaque cards of the atlas frame `floor_1` cropped to the rect in fill mode (`PX_FRAME`), so the sun lights them and their tiny shadows fall through the cards' sun twin; the lowest pixel row sits on the lift. By day only (the `day` factor); skipped where `hiddenAt`. Booked in the 'cards' lap. 127 critters on the dev page.

**Fireflies** (`fireflies.ts`). 70 additive glows (0.18 tiles across, colour `(0.8, 1.0, 0.4)`, blinking slowly) drifting in a 32 x 20 tile box that wraps around the viewer, 0.3 to 1.1 tiles up; shown with `night` as the brightness, none by day, none on a hidden texel. Seeded, cosmetic, no art. The box edges can show at a low zoom (the size is the user's pick).

**The cove** (`cove3d.ts`). `Pixies3d` draws the cove's pixies (7 in the cove, one hovering 14 px on the dev page): a glow and a core glow each, two wing fill cards in the ghost pass, and a moving light each (`Cand` `x<i>`, radius 2, gain 0.6, no shadow, kind Magic, `PIXIE_LIGHT`) joined to the candidates, so they light the trunk, roots and ground; the position is an ellipse (`pixieMath.ts`) with its vertical axis split into depth (0.6) and height (0.4) around the canopy middle, `CANOPY_UP` (50 px) up. The wings sit one art px left and right of the orb's centre, at its height (one px higher on the `wingUp` frames): a glow is not stretched but a card's height is (by 1 / cos(pitch), `cardMaterial`), so a wing card's lift is the pixie's height times cos(pitch), and a half-px offset centres the 1 px crop (#340; `cove3d.test.ts` checks it at 30°, 45° and 48.6° and with a turn). That is the path on the dev page; the cove in a run gives `Pixies3d` an `orbit` instead (below). `Cove3d` holds the cove's plants (#329): the willow (`tree()`), the bushes as grown bushes (`bushes()`, one spec per `cove_bush*` frame, scale from the frame's width over 32 px), the tufts and ferns as bushel spots of the ground cover (`bushelSpots()`: a cluster at each plant's foot, ferns lower and wider; they cast no shadow card), and the casters (`casters()`: ids from `CAST_ID0` = -1000 down, filtered by `hiddenAt` when sight is on). **The willow** (`coveWillow`, `fitWillow`): a `willow` spec at the cove tree's foot (`coveLayout(c).foot`) with the cove's seed. Its size is the largest `scalePct` from 200 down to 60 in steps of 5 (`WILLOW_SCALE`, first guesses; the cove's willow is a special, larger one than an ordinary willow) whose built reach (`BuiltTree.radius`) stays 0.5 tiles (`WALL_GAP`) inside the nearest wall; a size the generator's drawing canvas cannot hold throws and counts as too large; if none fits, the smallest is used. It is built once: `Cove3d.builder()` hands the forest the tree the fit already built. The forest of a cove run is the willow and the bushes (`RunView3d`, `coveStage.ts`); a level with both a woodland and a cove is refused (the run has one forest). **No sun:** `hasSun` is false in a dungeon, so there is no sun material, no shadow-map read in the colour shader, and the crown's form takes its light direction straight up; the willow and bushes are lit by the cove's torches, the pixies and the base light like everything there, and their shade steps through the palette ramps as in the woodland. Against those point lights the willow casts a flat stand-in card (the picture of the `cove_tree` frame, as wide as its reach and as tall as it is) and each bush its card, until #333 gives them real shadows. **The pixies** (`COVE_PIXIES`, all first guesses; the user: "we'll adjust later"): of the 7, the first 3 circle above the crown (`aboveLift` 0.5 tiles over the willow's height, bobbing `aboveBob` 0.25, on an ellipse `aboveSpan` 0.7 times its reach), the other 4 circle the trunk outside the curtains (`ringGap` 1 tile beyond the reach, wobbling `ringWobble` 0.4, at 0.15 to 0.45 of its height, one lap in about `ringPeriod` 45 s, alternating directions), always inside the room's walls; the pixies' lights follow them. **The cove's grass carpet** is the ground cover's cove source (`cover/cove.ts`): it grows 3D grass over the carpet's shape (`paintCove`) and keeps the carpet's colours.

**The trees (#329).** Every tree, conifer and bush of the 3D view is real geometry, grown at run time from the plant's values; the sun's shadow map lights and shades it, turning the camera shows another side of it, and one system carries the round, wide and tall trees, the willow, the conifer, the bush, the dead tree and the cove's willow. The cards of #306 (the plant pictures, root decals, trunk-card and clump-disc casters, `plantField.ts`) are gone. Plants are scenery: they always cast, a plant in the woodland is never hidden, and a tree reads its sight once at its foot in the shader. Nothing is painted into a tree: the light decides the brightness and the palette ramps give the colour. The look is the user's approved one (2026-11-14: leaf amounts, silhouettes, tones and the willow as they are); every number below is a first guess unless it says otherwise.

**From plant to tree** (`trees/spec.ts`, `grow.ts`, `build.ts`, `mesh.ts`). `treeSpecOf(PlantInfo)` gives a `TreeSpec { form, x, z, seed, scalePct, mirrored, leaf, dead }`: `form` is `round`, `wide`, `tall`, `conifer` or `bush` from the wire kind (`willow` exists only as a spec built by hand: the cove's tree and the dev page's), the foot is the px over 16, `leaf` is the percent over 100. `lookKey(spec)` = `form:seed:scalePct:mirrored:dead`: what the mesh depends on; the foot and the leaf amount are not in it, so equal looks share one build. `buildTree(spec)` grows a `Skeleton` (`growLeafy` for round, wide and tall; `growWillow`; `growPlant` for conifer and bush) and turns it into the mesh streams. **Sizes come from the plant recipe** (`trees/recipe.ts`, #334): each grower calls `plantRecipe(form, seed, scalePct)` (the willow `leafyRecipe({ ..., shape: 'wide', old: true })`) and takes the trunk path, its width and the scale from the `Recipe` (trunk, roots, limbs, lobes and skirts in the px of the painter's canvas, y down), so `scale_pct` means what it always meant and no size table exists. The module imports nothing and paints nothing. It is the part of the painters (`game/vegetation/*`) that measures: the frame, `trunkPath`, `rootPaths`, the leafy shapes' table, the conifer's tiers and skirt outlines (`coniferStart`, `coniferTier`, `skirtColumns`) and the bush's lobes, stem and twigs (`bushPlan`). A plant has one random stream per seed (`rng`), and the painter draws from it between the recipe's parts (a shadow-line draw per limb point, the bark lines, a noise value per skirt pixel, the dabs). The recipe steps over exactly those draws with `Rng.skip(n)` (the generator's state is a counter, so skipping costs nothing); the counts come from helpers both sides use (`limbSteps`, `barkLineCount`, `skirtColumns().draws`). So every `(form, seed, scalePct)` gives the recipe it gave when picture and recipe were made together: `recipe.test.ts` pins FNV hashes of 1 680 recipes recorded before the move, once from the module and once from `drawPlant(...).recipe`. When #333 deletes the painters, the skips stay (they are what keeps each tree's shape) and the second test goes. The grown tree then has its own limbs. **Forms:** a leafy tree has the recipe's trunk bent a little in depth with a root flare (radius up to 1.35 times at the foot, over 0.55 tiles) and 3 to 5 root ridges that sink into the ground, four limbs that fork twice in space (`TABLE`: round tilts 0.15 to 1.1 rad with a leader of 24 design units, wide 0.8 to 1.2 with a leader of 8, tall 0.3 to 0.6 with a leader of 34 and smaller lobes), a leader, and a crown lobe at every branch end (`coreBall`: an icosphere of 42 vertices and 80 triangles at 0.74 of the lobe, under `tufts`: 14 to 64 camera-facing leaf patches on the lobe's surface, shaded as the round lobe). The **willow** has no lobes: heavy limbs under curtains of hanging leaf tufts (`tuftPatch`) that start along the limbs and hang in tiers nearly to the ground, yellow-green by its own shift on top of the plant's colour (`WILLOW_SHIFT`), some lower tufts casting no shadow. A **conifer** is stacked cones with a zigzag hem (`skirt`), a **bush** a few lobes with tufts, both from the recipe's skirts and lobes. A **dead** leafy tree is the same wood with no lobes at all, in the dead-wood ramp and without sway; a dead conifer or bush (only the dev page can make one) keeps its shape and leaves with dead wood and no sway. `mirrored` mirrors the finished mesh left to right (`mirrorX`, winding flipped, which is why every tree material is double sided). `BuiltTree` also says the tree's `height`, its horizontal `radius` (leaves and sway included), the spots leaves may fall from and whether it sheds leaves. A leafy tree stays below the height the shadow map's fit assumes (`SUN_TOP`, tested for the scales the server sends); the cove's willow, which has no sun, may be higher.

**The leaf amount.** Every lobe, and every tuft of a willow's curtain, carries the leaf amount below which it is gone (its threshold, `misc.w`, 0 for wood and for a conifer's or bush's foliage). A vertex of foliage (`isFoliage`: skirt, tuft or core ball) collapses far below the ground when its threshold is above the plant's amount (`leafGone`), the same way in all three passes, so changing the amount needs no rebuild: it is a shader input (`plant.z`; the dev page's `leaf` replaces a living plant's own, `plantLeaf`). Wood is never thresholded: a tree at amount 0 is bare and still sways. In a leafy tree the lobes go in a fixed order: the inner lobes (where the leader forks) are the last to go, the outer ones go first, a whole branch at a time; the k-th of N lobes in that order is gone below `(k / N) ^ (1 / LEAF_CURVE)` with `LEAF_CURVE` 2.1, so the share of lobes left at amount a is a^2.1 (lobes overlap, which is why a linear curve hardly thins at first). A willow's tufts get random thresholds from 0.1 to 1. The amount acts on leafy trees and willows only: conifers and bushes ignore it for now. **Dead is a separate mark** (`plant.z` = -1 on the wire to the shader): dead means the dead-wood ramp, no leaves and no sway; a winter tree would be alive with amount 0.

**The vertex streams** (`TreeMesh`, `packTreeStreams`, `chunkGeometry`). A WebGPU pipeline takes at most 8 vertex buffers (a ninth made it draw no trees), so a chunk's geometry has exactly 8 attributes, four floats each except `position`: `position` (x, y, z in world tiles, the foot already added), `nrm` (the normal; w = the vertex's sway factor in tiles), `grad` (the crown gradient xyz, -1 to 1, and the height darkening in w), `part` (ramp row, shape code `SHAPE` wood 1, skirt 2, tuft 3, core 4, the vertex's own warm shift, its own bright shift), `misc` (the light lift; for wood the standing shade, for a skirt the hem's v, for a tuft 1 = casts no shadow; 1 on the trunk; the leaf threshold), `tuft` (a patch's corner x and y, half-size in tiles, seed), `plant` (foot x, z; the leaf amount, -1 for dead; the vertex's height for the card stretch) and `look` (leaf brightness, leaf warmth, bark lightness, bark grey: the plant's own colour). The sway factor and the height are packed into `nrm.w` and `plant.w` for the limit. `plant` and `look` are per vertex and built by the `Forest`; equal looks share one `TreeMesh`.

**Memory (#335).** Every stream is a typed array that grows (`Stream`: `Float32Array`, the index `Uint32Array`, the capacity doubled when full; `view` is the used part and shares the stream's memory). A `TreeMesh` (one look, plant-local, the streams `position`, `normal`, `grad`, `sway`, `part`, `misc`, `tuft`, `index`) is read through getters that return views; read a stream once per loop, not once per vertex. A chunk is a `ChunkMesh`: its streams are already the 8 attributes above, packed when a tree is appended (`ChunkMesh.append`: position moved to the foot, `nrm` and `plant` packed, `look` filled in, indices offset; `packedLeaf` is the one rule for leaf -1), so `chunkGeometry` hands three.js views of the forest's streams and three.js keeps no CPU copy of its own. That is safe because the forest only appends past a view's end, and a growth moves the stream to a new buffer and leaves old views as they were. The forest stays the source after a lost device: the new layer reads the chunks again. `packTreeStreams` is left for single meshes (the shader tests). On the demo woodland (692 plants, 1.49 million vertices, 36 chunks, measured with vite-node) the forest and its geometries went from about 676 MB of JS heap (472 MB of `number[]` streams, 204 MB of three.js copies) to 292 MB; about 85 MB of it is the doubling's spare capacity. Positions are float32 in the plant's frame and rounded again after the move to the foot (under 2.4e-7 tiles); every other stream is byte for byte what it was (`treeLayer.test.ts` pins the demo woodland's chunk geometries).

**The three passes** (`treeNodes.ts`, `treeMaterials`). All three build their point from one `treePoint`, so the sway, the collapse of leafless foliage and the patch spread cannot drift apart. (1) The **depth pre-pass** (`TREE_ORDER.depth` 0.25, after the opaque world, which has order 0): alpha tested, no colour written; it discards a patch's cut-out cells (the ragged edge is made from art-pixel cells of the patch's seed: `patchCut`), the cells the sight's stipple hides (`sight.shown`, the tree reads its sight once at its foot) and, on foliage only, the cells where the see-through mask has a disc (`seeOpenNode`: a hero or creature or chest that opens walls (#311) behind a crown thins the leaves in front of it; wood never opens). (2) The **colour pass** (`TREE_ORDER.colour` 0.5): no alpha test, no depth write, depth test **equal** to what the pre-pass wrote, so the expensive tree shader runs once per pixel and not once per overlapping patch. The two are different programs, so both materials are `TreePassMaterial`s whose clip position is **invariant** (GLSL `invariant gl_Position`, WGSL `@invariant`); without that the equal-depth test missed pixels and showed specks. (3) The **sun caster** (only with a sun, `renderOrder` 0 in the sun's scene): the cut-outs without the stipple and the see-through, and a patch marked "casts no shadow" is gone, so a plant's shadow does not change when foliage opens or sight changes. A leaf patch is spread across the axes of the camera that renders it (`cameraWorldMatrix`): the view camera's in the two camera passes, the sun camera's in the sun pass. All three are double sided. The tree's place in `TREE_ORDER`: ground 0, tree depth 0.25, tree colour 0.5, falling leaves 0.6, then the translucent passes (shadows 1, decals and force field 2, ghosts 3, glows 4).

**Light and colour.** The colour pass is lit by the view's own light (`treeLightAt` -> `lightAt` with the sun term through the shadow map read at the art-pixel cell of the point in the tree's swaying frame, nudged off the surface by 0.05 tiles toward the light and along the normal, cloud shadows, ambient, the point lights) and ends with `Sight.apply`. In a level without a sun the same material works with the point lights and the base light. The light picks the ramp step, read against the sky (`trees/tone.ts`, pure maths; `treeToneNode` builds the same expressions; #339): `b` is the luminance of the light; the sky's level is `skyLevel(ambient, colour)`, the luminance of the ambient light plus the sun's or moon's colour over noon's (`NOON_LIGHT`, the value at 13:00), between `SKY_LEVEL_MIN` 0.02 and 1, and 1 in a level without a sun; `rel = clamp(b / level, TREE_LIGHT.lo, TREE_LIGHT.hi)` (0.3 and 1.7: just under a noon shade's 0.34 and above the noon sun's 1.27) and the step is `log2(rel / TREE_LIGHT.mid) * TREE_LIGHT.gain` (`mid` 0.7, `gain` 0.8; all first guesses) plus the crown's own form term (the ball's normal and gradient against the light direction with the height darkening and a little noise); for leaves the thresholds `TREE_LIGHT.steps` (-0.75, -0.3, 0.2, 0.6) pick one of the five leaf tones, for bark `barkSteps` (-0.5, 0, 0.55) pick one of four (a step darker in shade, wavy dashed lines on the trunk); a skirt's hem is its darkest tone; a few light dabs lift a lit tone by one. The palette is a 5 x 4 nearest texture (`paletteBytes`: rows in `RAMP` order leaf, conifer, bark, dead; the 4-tone rows repeat their last tone) from the level's `LEAF_PALETTES[id]`; the summer palette is the painters' ramps (`P` in `game/vegetation/canvas.ts`). The result is the palette tone times the plant's colour, divided by `rel` and then multiplied by the light in `Lighting.finish`, so the ramp step decides, the light's colour tints it and the tone is as dark as the sky: a tree shows the same tones at dawn, dusk and night as at noon, darker by the sky's level. Outside the ramp's range the step stays at the range's end (the form term still picks different tones) and the tone darkens or brightens with the light; a torch at night lifts a tree to the light tones. At noon the rule is the light's own brightness, as before #339. Before it the step came from `b` itself and the divisor was `max(b, 0.12)`: at twilight every pixel fell on the darkest tone, shown at full strength in the ambient's tint (flat crowns, pink trunks). The falling leaves follow the same rule. **Leaf patches have no rim rule** (the spike had none; a rim there read as specks). **Darkvision:** `finish(albedo, lit, greyAlbedo)` has a third, optional argument, the colour the darkvision grey is made from; trees pass the undivided texel, because the division by the brightness belongs to the lit colour only (the grey was about 8 times too bright otherwise). Every other caller leaves it out and gets the default. **Colour variation per plant** (`variation.ts`, the user's picks of 2026-11-14): `plantVariation(x, z)` hashes the foot (on the 1/16 tile grid) into four values, uniform inside the ranges: leaf brightness within 24.5 % either way, leaf warmth (red against blue) within 28 %, bark brightness within 19 %, bark grey (toward grey, or negative toward more colour) within 38 %; leaves multiply by `(bright * (1 + warm), bright, bright * (1 - warm))`. The bark ranges stay as they are for now (easy to change in `RANGE`).

**Wind, gust and flutter.** A living tree sways as `swayNode(foot, 1, 1, k, t, on)` with the vertex's own factor `k = amp * (y / height)^2` (`swayK`; `amp` 0.11 leafy, 0.07 conifer, 0.05 bush, 0 dead); lobes use the factor at their centre, so the whole crown moves together and no art pixel snaps. In a gust the leaf patches also flutter on their own: along and across the wind by `FLUTTER_PX` 0.6 art pixels, with their own phase, scaled by `gustNode` and only for a living tree. The **shadow sways with the crown**: the sun caster uses the swayed point, so the sun flecks on the ground move.

**Card proportions.** Figures and the painters' pictures are stretched by 1 / cos(pitch) on the screen; the trees keep that height: the camera passes lift a point by `sway.y * (1 / cosP - 1)` (`cosP` is set by the view each frame in `TreeShared`), which stretches height only. Light, sight and shadows read the true position (`point`), and the sun pass is not lifted.

**The forest** (`forest.ts`, `chunks.ts`). `Forest(specs, map, palette, builder = buildTree)` builds the specs' looks a few per frame: `step(cam, budgetMs)` runs a `DrawQueue` (`game/woodland/plantpages.ts`) with `TREE_BUDGET_MS` = 3 ms (first guess; at least one look per call), nearest to the camera first, sorted again after the camera moved 4 tiles. Each look is built once (the key above) and appended into the mesh of the world chunk (`CHUNK` = 16 tiles, `plantChunks`) the plant's foot lies in, with its `plant` and `look` per vertex (`plantVariation` of the foot), already packed (a dead plant's leaf is -1). Once every tree is built the forest lets go of its look cache (`cachedLooks` is 0): the chunks keep their merged meshes, and the cache only shared builds between trees. A chunk's **bounds** hold what is drawn, not the raw vertices: the patches' spread across the camera axes (the largest half-size times sqrt 2, since the right and up axes' horizontal parts together reach that far), the sway (the mesh's largest sway factor times `SWAY_WAVE_MAX` = 0.5 + 0.25 + 1.4 = 2.15, the largest value of the wave in `wind.ts` that `sway` and the shader's `swayNode` share; a willow's curtain swing included), the flutter and at least 0.3 tiles all round, and the height stretched by 1 / cos of the steepest pitch. Every tree added raises its chunk's version (`TreeFrame.versions`), `built` and `total` count the trees, and `TreeFrame.complete` says that every tree is built. `frame()` also carries the palette id, the wind switch, the time and the dev page's `leafOverride`, `plain` and `leafRate`.

**TreeLayer** (`treeLayer.ts`). The view makes one `TreeLayer` (`buildView`, fed `FrameInput.trees`): for every changed chunk one `BufferGeometry` with three meshes on it (depth and colour in the scene, the caster in the sun's scene when there is a sun), and one more mesh with the chunk's falling leaves when it has any; each is culled by the chunk's bounds. A chunk still growing is uploaded at most every `TREE_UPLOAD_MS` = 250 ms (first guess; the first time at once, and at once when the forest is complete) so an upload does not repeat for every added tree. `ChunkSync` tells a consumer which chunk versions are new; a different `Forest` makes the layer start over, and a rebuilt view (a lost device) makes a new layer that reads every chunk again. One palette texture and one set of materials serve all chunks (`makeTreeShared` holds the uniforms: `t`, `windOn`, `cosP`, `leafOverride`, `plain`, `leafRate`). The leaf program is compiled before the first trees come (`warm`, which puts one gone leaf in the scene for `compileAsync`), so it does not add to the frame the first trees appear in.

**Falling leaves** (`leaves.ts`, `leafMaterial`, `leafGeometry`). Rare single leaves start from a tree's real lobes (one per lobe; for a willow from the end of each limb), only from lobes that still have leaves (the leaf's threshold is the lobe's and the same `plantLeaf` rule gates it, also for the dev page's override), in the plant's own tint (a willow's with its shift). Each leaf has its own period of 90 to 300 s (`LEAF_PERIOD`; with its own phase, so two trees of one look fall out of step), falls for `LEAF_FALL` 6 s while the wind carries it 0.6 to 1.4 times `LEAF_DRIFT` (1.8 tiles) and it flutters aside by up to `LEAF_FLUTTER` 0.12 tiles, lies flat on the ground for `LEAF_LIE` 8 s and fades for `LEAF_FADE` 2 s (a Bayer pattern, the world is pixel art), then waits for its next period. All of these are first guesses. A leaf is a camera-facing quad of `LEAF_HALF` = 1/16 tile half-width (two art pixels across) while falling and flat on the ground when it lies; it is lit like the leaves (tone picked by the light, on an up-pointing normal), takes the card stretch and the sight step, casts no shadow and has no depth pass. Only a living leafy tree or willow sheds leaves (`BuiltTree.drops`): none for a dead tree, a conifer or a bush. `leafAt` is the CPU twin of the shader's path (a test pins that the shader carries the same constants). The dev page's `leafRate` divides the period (never below a leaf's whole life, `LEAF_LIFE`).

**Speed.** Measured once, 2026-11-14, on the dev notebook's discrete card (RTX 3060), a woodland run at High, 2560 x 1280, zoom 8, uncapped: WebGL2 238 fps mean (median frame 4.1 ms, 1 % low 105 fps, longest frame 11 ms), WebGPU 178 fps mean (median 1.9 ms, 1 % low 11 fps, longest 91 ms); the forest of about 690 trees was built in 7.2 s, the longest build frame about 47 ms (66.7 ms once the falling leaves were added, 33.4 ms after their program was compiled ahead), about 2 million triangles in 49 to 50 draw calls. High, Balanced and Fast draw the same trees. If a real run misses 60 fps, the first lever is less detail on trees far from the camera and at the forest's edge; the willow's look near the camera is not changed without asking the user. WebGPU's long frames and the build hitches (which the loading screen, #322, may cover) are open.

**The dev page and parameters.** `?r3d=woodland` (`woodlandStage.ts`, `woodlandMap.ts`): a 40 x 26 map with a forest edge (thicket and trunk rows: a round, a wide, a mirrored tall, a conifer, a dead and a mirrored round tree, and six bushes) north of a road, the line-ups for the pictures (`LEAF_ROW`: five round trees of one seed at leaf amount 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100 on row 22; `SHAPE_ROW`: round, wide and tall trees at three seeds each on row 10, to tell the silhouettes apart; `DEAD_TREE`, a dead tall tree; and a willow, `WOODLAND_STAGE_EXTRA`, a spec the server never sends), the ground painted whole as in a run, four standing figures, a fireball circling (its light joins the pixie's), and a knight on WASD with per-axis tile collision; the daylight mist's sight mask runs around the knight. URL parameters, besides the 3D keys: `hour` (0..24; default 9.5, `DEFAULT_HOUR`), `hourRate` (0..100, times `HOUR_RATE`; 0 stops time), `wind=0`, `clouds=0`, and for the trees (#329) `leaf` (0..100: the leaf amount forced on every living tree; absent: each tree's own; a dead tree keeps none), `plain=1` (every tree in flat colour, one tone per ramp: the silhouettes, for telling the shapes apart) and `leafRate` (1..1000, times as often as the falling leaves fall; not in the panel), and `at=x,z` (the knight, and so the camera, starts on that walkable tile; for the pictures). The panel (`Panel.setSky`, `Host.setSky`) shows a "Time of day HH:MM" slider (step 15 min) and the switches Time runs, Wind and Clouds while a sky is present (the woodland page, and every woodland run), and hides "Mist day", which the hour then drives. With a sky it also shows a "Leaves N" slider (0 to 100, step 5) with an "Own" button ("Leaves own": each tree's own amount) and a "Plain trees" switch. The slider shows the URL's hour (or 9:30), not the live hour. A woodland run uses the work light only with `light=work` (`WORK_AMBIENT`, no sun; plants still drawn).

**F3 lines.** In a woodland run and on the page: `sun        <hh:mm> <px> px` (the hour label and the shadow map's size in px) and `plants     <built> of <total>` (the trees built of all: it counts up while the forest grows; `treeLine` in `stats.ts`; a cove run shows this line without the `sun` line); the laps `sun` (the view's pass) and `plants` (the forest's step, booked on dungeon runs too, about 0 ms). The dev pages put the same two numbers in `window.__r3d.plants` (`{ built, total }`) for the scripts.

**Open questions for the user's hardware check.** 60 fps on High with a full view of trees, the WebGPU shadow direction (checked by readback, not by eye), the look of the hours, the sway, the clouds and the night. First guesses: the pace (720 s per hour), the fireflies, the pixie light, the cloud numbers, and every number of the trees (budgets, `LEAF_CURVE`, `TREE_LIGHT`, the leaves' periods and paths, the cove's willow size range and its pixies' paths). Point-light shadows of woodland plants are a follow-up only if wanted; the cove's 3D plants cast flat stand-ins until #333.

**Tests.** `sun.test.ts` (the sky at fixed hours, `startHour`, the clock and `SkyClock`, the fit at 5, 62 degrees and the moon, texel snap, edge margin, screen-bottom coverage at four yaws, `sunView`, `sunCoord`), `sunNodes.test.ts` (`sunCamera` against `sunCoord`, the term builds in GLSL and WGSL with the compare sampler and the step fallback, one depth sample at the top level, `set`), `wind.test.ts` (with the `gust` split), `windNodes.test.ts`, `cove3d.test.ts`, `critters3d.test.ts`, `fireflies.test.ts`, `woodlandStage.test.ts`, `woodlandMap.test.ts`, plus additions to `sightMaterials.test.ts` (every new material, no sun code in a world without one), `lightNodes.test.ts`, `sightmask.test.ts` (seen trunks), `vegetation.test.ts` (the shadow shape), `runView3d.test.ts`, `params.test.ts`, `panel.test.ts`, `stats.test.ts`. The trees' own tests are listed in §12.

### Ground cover (#330)

Soft cover on the ground of the 3D view: grass in the woodland and in the nymph's cove, moss in some dungeon rooms. Look only: no server or protocol change. The design and the plan are on issue #330. Code: `render3d/cover/`.

**The model.** The ground stays bare. Over it every art pixel (1/16 tile) of the map has a cover height of 0 to 15 art px. The layer draws that height as stacked alpha-tested sheets, one flat quad per cell per sheet, sheet `n` at `n * gh / 16` tiles up; a sheet keeps the art pixels whose height is at least `n`. The pixels are small and dense, so a foot behind them is hidden by the depth test alone: heroes and creatures stand a little in the cover, with no per-figure code.

**The encoding.** The cover image has one RGBA texel per art pixel (`CoverImage`, `map.w * 16` by `map.h * 16`): rgb is the colour, alpha is `encodeCover(h, id)` = `h | id << 4` (0 means no cover; `coverH`, `coverId`). One texture and one set of meshes therefore carry every cover kind, and each pixel keeps its own kind, so moss beside grass keeps its own colours, height, foot shade and floor darkening.
