**Cost** (#364's gate: models minus cards at most 3 ms in every row). `GPU=uncapped KINDS=goblin,orc,necromancer,summoner,disciple,imp,chort,npcKnight node models-cost.mjs http://localhost:5173/ humanoids` (`KINDS` takes model keys; `orc` stands for both orc kinds), `&crowd=40` with the nine as the crowd (372 figures as models, 304 as cards, the model block included), the RTX 3060, a 2560 x 1280 window, measured 2026-11-18 while the Orc Archer still had a model of its own (`orcArcher`, about as many vertices as the orc) against the Vite dev server (not a production build), one run per row, so the numbers are indicative. Median frame time in ms (the means are 99 to 125 ms for every row, cards too, because of the one-second stall every run has, as in #364's table):

| | models / cards | models - cards | figure draws |
|---|---|---|---|
| WebGL2, work light | 7.9 / 7.8 | +0.1 | 90 / 87 |
| WebGL2, `&light=torch` | 7.6 / 7.7 | -0.1 | 87 / 87 |
| WebGPU, work light | 5.8 / 6.7 | -0.9 | 87 / 87 |
| WebGPU, `&light=torch` | 6.5 / 6.1 | +0.4 | 87 / 90 |

Every row is under the 3 ms gate. The conditions differ from #364's table: that one is the median of three runs on a production build, this one is one run per row on the Vite dev server. A first attempt with `GPU=1` (the capped start line) showed 1000 ms frames in every row, so its numbers were discarded: use `GPU=uncapped`.

**Adding a two-legged creature.** As "Adding a tube creature" above, with `stance: 'two'` and `arms` (copy `goblin.ts` for a bare-armed one, `robed.ts` for a robe, `chort.ts` for a hock); if its kind carries a weapon, give the arms an `elbow` and hang the forearm and the fist on the hand bones (see "Holding weapons"; the test of `BIPEDS` asks it of every weapon carrier); give the `legs` list entries with a `lower` where the figure has knees, or no `legs` for a glider; keep every tube point at 0.35 px or more (the check fails otherwise); add the key to `pose3d.test.ts`'s `BIPEDS` and the facts to `BIPEDS` in `tubeModel.test.ts`; add its row to `KINDS` in `models-shots.mjs`.

**Known limitations.** The arms have no elbows. The Knight's shield is look only: it does not move to block. The thickness check covers two-legged descriptions only. The gallery's walker turns as it paces, so a `move` shot has no fixed direction. All the poses and sizes are first guesses for the user's check.

### Ghost and Shade (#359)

The Ghost and the Shade are the 3D models of the two incorporeal ambushers (their behaviour is in §5, "Incorporeal ambushers"; their cards are described under "Ghost and Shade looks" in §9). Like the other models they are **look only and on the dev pages only**: no run draws them (`FigureLook.models` is set by the stages alone), the cards and the Shade's 2D veil (`game/shadeHaze.ts`) stay as they are, and the server's hit circle is unchanged. `MODEL_OF_KIND` gives `KIND.Ghost` the key `ghost` and `KIND.Shade` the key `shade`; both are tube descriptions (`TUBE_DESCS`, `TubeKey`), so they use `tubeModel.ts`, the pose of `pose3d.ts` and the layer of `layer.ts` like the two-legged nine, and the look sits in five places only: a `SpectreSpec` on the description, the pose's floating gait (and, for the Shade, floating parts), the material's painted eyes and see-through draw, and the effects (the Shade's smoke body, the Ghost's halo, the swooshes). No file but the description names a creature: `pose3d.ts`, `layer.ts`, `nodes.ts`, `figures.ts` and `fx/` read fields of `FigureModel` (`gait`, `swipe`, `apart`, `spectre`). The Ghost is 1 human tall (its description takes the Knight's `size`, `fitSize(HEIGHT_FACTOR.knight, …)`, through `...npcKnight`; nothing reads `HEIGHT_FACTOR.spectre`); the Shade is not fitted to it: its `size` is 0.84 over `PAGE_TO_GAME`, which puts the top of the smoke's hump at 14.28 game px against `HUMAN_HEIGHT` 18.

**The two descriptions** (`models/descs/ghost.ts`, `shade.ts`). The Ghost is the Knight NPC's body (`npcKnight.ts` exports `KNIGHT_PIECES`) without the shield and the comb, so it is one build of the same pieces with `line: 0` on every piece (no outline hull). The Shade is a creature of smoke with three kinds of hard part and nothing that joins them: a low head, and two thin bony arms (upper arm, forearm, hand) whose hands carry four long claws and a shorter thumb claw each. The claws are pieces of the hand, tubes of four points whose radius tapers from 0.42 to a 0.03 tip; they measure 1.04 times their nominal length. There is no torso, no neck, no shoulder and no leg piece. Bones are about 0.45 model px in radius and the description never makes a bone or a claw thinner than its values, because they break up at game size. Every piece is `glow: true` and near black (`0x030205`) with `line: 0`, so no lamp shows its shape and it has no outline hull: at game size it reads as a shifting black cloud with three sharp things in it, the claws, the head and the two eyes. The body is a `SmokeSpec` on `spectre.smoke`, drawn by the effects (below); its middle line runs from the hump (17 model px of the drawing times 0.84, so about 14.3 game px high, plus the hover) down a tapering tail that stops short of the ground. `SMOKE_TONES` (`tubeDesc.ts`) holds the second tone that is mixed into the black: `grey` (the description's own) and `purple` (kept for a stronger Shade later; only the gallery's `&tone=purple` reaches it). Neither has a face, a nose, fangs, a weapon, a ground oval, a lamp caster or a sun shadow (the layer leaves the sun draw out of a model with `spectre`, and `figures.ts` skips the shadow and the oval for it). `SpectreSpec` (`tubeDesc.ts`) is what makes a description a spectre: `seeThrough?: { peak }` (the Ghost; `peak` is the instance alpha that shows the approved look, `GHOST_ALPHA_MAX`), `eyes: EyeSpec`, `smoke?: SmokeSpec` (the Shade: `line`, the middle line as [x forward, y up, half depth, half width] points; `tone`; `on`, the points on bones that carry thin smoke, each with a bone, a place, a radius, a share of the wisps and a rise factor), `halo?` (the Ghost's glow, below) and `swoosh: { colour, head? }`. `TubeDesc.float: true` (two-legged descriptions only) selects the floating gait and `swipe: true` lets a melee swing the arm (the Ghost). `TubeDesc.apart: true` (the Shade; it needs `float`, explicit pivots for `armR`, `handR` (the elbow), `armL`, `handL` and no `arms` entry; `check` refuses anything else) makes the parts float apart (below). A description with a `smoke` entry has its height measured to the hump's top (without the hover), so `baseHeight` and the line-up reach it, and `hitRadius` reads the smoke's middle line (the box of x by the half depth and z by the half width) where it reads a torso piece for other bodies (the floor of 4 px is expected to hold, as for every upright body so far; the server's circle itself is set only after the user has approved looks and sizes). `floats(kind)` in `table.ts` tells the gallery's `ent()` to give a kind that floats the incorporeal flag, as a run does.

**The floating gait** (`pose3d.ts`, `FLOAT`, `FigureModel.gait: 'float'`). Nothing steps and nothing moves in time with the speed: a floating model does not read `mem.walk` or `mem.phi` (`floating` sets `w` and `phi` to 0). It hovers `FLOAT.hover` 1.5 px above the ground and bobs on three slow sines of the clock (`FLOAT.bob`, each offset by the entity's own phase `mem.phase`, set from `ghostPhase(id)`, so a group is out of step). The legs, the arms and the head trail behind a move by `mem.drag`, and the whole body leans at the hips (`mem.lean`, the bone `hips`); both follow the speed late (`FLOAT.rate` 3 per second) and are capped (`FLOAT.drag.max`, `FLOAT.lean.max`). The hover is in game px and is not scaled by the model, like the lunge. A hit leans the hips (`FLOAT.hitLean`). A model with `swipe` raises the arm its attack uses (`elbowArms` with the new `limp` argument: the used arm blends from the limp turn, the other arm hangs) and twists the body into the blow; without `swipe` both arms stay limp.

**Floating parts** (`pose3d.ts`, `APART`; `FigureModel.apart`). For a model whose parts float apart the hover is `APART.hover` 2 px (the bob stays `FLOAT.bob`). The head and each arm drift on their own, a fraction of a px to half a px (`APART.head`, `APART.arm`: an amplitude, a rate and a share of the entity's phase per axis; the second arm runs `APART.arm.phase` out of step), and nothing is shaken: a wind-up does not shiver such a model (the shiver would shake the smoke), and a sleeper does not lie down but hangs lower with the head drooped (`APART.asleep`). The drift's peak is about 0.67 px. The blow is made of three curves of the clock in units of `MELEE_T` (`APART.time`): the **wind** (`mem.wind`) takes the right arm up and back and turns and lifts the body while the server's wind-up runs, and follows the animation continuously; the **swing** (the sweep, held to `hold` and back by `back`) carries the claw across the front inside the game's swing, so it crosses when the damage lands; the **reach** (lasting `reach` units, `reachPow`) lunges the root, pushes the head forward, flings the other arm back (`APART.other`) and lets the arm's root go further out than a jointed arm could. The pose memory (`mem.blowT`, `mem.wind`, `mem.carry`, `windStart`, `prevAnim`) lets the parts drift back to their places after the swing ends, over about 0.8 s, and a blow that starts during the drift back starts from where the parts are: the carry of the swing is taken whenever a wind-up begins and on a melee that no wind-up preceded, so no bone moves by more than 1 px between the frames around a new blow's start. The elbow is a bone of its own (`handR`, `handL` hold the elbow's pivot): the forearm bends against the upper arm in the wind-up and the blow. The ambush forming draws the parts in from the point `APART.inside` inside the smoke as `form` goes from `APART.form[0]` to `APART.form[1]`.

**The eyes** (`nodes.ts`, `eyeMask`, `EYE`; `EyeSpec` in `tubeDesc.ts`). The eyes are not pieces: the material paints two ovals, mirrored in z, slanted by the description's `slant` (the Ghost's slant down toward the middle), on the front of the head from each vertex's rest position, so the pose and the skinning move them with the head and they cannot be a separate piece or a glow between or under the eyes. A vertex counts when its bone is `head` or `hat` and its rest x is at least `EyeSpec.front`. `core` is the bright oval and `halo` a wider glow round it (`EYE.core`, `EYE.halo`, `EYE.glow`). The mask reads the instance's **extra texel** (`ROW.extra` 53 in `instances.ts`; `FigureInst.flare` 0..1 widens and brightens the eyes, `FigureInst.eye` 0..1 is how much shows, default 1): every other row (a weapon, a skull, an object, every existing figure) packs flare 0 and eye 1, and a material built without eyes does not read the texel (`figExtra` is not emitted). A melee swing sets `flare` to `sin(min(1, FLARE_RATE * animT / MELEE_T) * PI)` (`figures.ts`, `FLARE_RATE` 1.6); the ambush forming sets `eye` (below); a dying figure's copy (the fading image) shuts its eyes. The Ghost's eyes are slanted ovals (`half: [0.6, 0.32]`, `slant: 0.15`) with a pale blue ring (`0x99ccff`) that makes the halo. The Shade's are two small round pale beads (`half: [0.22, 0.22]`, `slant: 0`, core `0xe6ecf2`) with a black `ring`, so no halo shows, on a head front that begins at `front: 3.6`: the only bright things in its face. A stone (petrified) figure shows no eyes (`select(isStone, ...)`).

**The see-through draw** (the Ghost; `nodes.ts` `seeMaterials`, `SEE`, `SEE_DEPTH_ORDER` 3.1, `SEE_ORDER` 3.2; `layer.ts`). A blended body that shows its inside and far side through itself looks like a heap of overlapping parts, so the Ghost is drawn twice: a **depth-only draw** (colour write off, depth write on) and then a **blended colour draw** (no depth write, one pass) that passes only where its depth equals the first one's, so each pixel blends the nearest surface of the figure once. Both draws take the same position node, so their depths can be equal, and both gate on one value, `finalAlpha` (the colour draw's alpha, 0 where the figure is cut below the ground): the depth draw keeps a fragment exactly when the colour draw does (`SEE.gate` 0.02). The depth material is `transparent: true`: three draws every non-transparent material first, in the opaque list, so an opaque depth draw would come before the opaque scene; as a transparent material it sorts by render order with the cards (3), after the opaque scene and the cards: the order is the opaque scene, the cards, the see depth draw 3.1, the see colour draw 3.2, then the glows (4) and the effects (5). The colour is the body's own light: no lamp and no ambient, a tone mixed from the paint's luminance and the height (`SEE.dark`, `SEE.light`, `toneLum`, `toneHeight`), whitened toward the rim, with alpha `SEE.solid` plus the rim term `SEE.rim * f^rimPow` (`f` = 1 - |N . ray|) times a foot fade (`SEE.foot`, `footEdge`) times the instance alpha over `peak` times what the hero sees (`sight.shown`; the sight step still applies to the colour). The row's `dark` (an entity in the dark, `FigureLook.dark`) reaches this material only through the alpha: in the fade mode `figures.ts` multiplies the body's alpha by the fade; in the ambient mode the material does not read the row's dark flag, so a self-lit Ghost stays bright, as every `glow` piece does. The eyes are drawn in this material (`figureMaterials` gets `eyes: null` for a see-through model). The meshes of the see entries are on `AIR_SKIP_LAYER` (the translucent layer), so the shafts' depth pre-pass, the water mirror and a pool's scene copy leave them out. **Entries:** the layer groups instances by `key` or `key/see`; an instance of a model with `spectre.seeThrough` that is not `stone` goes to the `<key>/see` entry (its materials are made on first use and kept in `ModelRes.see`, and are disposed with the model), every other instance to the ordinary entry. A frame with a petrified Ghost and a normal one has both entries, one instance each. An entry not used in a frame has `instanceCount` 0 and `visible` false, so a frame without a Ghost after one with Ghosts draws no see mesh (the layer test "a frame without figures after one with a Ghost hides both see meshes"); after a lost device the new view's layer builds its entries again and the Ghost is drawn again (`lose-ghost-*` in `models-shots.mjs`). **The stone case:** a petrified Ghost is drawn solid in the stone paint by the ordinary piece draw; its pulse would only stipple it into a dot pattern. `figures.ts` replaces only the pulse: `pose.alpha` is the Ghost's alpha (pulse, or 0 in the ambush's blink-off phase) times the other fades (appearing, falling), so a petrified see-through model whose definition has a `pulse` divides `ghostAlpha` out of it and keeps the other fades (a see-through kind without a pulse is left as it is); a blinked-off Ghost stays 0. An invisible Ghost is not in `pose.alpha`: the layer scales an instance by what the hero sees.

**What the model path changes** (`figures.ts`). `spectral(d)` gives `figurePose` the definition with `incorporealAlpha: 1` and no silhouette in the ambush (the model's own look carries both); the model's body alpha is `pose.alpha * k` times `bodyForm`. A spectre makes no sun shadow and no ground oval. A model with `spectre.smoke` forms out of its smoke during the ambush warning (`ANIM.Ambush`): `form` = `animT / CONST.AMBUSH_WARNING` (0..1; `DrawnFig.form`), the smoke gathers as a low pool on the floor and rises into the body, the body's alpha (which fades the hard parts in) is the smoothstep of `form` between 0.45 and 0.95 (`SPECTRE_FORM.body`), the eyes open between 0.8 and 1 (`SPECTRE_FORM.eyes`), and the smoke draws from `form`. The Ghost blinks in the warning through `ghostAlpha(clock, phase, animT)`, in 0.06 s steps.

**The smoke** (`fx/smoke.ts`, `Smoke`, `SMOKE`; `fx/status.ts`, `SMOKES`, `SMOKE_FRAME`, `smokePlace`). A figure whose `spectre.smoke` is set has a body of smoke that the effects pass draws, in two layers of camera-facing patches. The **core** is dense near-black patches fixed to points along the middle line (`SMOKE.core.points` 13, with a pair beside each point where the body is wider than `pairOver` 2 px to a side) and to the `on` points of the spec (the head's back, the arm sockets), so the smoke hides what is behind it and each arm comes out of smoke, not clear air. The **wisps** (`SMOKE.wisps` 340 at full detail, scaled by the effects' `detail`; life 1.4 to 3 s) are born on the rim of a core point picked by weight, drift slowly outward (0.3 to 0.8 px/s) with only a faint rise (the top 30 % of the line rise 1.5 times as fast) and fade, 45 % staying black and the others taking 25 to 85 % of the second tone as they age; they are drawn 2.2 times as tall as wide, so nothing is round. Thin smoke runs along the arms (the `on` points of the bones) and the claws are clear of it. The noise clock runs at half the game clock (`SMOKE.clock`).

*Following.* A core point follows its place on the body at a rate that goes from 30 per second at the top to 12 down the tail (`SMOKE.rate`), the tail hanging back a little more; a wisp moves with the body by 0.88 of each step (`SMOKE.follow`) plus its own drift and takes no speed from the body. A wisp trails at most about 15 px behind a Shade moving at 44 px/s and rises at most about 3.2 px above the hump. When the Shade stops the smoke stays where it is and fades: no wisp or core point is ever further along the old direction than its place on the standing body. `smokePlace` turns a bone point of the pose into a world point (the bone -1 stands for the root, `hips`).

*State.* The state per Shade id is in `SMOKES` (`status.ts`): a `Smoke`, its clock and its last place, alpha, tone and pose. It restarts at the figure's place (`settle`, no streak across the room) when the figure was unseen for more than `SMOKE_FRAME.gap` 3 s, moved more than `SMOKE_FRAME.jump` 1 tile in one frame, or the clock went back by more than `SMOKE_STEP.rewind`. The step is capped at `SMOKE_STEP.max` 0.1 s and a `dt` of 0 repeats the picture. The smoke's own clock runs on in death and stands still only for a petrified or frozen Shade (`rateOf`: asleep runs at half speed), so such a Shade's smoke quads are identical in two frames a second apart. The step is made from the last step (`stepped`), scaled by `rateOf`: a sleeping Shade's smoke moves and ages at half speed, a petrified one's not at all, and a Shade that returns while its smoke was fading is stepped once for the time gone, not twice. A figure gone from the frame (out of view, or faint below `SMOKE_STEP.minAlpha`) leaves its smoke fading for `SMOKE_FRAME.fade` 0.3 s; during that time it is stepped on a held place (the pose it last had) so it keeps moving as it thins. The smoke's alpha is the figure's alpha times what the hero sees. The ambush's `form` makes the **forming**: the core points gather as a low pool of radius `SMOKE.pool.r` 5 px on the floor and rise into the body (`SMOKE.pool`), the parts carry smoke as their forming alpha rises. The drawer orders the Shades far to near.

*Death.* A figure that a `ShadeVeil` record follows is left to the record (`Effects3d.following(kind, id)`). On `Died` the record (`drawVeilDeath`) draws the smoke of that id, if there is one, spreading outward (`SMOKE.death.spread` 6 px/s) and swelling (`SMOKE.death.grow`) while it thins over `VEIL_DEATH_LIFE`, from the alpha it had alive, so a part-formed Shade does not jump to full darkness; without a smoke it draws the card's veil as before. A Shade that died out of sight (no smoke of its own for it), in a session that has drawn smoke, gets a settled **resting smoke** at the record's spot (`restingSmoke`, built once per record, from the look of the last smoke drawn) that spreads and thins the same way. `clearVeils()` forgets every veil and smoke (tests and the gallery's start and end).

*Drawing.* `Smoke.write` puts quads (two triangles each, a corner coordinate per vertex) into a `SmokeBuf` (`out.smoke` of the frame's `FxOut`; `FxPass.smoke`; at most `FX_SMOKE_CAP` 32000 triangles, 43 Shades of 728; the rest are dropped, nearest last, and counted in `FxPass.droppedTotal`; `FxPass.triCount`, and so the F3 line, includes the smoke's triangles). `FrameInput.fx.smoke` carries the buffer to the view, which uploads it with the sight data (`smokeUpload` in `sightAttrs.ts`) into its own dynamic mesh, `smokeDyn`, and draws nothing when the count is 0 (a frame without a Shade after one that had some, also after a lost device). The material is `smokeMaterial` in `materials.ts` (`SMOKE_SHAPE`): unlit, translucent, depth tested and not written, double sided, its alpha a falloff from the patch's centre bent by slow noise so it is never a disc, torn into upright streaks by a second, stretched noise (`mx_noise_float`, which compiles in WebGL2 and WebGPU). The mesh's render order is `SMOKE_ORDER` 4.9: after the hard parts and the glows (4), before the effect triangles (5), so swooshes and status marks draw over it. It is not on the id pass, and it is skipped by the water mirror, the shafts' pre-pass and a pool's scene copy (`skipInAirPrepass`), as the card Shade's veil is. The Shade's hard parts are tested against the same depth, so the core can hide the far arm, the eyes from behind or a claw; nothing is cut out of the smoke to show them. A frame's tone is the description's (`SpectreSpec.smoke.tone`) unless the gallery passes one in `FxPassIn.smokeTone` (`FxCtx.smokeTone`), which `&tone=purple` sets.

*The hard parts on the translucent layer.* The layer puts the piece meshes of a model with `spectre.smoke` on the translucent layer (`see.layer`, not the id draw), so no head or claws without a body show in water's mirror or a pool.

**Swoosh, halo, light** (`fx/figureFx.ts`, `models/fx.ts`). A melee swoosh of a spectre has the colour of `spectre.swoosh.colour` (the Ghost's pale blue-white, the Shade's black) and, with `head`, a leading edge that mixes from the base colour toward `head` over the last `SWOOSH_HEAD_WIDTH` 0.25 of the arc (the Shade's blue-white head on a black blade, `swooshTris(..., head)`). The Ghost's **halo** (`halo` in `figureFx.ts`, `SpectreSpec.halo`): a soft glow quad on the body (`size`, `height` in px, colour `glow` times `k`; it is drawn with the figure, not lifted toward the camera) and a light on the floor (`addLight`, colour `light`, `radius`, `LIGHT_KIND.Magic`, at `HALO.lightUp` 5 px above the foot), both scaled by the figure's alpha over `peak`; a petrified, hidden or faint (alpha under `HALO.minAlpha` 0.05) figure has none. The Shade has none: it is a hole in the light.

**The spectre block and `&at=spectre:<i>`** (`galleryStage.ts`, `galleryMap.ts`). The gallery's last block, behind its own wall row, `SPECTRE_PLACES` 6 places `SPECTRE_PITCH` 6 tiles apart, ids `ID.spectre` 4800 `+ place`: places 0 to 2 the Ghost, 3 to 5 the Shade; in each trio the first stands (idle), the second walks (the gallery's `walk` sway, 1.5 tiles at 2 rad/s) and the third is the **life figure**, which plays a loop of `SPECTRE_LIFE` 9 s (`lifeState`): it forms in the ambush warning (`CONST.AMBUSH_WARNING`, 0.5 s), stands idle, winds up `SPECTRE_WINDUP` 0.25 s before each blow at 3 s and 5 s and swings `SPECTRE_BLOW` 0.5 s, and is gone from `SPECTRE_GONE` 7 s (a `Died` event is sent at that second, `spectreDiedBetween`; the first frame looks back `CATCH_UP` 1 s so a page started just after a death shows the death's smoke) until the loop repeats. There is one torch over every place (the block's wall row, `ID.pillarTorch + 20 + place`; a Shade stands in front of a lit wall and shows as a hole in it). The gallery owns an `Effects3d` and an `FxPass` that run over the figures of every Ghost and Shade drawn as a model, wherever it stands (`spectrePassIn`: a drawn figure with `spectre` set, so the kind rows, the effect rows' columns 12 and 13, the dying row, the line-up, the crowd and the block all have the smoke, halo, floor light, swoosh and death effects; no other kind reaches the pass, so no other block changes), and the pass's lights join the stage's light list; the dying row's `Died` events of the two spectre kinds also go to the effects, and so do the `Died` events of the liquids page (`liquidsStage.ts`); the pass's blended and lit triangles also take the `&circles=1` rings. `&at=spectre:<i>` puts the camera over place `i` (0 to 5); `&zoom=8` fills the window with one figure and `&zoom=4` shows a trio. `&tone=purple` draws every Shade's smoke in the purple tone (`parseGallery`'s `smokeTone`, handed to the pass as `FxPassIn.smokeTone`; any other value leaves the description's tone). The model kinds in the rows are 24 and the line-up has 24 keys at pitch 2.0 (the Shade is place 11). **Check shots** (`models-shots.mjs`; `ONLY='^spectre-'` takes them alone): `spectre-trio-<ghost|shade>-<work|torch>`, `spectre-place-0..5`, `spectre-life-<ghost|shade>-<s>` (ghost 0.21, 0.45, `blinkoff-0.4`, 3.1, 7.3; shade 0.15, 0.3, 0.47 (the pool, the rising body, the parts and eyes arriving), 2.9 (the wind-up), 3.08 and 3.18 (the sweep and its end), 3.6 (the drift back), 7.1 and 7.6 (the death)), `spectre-close-<kind>-<front|side>-<work|torch>` (zoom 16; the Shade also `side2` and `back`, aims 180 and 270), `spectre-shade-gamesize` (zoom 2), `spectre-shade-purple`, `spectre-shade-walk-t1.7` and `-t2.2` (the walker 0.5 s apart), `spectre-torch-<kind>-walk`, `spectre-effects-<asleep|stone|frozen|poisoned|invisible>`, `spectre-liquids-<kind>-<wade|chasm|lava>`, `spectre-lineup-<4|11|17>`, and `lose-ghost-before` / `lose-ghost-after` and `lose-shade-before` / `lose-shade-after` (a lost device with a Ghost or a Shade in view; the run fails if the draws differ before and after). The gallery sets `window.__r3d.fx` (`records`, `tris`, `dropped`: the spectre pass's counts, the shape a run's and the effects page's have); every `spectre-` shot fails when `dropped` is over 0 (a smoke cut off at `FX_SMOKE_CAP`), and so does each row of `models-cost.mjs`. The liquids page sets no `__r3d.fx`, so its shots are not checked this way.

**Cost** (the gate is models minus cards at most 3 ms; it is reported, not enforced, and the smoke's wisp count and noise are never cut to meet it). `GPU=uncapped KINDS=ghost,shade node models-cost.mjs http://localhost:4175/ spectres`: `&crowd=40` with the Ghost and the Shade as the crowd (about 440 figures as models, about 400 as cards), the RTX 3060 (the script prints the adapter: WebGL2 on ANGLE over Vulkan on the NVIDIA card, WebGPU `nvidia ampere`; it fails on a software renderer), a 2560 x 1280 window, measured 2026-11-19 on a **production build** (`npm run build`, served by `vite preview`), three runs one after the other. The machine was not idle (load average 4.0, 9.0 and 9.0 at the starts of the runs; the GPU 0 %, 0 % and 1 % busy; the load was sampled only there). The effects pass covers every model Ghost and Shade on the page, so the models rows carry the smoke of the Shades in the crowd (728 triangles each) plus the Ghosts' halos and floor lights; the cards rows still carry the effects of the model spectres outside the crowd (the kind rows, the effect columns, the dying row, the line-up and the block). The script does not count the Shades in view; the effect triangles below are the measure. The script reads the pass's counts after each row and fails when it dropped any triangle at its caps: in all 24 rows the effect triangles were 17.9 to 18.5 thousand for the models rows and 7.4 to 8.0 thousand for the cards rows, and **0 were dropped** (`FX_SMOKE_CAP` is 32000). Median frame time in ms, models / cards, then the difference (the figure draws are 102 in every row, models and cards):

| | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 | models - cards, the three runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WebGL2, work light | 25.5 / 25.2 | 26.4 / 40.1 | 25.5 / 19.8 | +0.3, -13.7, +5.7 |
| WebGL2, `&light=torch` | 33.7 / 38.5 | 45.3 / 24.1 | 25.9 / 20.7 | -4.8, +21.2, +5.2 |
| WebGPU, work light | 29.9 / 19.5 | 31.9 / 21.0 | 25.6 / 19.6 | +10.4, +10.9, +6.0 |
| WebGPU, `&light=torch` | 30.4 / 25.2 | 31.7 / 20.1 | 25.5 / 19.6 | +5.2, +11.6, +5.9 |

The means (models rows 26 to 51 ms) and the 99th percentiles (models rows 40 to 146 ms, most of them 40 to 55 ms; the cards rows reach 104 ms in the noisy runs) come from the same runs. The difference is positive in 10 of the 12 pairs, from +0.3 to +21 ms, and 5 to 6 ms in every row of the quietest run (run 3); in the other two pairs the cards row was the slower one, and the runs do not show why. The runs do not separate the smoke from the Ghosts' halos and floor lights, so none of the difference is assigned to one of them. The 3 ms gate is not met on this page, where the crowd's 40 figures are all Ghosts and Shades; no run draws models yet.

**Reading.** The gate is **not shown to hold** with about 20 Shades in view: in 11 of the 12 comparisons of the medians (three runs of four rows) the models rows are above the cards rows by more than 3 ms, by 4.5 to 31.9 ms (only WebGPU with `&light=torch` in run 2, +1.4 ms, is inside it). The runs differ a good deal, because the machine was not idle (the cards rows of the same row are 18.6 to 26.7 ms on WebGPU and 20.9 to 38.3 ms on WebGL2), so the sizes are not to be quoted closer than that; the clearest rows are WebGPU with the work light, where the cards rows are steady at 19 ms and the models rows are 30 to 51 ms. The cause is the smoke: about 20 Shades are about 15 000 blended triangles of the one smoke mesh, each with two noise lookups per fragment, and the Shades overlap one another in the crowd, so the fill is far more than their count suggests; the Ghosts' halos and floor lights are part of the difference but were not measured apart. There are no extra figure draws (102 in every row). **What to do about it is the user's decision**: the wisp count (`SMOKE.wisps`) and the noise are the levers, and they are the look, so they were not changed. A run has one or two Shades, so a run's cost is far below the crowd's; the frame budget of a run is to be checked when runs draw models.

**Tests.** `nodes.test.ts` (the see-through states, orders and constants; the eye mask compiles and reads `figExtra` only with eyes; the pair compiles), `layer.test.ts` (the see entry, a petrified and a normal Ghost in one frame, a frame without Ghosts hides the see meshes, also after a lost device), `instances.test.ts` (rows without the new fields pack flare 0 and eye 1), `pose3d.test.ts` (the floating gait and the floating parts: the blow's curves, no bone moving more than 1 px around a second blow's start; the wolf's and the two-legged #338 pose tables are unchanged), `figures.test.ts` (the model path: no shadow, forming, flare, the petrified Ghost), `fx/smoke.test.ts` (the smoke's maths: following, nothing overshooting on a stop, restart, forming, a standing clock), `fx/status.test.ts` (the per-Shade state, a jump and a gap, the smoke of a Shade that left the frame, the death, `smokeTone`, a petrified or frozen Shade's identical quads), `fx/figureFx.test.ts` (swoosh, halo), `galleryStage.test.ts` (the block, its life loop and torches, `&tone=purple`, and `spectrePassIn`: a model Shade of any row gets smoke, no other kind reaches the pass), `descs.test.ts` and `tubeModel.test.ts` (the descriptions: the Shade's parts, claws, pivots and the `apart` checks), `layer.test.ts` (a smoke body's piece mesh is on the translucent layer only), `sightAttrs.test.ts` (`smokeUpload` gives 0 corners for no buffer or no triangle, so the mesh draws nothing), `fx/pass.test.ts` (the smoke buffer is cleared each frame and its dropped count joins the pass's total). The server's hit circle for the Shade is not changed yet: `hitRadius` reads the smoke's line, and the list of kinds whose circle is still pending is in `tubeModel.test.ts`.

**Known limitations.** A see-through figure hides effects and other see-through figures behind it: its depth is written before its colour, so what is drawn after it (the glows, the effects, another Ghost) fails the depth test where the Ghost is. It has no reflection in water (the mirror leaves the translucent layer out). A petrified Ghost has no outline (it is an ordinary figure with `line: 0`). The Shade's smoke and hard parts have no reflection in water (they stay out of the mirror, the shafts' pre-pass and a pool's scene copy). The smoke is not sorted against the other effect triangles: it draws before them (`SMOKE_ORDER`), so a swoosh or status mark is always over it, and a see-through figure (a Ghost) behind a Shade's smoke is hidden by its core. The smoke's cost grows with the number of Shades in view (about 740 blended triangles each at full detail). The numbers (`FLOAT`, `APART`, `SMOKE`, `SMOKE_SHAPE`, `SEE`, `EYE`, `SPECTRE_FORM`) are first values for the user's eye. No run shows the models yet; the server's hit circle is unchanged until the user has approved looks and sizes.

### Object models: chest, Mimic, barrel (#317)

The treasure chest, the Mimic and the explosive barrel are 3D models built from descriptions in code (`models/descs/chest.ts`, `mimic.ts`, `barrel.ts`), not from their sprites. Look only: no change to the server, the protocol, `client/src/generated` or a fingerprint test. **No run shows them yet:** only the dev pages choose them (below); a run draws the cards until #355 switches models on. They are `TubeDesc`s in `TUBE_DESCS` (`TubeKey` gained `chest`, `mimic`, `barrel`), so the builder, the kit, instancing, hulls, the id pass, the lamp outline frames (`model_chest`, `model_mimic`, `model_barrel`; run `npm run atlas`) and the sight all work as for the creatures. The design and the plan are on issue #317.

**The prism** (`prism` and `welded` in `models/tubes.ts`; `Shape` gained `{ prism: { outline, z: [z0, z1] } }`, `meshOf` in `tubeModel.ts`). `prism(outline, z0, z1)` pushes a simple polygon in the x-y plane (counter-clockwise from +z, not necessarily convex; triangulated with three's `ShapeUtils`) along z. The edges are hard: every side face and both caps have their own points and one normal, uv is null. A prism cannot be painted (`check` rejects a prism with a function colour) and cannot sway. A piece with `line > 0` gets a hull as every tube does, but the hull of a prism is built from `welded(mesh)`: points at the same position (to 1e-4) share the normalised sum of their normals, because a hull grown along hard-edge normals tears open at the edges. At a box corner the welded normal is diagonal, so the outline stands `line / sqrt(3)` off each face there, not `line`. The barrel is an ordinary tube and uses no prism.

**What is built** (game px; one description px is one game px, so `size` is `1 / PAGE_TO_GAME`; x is the front, y up, z the width).
- **Chest** (`chest.ts`; `CHEST`, `HINGE`, `chestShell`). 14 wide (z), 9 deep (x), 9 high: a base 5.5 high (`CHEST.base`), a domed lid rising 3.5 (`CHEST.rise`) on a hinge at the back of the rim (`HINGE` [-4.5, 5.5, 0], the `head` bone's pivot). The base is a trough with 1 px walls and end plates; the lid is an arch (a band between two half-ellipses) with end plates; both are hollow, with a lining (`0x38262d` in the chest, the colour of the seams) so an open chest is an inside you look into. Wood `0xee8e2e` (lid top), `0xc56025` (sides), `0x8f4029` (the base's end walls; the lid's end plates are `0xc56025`); gold `0xfacb3e` straps front and back, a lock with a keyhole ball; dark seams (`0x38262d`) round the base and across the lid. The loot is on the `hat` bone: a gold heap and three gems (`0xffad3b`, `0x97da3f`, `0x72d6ce`, read from the pack frame `chest_full_open_anim_f2`). `footprint` [4.5, 7]. Stages: wood and linings are `body` (base) and `head` (lid); **all trim, gold and seams on the base and on the lid, is stage `collar`**, because one stage gives one outline rule (the gold gets one outline above and below the lid line) and trim on the lid stage would have raised `baseHeight` (the `head` stage's top) above 9.
- **Mimic** (`mimic.ts`). `chestShell(0x5f2d56)`: the same shell with a red-violet lining, so the lining is the only difference to the chest (the hiding Mimic must not be told from a chest); plus 11 teeth in the stage `spikes` (6 on the base, 5 on the lid, `0xfdf7ed`, short tubes) and a tongue (`0xdc4a7b`, a flat tube on the `hat` bone with `bend: { sway: 1 }`, foot at `TONGUE_AT` [2.5, 1.5, 0]) behind the lid.
- **Barrel** (`barrel.ts`). One painted tube, 12.8 wide (radius 6.4 at the belly, 5.3 at both ends) and 15 high with a wooden head on the `body` bone: a low dome ball (`0xee8e2e`) with three seam prisms (`HEAD_SEAMS`, stage `collar`, no hull) that follow the dome, so it reads as four planks; `footprint` [6.4, 6.4]. `barrelPaint(u, d)`: iron hoops `0x7b8994` at `u` 0.2 to 0.27 and 0.68 to 0.75, a red band `0xda4e38` at 0.42 to 0.56, staves elsewhere (`0xc56025`, seams `0x38262d`). The paint's `d` runs from the top (0) to the underside (1) and mirrors on the other half, so the stave rule is `((d * 4 + 0.125) % 1) < 0.25`: 8 even staves round, seams of exactly 2 of the 64 paint rows (`objects.test.ts` counts them). (A rule written for `d` running once round the tube would give too few staves.)

**Sizes and the server's circle.** The built height (`objectHeight` in `figures.ts`: `round(stands * scale)`, every non-hull point) is 9 for the chest and the Mimic and 15 for the barrel (lid top 15.4). The chest's and the Mimic's `baseHeight` (the top of the `head` stage only) is 9, which `objects.test.ts` pins. The server's circle is 6 for all three (`CHEST_RADIUS`, `BARREL_RADIUS`, the Mimic's `radius`), and `hitRadius(desc)` gives 6 for each, which `tubeModel.test.ts` pins in its literal of every key (`chest: 6, mimic: 6, barrel: 6`) and `objects.test.ts` checks again. A size change that breaks this is not allowed without changing the server.

**The table.** The objects are not creatures, so they have their own table in `models/table.ts`: `OBJECT_MODEL` (kind to key: `KIND.Chest` to `chest`, `KIND.Mimic` to `mimic`, `KIND.Barrel` to `barrel`) and `objectModelOf(kind)`. `MODEL_OF_KIND` stays creature-only (a test checks that the object kinds are absent from `MODEL_OF_KIND` and that `objectModelOf` of a creature kind is null). The kits build models on first use (`kit.get`), so no list of keys names them.

**The pose** (`models/objectPose3d.ts`, pure; the 2D-side state is `game/objectPose.ts`). `poseObject(model, input, out?)` writes one `Pose3d` from `ObjectPoseIn`: `lid` (rad about the hinge; `rz` of the `head` bone), `hat` (the scale of the `hat` bone: the chest's loot, the Mimic's tongue; 0 hides it), `lift` (px above the ground), `sx`/`sy` (squash and stretch, both ways along the ground and the height: the root's `sx`, `sz` and `sy`, as for the creatures), `pitch` (rad, the front up; `rz` of the `body` bone), `ahead` and `side` (px along and across the facing: the root's x and z), `sway` (the tongue's bend) and `yaw`. A non-finite input counts as its rest value (`REST_OBJECT`). `newPose` was exported from `pose3d.ts` for it. `game/objectPose.ts` gained `chestLid(opened)` (the lid's opening, 0 to 1, a smoothstep over the 0.3 s of the opening), `chestLoot(opened)` (the loot is there until `EMPTY_AFTER`) and three fields on `MimicPose` that the card path ignores: `lid` (0 shut to 1 jaws wide), `lunge` (px along the aim, the bite) and `pitch` (degrees). The chest: `chestPoseIn(opened)`, lid `chestLid * CHEST_OPEN` (110 degrees). The Mimic: `mimicPoseIn(pose, hiding, yaw, clock)`, lid `pose.lid * MIMIC_GAPE` (75 degrees), the tongue `smooth(lid / 0.3)` once awake (0 while hiding), a sway of `0.25 * sin(3 * clock)`; a hiding Mimic shivers sideways (`side` = the pose's `bx`) where the card shakes. The barrel stands at rest at its own fixed turn, `barrelYaw(id)` (the golden angle times the id, so a row of barrels is not lined up); its sink and fall come from `barrelPose` and the dips as for the card, only the fuse's flash differs (below).

**Facing.** The chest faces south (`SOUTH`, the lock towards the camera); a hiding Mimic too, so it is the chest. An awake Mimic turns towards its aim at `TURN_RATE` (`turnTo`, the short way round, in `Figures3d`'s per-Mimic memory, which starts south and never takes a non-finite aim). The squash and the lunge follow the facing.

**The path in `figures.ts`.** `builtObject(e)` returns the model when the stage chose models for the entity (`models.is(e)`), `objectModelOf(kind)` has a key for it and the kit has built it; otherwise null and `drawChest`, `drawMimic` and `drawBarrel` draw the card exactly as before. **The chest and the Mimic become models only when both models are built:** for either kind `builtObject` asks the kit for `chest` and `mimic` together (so both builds start with the first object of either kind) and returns a model only when both are there. Chests are seen long before a Mimic, and a hiding Mimic drawn as a card (tag height 16) among model chests (height 9) could be told from a chest. The barrel only needs its own model. **The instances carry `scenery` like their cards:** `FigureInst.scenery` is true for the chest and the barrel and for the Mimic while it hides (false awake), as the three card literals set it; `figureSight` and `hiddenByPicture` in `sightAttrs.ts` give a scenery instance `hard` 0 (and leave it out of the hidden things) on a daylight mask, so props stay shown out of sight on daylight maps as their cards do (`cardShown`); on a dungeon map they are hidden like anything else. One difference from the card is left for #355: a card keeps its real `hard` for the mist (`sight.apply`) and uses `scenery` only for how much of it is drawn, so an out-of-sight card is drawn misted; the instance's `hard` 0 also feeds the mist, so an out-of-sight scenery model is drawn almost unmisted. Card parity needs the real `hard` in the instance row and the scenery bit in the shader's `sight.shown` (`keepOf` in `models/nodes.ts`). `pushObject` writes the pose into the entity's own `Pose3d` (the `objectPoses` map, pruned with the entities) and pushes one `FigureInst` (no weapon bone, never stone, the dark fade as for figures), plus the lamp caster from an outline frame when the entity is shadowed; a barrel that falls is returned as the pieces it is drawn from, as the card is. The decisions, as built:
1. A new shape, `prism`; the barrel is an ordinary tube.
2. The objects have `OBJECT_MODEL`, not `MODEL_OF_KIND`; their descriptions are in `TUBE_DESCS`.
3. Squash and stretch stay: the root of the pose has `sx`, `sy`, `sz`.
4. Hurt: the Mimic model jolts back `MIMIC_JOLT` (1.5 px) along its facing, easing out over `HIT_SECS` (0.26 s), once when the hurt flag rises; there is no white fill. **A hiding Mimic gets no jolt**, because it must not be told from a chest.
5. The barrel's fuse flash is a bright tint, `FUSE_FLASH` [2.2, 2.0, 1.6], on the frames where `barrelPose` flashes (`fill` not null).
6. A smashed chest leaves the snapshot, so nothing is built for it.
7. The bar and the name hang from the built height, `round(stands * scale)` (9 for the chest and the Mimic, 15 for the barrel; `objectAnchor` takes the height, `objectHeight`), and so does the opener's mid; the cards keep `CHEST_HEIGHT` 16.
8. The Mimic's lamp shadow is the frame `model_chest` while it hides and `model_mimic` once awake (`outlineFrame(hiding ? 'chest' : key)`).
9. The model lifts the lid a crack on a rattle (`lid` 0.25 times the rattle's strength, with the pitch); the card never does.

The ground oval and the shadow decal stay the card's (`objShadow`). The instance stands on the entity's foot (the bed under it), and its sight `reach` is the larger of the two `footprint` half-extents times the scale. **A hiding Mimic is a chest in every part** (but the rattle, decision 9, which lifts its lid a crack and pitches it): same shape, size, colours (the lining differs only inside the shell, hidden when shut), facing, lamp shadow, ground oval, tag and bar height; `objects.test.ts`, `objectPose3d.test.ts` and `figures.test.ts` pin it.

**Dev pages.** `?r3d=gallery&focus=models` draws the gallery's object row (the board objects of `ID.object`) as models, 3 tiles apart from x 4 (`OBJECT_PLACES` 7, `GALLERY.object(i)`): place 0 a chest, 1 an opening chest, 2 a barrel, 3 the cycling explosive barrel, 4 a hiding Mimic, 5 a hopping Mimic, 6 a Mimic that reveals itself every 6 s; `&at=object:<i>` puts the camera on place `i`. Without `focus=models` the same row is cards. The size line-up has 24 keys (`LINEUP_MAX`, `LINEUP_PITCH` 2.0 tiles) with the chest, an awake Mimic and the barrel in it (`lineupKind` takes the objects' kinds from `OBJECT_MODEL`). On `?r3d=liquids`, `&models=barrel` draws only the barrel as a model (`LiquidsOpts.barrelModel`), so the chasm and water pushes show it; `&models=<creature key>` draws the barrel as a model too, along with the creature cast.

**Adding an object model.**
1. Write `models/descs/<name>.ts` exporting a `TubeDesc`: game px, `size: 1 / PAGE_TO_GAME`, a `footprint`, no `legs`. Mark the main body piece `torso: true` (a prism or a tube): `hitRadius` throws without one and measures that piece alone. Use `prism` for hard-edged boxes (counter-clockwise outline) and a painted tube for round ones; put all trim in one stage.
2. Register it in `table.ts`: the name in `TubeKey`, the description in `TUBE_DESCS`, the kind in `OBJECT_MODEL`.
3. Set the circle: `hitRadius` must equal the server's radius for the kind; add it to the literal in `tubeModel.test.ts`.
4. Add a pose input in `objectPose3d.ts` if it moves, and the draw path in `figures.ts`, with a branch for the new `e.kind` in the dispatch of `Figures3d` (the chain of `isFigureKind` / `KIND.Chest` / `KIND.Barrel` / `KIND.Mimic`) (copy `drawBarrel`: `builtObject`, `pushObject`, the built height for `objectAnchor`).
5. Run `npm run atlas` for the lamp outline `model_<key>`, and look at it on `?r3d=gallery&focus=models` (add a place to the object row and to the line-up).

**Tests.** `models/tubes.test.ts` (the prism's faces, normals and winding, a concave outline, `welded` joining the hard edges), `models/objects.test.ts` (the three descriptions: the circle 6, finite streams, the same height for chest and Mimic, the Mimic's shell the chest's with its own lining, the hinge, all trim in `collar`, loot, 11 teeth and the tongue, the barrel's size, its paint colours and its 8 even staves, the object table), `models/objectPose3d.test.ts` (`poseObject`: rest, the lid about the hinge, the loot collapsed at `hat` 0, a hiding Mimic equal to the chest, squash, lift, pitch, `ahead` and `side`, non-finite inputs, the sway; `mimicPoseIn`, `turnTo`, `barrelYaw`), `models/modelChecks.testutil.ts` (checks on a built model shared by the model tests: `expectStreams`, `expectFinite`), the cases for `chestLid`, `chestLoot` and the new `MimicPose` fields in `game/objectPose.test.ts`, the object-model cases of `figures.test.ts` (a chest as one instance with its caster, opener and built-height anchor; the hiding Mimic like a chest; an awake Mimic turning to its aim; the jolt once; the barrel's tint, fall and cards when no model is built; the chest and the Mimic as cards unless both models are built; `scenery` on the instances) and of `sightAttrs.test.ts` (a scenery instance stays shown on a daylight map, is hidden on a dungeon map, a figure is hidden on both) and of `objects.test.ts` (no solid body piece inside the cavity of the chest and the Mimic), and of `galleryStage.test.ts` and `liquidsStage.test.ts` (the object row, `&at=object:`, the line-up, `barrelModel`).

### Effects in 3D (#308)

Package 8 of the 3D renderer (#300). The design and the plan are on issue #308. Every effect of the game exists in the 3D run, each at its trigger, place and moment, plus the runes round every magic cast and 3D shapes for the fireball and the frost burst. Everything is runtime shapes (triangles, glows, rings, strokes): no art. Nothing here touches prediction, interpolation or the simulation.

**The system** (`render3d/fx/`). `Effects3d` (`effects3d.ts`) is owned by `RunView3d` (so a lost device keeps it) and by the `?r3d=fx` page. It turns picture events (`RunView3d.event` forwards them after `Figures3d.event`, which keeps the coins and skulls and returns true for a figure lost to a chasm or the deep: then nothing is made), `shotEnded` and `tileChanged` into small records (`FxRec`: kind, start time, place, seed, the entity it follows and four numbers `a` to `d`). Records live `FX_LIFE[kind]` seconds (first guesses; a drawer may end earlier by returning `false`), at most `FX_KIND_CAP` (12) of a kind (the oldest goes first). Events marked `dark` make nothing. Besides the records, the per-frame drawers (`registerFrameFx`) draw what belongs to the drawn figures of the frame: the swoosh and fangs, release sparks, status marks, hazards, the force-field glitter, and they may add records. Drawers register themselves from `FxPass`'s constructor (`registerRunes`, `registerShots`, `registerBursts`, `registerBooms`, `registerFigureFx`, `registerHazards`, `registerField`, `registerStatus`).

**Pure shapes.** A record's picture is a pure function of its age, its seed and its numbers (`rand.ts`: `hash3`, `noise3`; no `Math.random()` in `fx/`), so a frozen clock shows the same picture (`?r3d=fx&t=`). Nothing is stepped per frame except three small things kept on purpose: the card Shade's veil strands (stateful: `VEILS` in `fx/status.ts`), the model Shade's smokes (`SMOKES`, stepped by their frame time) and the memory of each figure's last one-shot action (`Effects3d.actions`, pruned after 3 s unseen, so a spark is made once per action). The shapes (`shapes.ts`: bursts of motes or streaks, flat rings, camera-facing dots, lines, the noise-displaced fire ball, ice crystals, thrown pieces under gravity) are general helpers; #324, #344 and #345 may use them for splashes. They write scalars into a reused `FxTris` (`tris.ts`): nothing is allocated per triangle or frame.

**The passes.** `FxPass` (`pass.ts`) is the glue a run and the dev page share: it fills the context (`FxCtx`: camera, screen axes, clock, `detail`, `ballDetail`), runs `Effects3d.frame` into `FxOut` (`tris`, `lit`, `glows`, `lights`), appends the effect glows to the figures' glow quads before the caller copies them, and sorts the two triangle lists far to near by the view ray (`FxTris.flush`) into two `FxBuf`s that go to `FrameInput.fx = { tris, lit }`. `view.ts` draws them as two dynamic meshes (`fxUpload`), both `renderOrder` 5, depth-tested, not depth-written, skipped by the air prepass: the **blended** one with `fxMaterial` (flat vertex colour, unlit, alpha times the sight) and the **lit** one with `fxLitMaterial` (`materials.ts`: the vertex colour lit like a card by the game's lights and ambient, no card shade, no sun term, alpha times the sight). The per-vertex sight is `fxSightData` (every effect is hidden where the hero cannot see; ids in the run's `hidden` set get no marks and no runes). The glows are the existing additive glow quads. Budgets (`fx/types.ts`, first guesses): `FX_TRI_CAP` 24000 blended and `FX_LIT_CAP` 6000 lit triangles a frame (`FxTris` counts what it refuses in `dropped`), `FX_KIND_CAP`, `FX_LIGHT_CAP` 6 client lights a frame.

**Lit or glowing (closes #108's question, design §7.3).** There is no darkness layer in 3D, so each effect is either lit or glows by itself. *Glows by itself* (blended, unlit, so visible in the dark): everything magical, hot or bright: runes, bolts, fire, frost, sparks, swooshes, fangs, rings, telegraphs, beams, holy and nature motes, status marks, the force field. *Takes the scene's light* (the lit mesh): physical matter: stone chunks, dust, wood chips, smoke, blood and bone motes, vine strands, and the scorch and the frost star on the floor (they darken the lit floor). #108 is answered by that rule.

**Light.** Effect lights enter the same candidate list as the moving lights (`RunView3d` appends `fxPass.out.lights` to `movingCands`, then `selectLights` picks under the existing cap `lcap`), so shadows and the cap work unchanged. Where the server already sends a light (shots, the Blast and Magic flashes, hazards, the breath, Holy Light) the client adds none for the same thing. The server does not light: the runes (the caster's light in the palette colour, id `m<caster>.9` so the carried-light skip of its own shadow applies, strength the runes' summed strength, `LIGHT_KIND.Magic`, radius `LIGHT_RADIUS` in `runes.ts`), the Ring of Fire (`bk`), the Nova (`bg`), the Zap (`zp`, one at each end), the boss death (`dk`) and the Burning mark (`bn`, only inside the cap). They use `addLight` (`booms.ts`) and are refused after `FX_LIGHT_CAP` per frame; the cap is per frame and client-side, on top of `lcap`.

**Quality.** The panel's quality (`c.params.quality`) sets `FxCtx.detail` (High 1, Balanced 0.7, Fast 0.5), which multiplies the counts of bursts and motes (`booms.run`: `round(n * detail)`, at least 1), and `ballDetail` (the fire ball's icosphere: 2, on Fast 1). Tests: `fx/quality.test.ts`. F3 shows a line `fx  <records> rec, <tris> tris (<dropped> dropped total)` (`stats.ts` `fxLine`, from `RunView3d.fxStat`); `window.__r3d.fx` carries the same numbers and `tools/e2e/r3d-run.mjs` counts them (it casts Holy Light and waits for its runes, and wants 0 dropped).

**Runes** (`fx/runes.ts`). Every `magical` ability shows runes (design §4): the server sends `Ev::Cast` (§4 Protocol) and the client colours by `RUNE_PALETTES[look]`. Fire is orange, Cold ice-blue, Arcane magenta, Holy gold, Gloom dark violet, Nature green; a channelling figure (the disciple's Channel, which has no `Cast`) takes its own colour (`channelPalette(FigureDef.base)`) and keeps a slow swirl for as long as its anim is Channel. A cast has a mode (`castMode`): *windup* and *instant* (the sphere round the caster; one rule for both: the runes stand for the wind-up but at least `SPHERE_MIN` 0.3 s, so an instant cast is a wind-up of 0.3 s; the same 16 runes come one by one over the same share of that time, and the sphere turns faster the shorter the cast, `SPIN_REF / time` capped at 4 times; only a real wind-up can be broken off), *primary* (a vortex of about 12 runes facing the camera on a ring in front of the hand, spinning fast, spiralling forward along the path on the release; it pops in 0.2 s before the release, which for a monster's primary with a wind-up (Fire Bolt 0.35 s, Shadow Bolt 0.5 s) is the end of the wind-up, so it leaves with the bolt, and such a cast is broken off like a *windup* one; a hero's primary has no wind-up and releases after 0.2 s) and *channel*. Every rune shows at its full size from its first frame, brightens over `APPEAR` 0.1 s and is whole at least `HOLD` 0.05 s before the release (`runeBorn`), on a short cast as on a long one (the user, #308). Sphere runes are 16 stroke glyphs on a sphere round the caster, twice the size of the vortex's (`SPHERE_SIZE` 2, approved by the user: at the vortex's size they were specks on a short cast); each stroke is a wide faint band plus a thin core, each rune has an additive glow. On the release they flare white and burst outward; if the caster dies, is hidden or leaves the drawn figures, or the cast runs out without a release, they wink out where they are (`WINK`). The counts, sizes and angles are first guesses for the user to judge. **The own hero's cast starts on the key press** (drawing only): the session sets `RunFrame.ownCast` (`OwnCast { seq, look, primary, aim }`, from `ABILITY_RUNES` of the predicted ability, in `RunSession.predictAbility`), the effects start a record for the hero and keep a small fixed queue of pending predicted casts (look and time, at most 8, expiring after 2 s); each server `Cast` for `you` takes the oldest pending cast of its look and draws nothing, so there is exactly one cast even when the server's event is late (decision on #308). `Cast` records have their own cap, `FX_CAST_CAP` 24 (other kinds keep `FX_KIND_CAP` 12): when full, the new cast pushes out the record with the least time left (release plus burst, or the end plus the wink-out), never the own hero's nor an open channel (decision on #308). A refused prediction followed within 2 s by an unpredicted server cast of the same look shows no runes for that one cast.

**Where each effect lives.**

| File | Effects |
|---|---|
| `fx/runes.ts` | the runes and their caster light |
| `fx/shots.ts` | fire bolt (lumpy fire ball, spark trail), frost bolt (ice crystal, dust), every other glowing shot (core, glow, trail; gloom wisps), wall hits (`ShotHit`) |
| `fx/bursts.ts` | the fireball explosion (Boom k 0, 4, 7) and the frost burst (k 10) of design §3; the scorch and the frost star are lit floor marks |
| `fx/booms.ts` | the other booms (Holy Light and Purify rings, Ring of Fire, Ground Slam and Smash, lich Nova, chest, wolf puff, War Cry, Haste), Zap, deaths (ordinary, Ghost, boss), Raise, Immune, Dmg sparks, the shared `addLight` |
| `fx/figureFx.ts` | swoosh and fangs (cards and models alike, using `models/fx.ts`), channel swirl, release sparks, the lich's nova wind-up, slam telegraphs on the floor, dragon breath and bite, stone gaze, lich drain, mimic wake and landing |
| `fx/hazards.ts` | FirePatch, HolyGround, Roots (radius from `aim`, life from `extra`) |
| `fx/status.ts` | the marks of the `effects` bits; the card Shade's veil (dissolved over 1 s after its death by `ShadeVeil`); the model Shade's smoke per id (`SMOKES`: stepping, fading after it leaves the frame, spreading in death, the resting smoke) |
| `fx/smoke.ts` | the smoke body (`Smoke`, `SMOKE`): the core and the wisps as camera-facing quads in a `SmokeBuf` (`FX_SMOKE_CAP`) |
| `fx/field.ts` and `fieldMesh.ts` | the force field |
| `fx/shake.ts` | camera shake |
| `game/fxLooks.ts` | the colours and rhythms of the effects (gloom palette, hazard and entangle looks, raise looks, the Accelerated tint `ACCEL_TINT` 0xffe680 (decision on #308), gaze pulse) |

Two rulings of #308. A **lich's drain beam is not drawn when the lich is hidden** (the sight rule outranks the beam, decision on #308). Chain Spark's Zap and the boss death get a client light the server does not send.

**Camera shake** (`fx/shake.ts`: for `ms` a random screen offset of up to `intensity x w` and `intensity x h` px, seeded from the frame count). All triggers are in `RunView3d.event`: a hit on the own hero, a barrel or fireball blast (k 0, 4, 7), a Nova or Frost Nova (k 6, 10), Ground Slam (k 5), a boss's death, and War Cry within two hero radii of the own hero. `groundShift` turns the screen offset into ground tiles; only the drawn camera and the overlay use the shifted camera, the pointer, the follow camera and the effects keep the steady one.

**The force field.** `fieldMaterial(sight, uniforms)` (`fieldMesh.ts`) draws the force field's look: fill `FIELD_FILL` (0x3a8cff) pulsing in alpha (0.22 to 0.32), rising from the floor in `FIELD_FADE_IN` (0.5 s: nothing above the fraction `age / 0.5` of the height), three drifting bands and bright rims (`FIELD_EDGE`, 0x9fd8ff) at the floor and the top. Per-vertex attributes `edge` (1 on the 1 px rim bands) and `y01` (height over `WALL_H`) let one mesh do it; the bands and rims lie over the fill with `overLayer` (the source-over rule, one function with a test, generic over numbers and TSL nodes). The only uniform is `age`, set from `FrameInput.field.age` each frame (`Effects3d.fieldAge`: a door that closes starts it at `tileChanged`; a field there at the start has age `FIELD_OLD` 10 s plus the clock, so no fade-in). Nothing is rebuilt per frame. The glitter and the burst are triangles of the effects (`fx/field.ts`): per sealed cell a stream of motes (30 a second at full detail, life 0.4 to 0.9 s, rising 12 px, 0xbfe8ff / 0xffffff / 0x7fc4ff), and a burst of 40 (`FIELD_BURST`) when the field appears. The field is unlit and reads the sight like the other effects.

**Dev page `?r3d=fx`** (`fxStage.ts`, booted by `bootFx` in `boot.ts`, zoom 4 unless the URL picks one; no server, no login). Every row of `FX_ROWS` is a scenario in its own small room (a still wizard caster, three skeleton targets, two pillars and two torches); a group shows its rows side by side and loops each, through `galleryFrame`, `Figures3d.frame` and the same `Effects3d` and `FxPass` as a run. Groups (panel buttons and `&fx=`): `spells` (runes: wind-up, instant, primary, channel, broken off; Fireball; then one room per game spell, cast by its own figure with its server look, wind-up and mode: Holy Light, Judgement, Haste, Magic Missile, Frost Nova, Frost Shard, Entangle, Charm with a 0.6 s wind-up, the necromancer's Shadow Bolt, the imp's Fire Bolt), `booms`, `shots`, `figures` (swoosh, bite, release sparks, mimic), `bosses` (lich nova, gaze, drain, raise; dragon breath, landing and fireball; demon Ring of Fire; ogre slam), `hazards`, `status`, `objects` (the force field appearing). URL parameters: `&fx=<group>` (default `spells`), `&t=<s>` pins the clock (the same picture each time), `&slow=<n>`, `&at=<slot>` starts the camera over that room, `&torches=0`, and the usual 3D parameters (`quality`, `yaw`, `pitch`, `zoom`, `light`). Panel buttons: the groups, `Slow` (five times slower), `Cast now` (restarts every row), `Torches`. Check shot: `cd tools/e2e && BROWSER=/usr/bin/brave-browser node r3d-shot.mjs http://localhost:5173/ shots "&fx=booms&t=0.25" webgl "" fx`. Tests: `fx/*.test.ts` (counts, bounds, life ends, pure in the clock), `fxStage.test.ts`, `runView3d.test.ts` (the run owns the effects and shakes).

**Lengths.** The approved fireball and frost burst last about 1.7 s and 1 s with floor marks for 3 to 4 s, longer than the 0.6 s they had; triggers and gameplay timing are unchanged. All counts, sizes, colours, lengths and the budgets above are first guesses for the user to judge on real hardware.

## 10. Debug and dev features

The dev pages are `?r3d=<page>` with `<page>` one of `demo`, `gallery`, `lights`, `woodland`, `cove`, `mood`, `sunlight`, `fx` and `liquids` (`r3dPage` in `r3dFlag.ts`, opened by `main.ts` before any login); they need no server and no account. Their panel and URL keys are the dev tools of `render3d/params.ts` and `panel.ts`. A run has the dev tools only for a debug account with `?r3d` in the URL.

| URL | Effect |
|---|---|
| Debug accounts (no URL) | The admin marks a game account in `/admin`; `Welcome.debug` turns the debug UI on (hub free buys and Grant, the waiting room's debug pickers). In a debug run (`RunStartInfo.debug`) every hero starts immortal and the server sends `DebugPath` every 0.5 s (A* to the boss, string-pulled with line of sight, plus `ogre`: the same to the living ogre, empty once it is dead, and `cove`: the same to the walkable cove tile nearest the room's centre, empty while the hero stands in the cove (#232)). Client: a badge at the right edge above the help line (clear of the rules log at the bottom left): a title that says immortal or MORTAL (red), then one row per debug key (`DEBUG_KEYS` in `ui/hud.ts`: F4, F7, F8, F9 and the Shift forms) and per path colour (`DEBUG_PATHS`: yellow to the boss, blue to the ogre, green to the cove). The session keeps the paths, the notice circles and the dodge lines in `DebugDraw`, but no view draws them (§13). There is no in-game toggle for debug mode itself and the game reads no `?debug` or `&boss=` parameter; F9 only switches immortality inside a debug run (`DebugImmortal`). F7 (`DebugIncorporeal`) makes the nearest enemy incorporeal so the rules can be tried on any enemy: it vanishes at once, a hit reveals it, it drifts through walls. Smart behaviour (#220): `DebugPath` also carries `notice`, a thin white circle of the notice distance around every living creature smart enough to dodge (drawn at its interpolated position), and each planned dodge sends `DebugDodge` (from, to; only to debug heroes of a debug run). The host picks the boss in the waiting room. |
| F3 (no URL, every account) | The stats panel; see "The F3 panel" below. |
| Esc menu, Settings, Quality (every account) | High, Balanced (the default) or Fast (`QUALITIES` in `render3d/quality.ts`): the render resolution (§9 "Quality levels"). It is a radio row (`Choice` in `ui/settings.ts`, wired in `render3d/runStage.ts`) next to the rules log and network toggles. The choice applies at once and is kept in this browser (`localStorage` key `ta.quality`, `render3d/qualityPref.ts`; read and written inside try/catch, Balanced when nothing is stored or storage is blocked). The Esc row and the dev panel show and change one value, the host's. |
| `?backend=webgl` (every account) | Forces three.js's WebGL2 fallback instead of WebGPU where the browser offers it (`backend=auto` is the default). The way past a broken WebGPU. |
| `?r3d=demo` | The 3D view's dev scene (#301, §9 "The 3D view"), no server and no login: a torch-lit hall with corridors, figures on cards and a knight that walks with WASD or the arrow keys (turned by the camera's yaw). A compact, see-through panel at the bottom with a Hide button: camera pitch (slider 30° to 80°, buttons 30°, 45°, 48.6°), camera turn (slider, 45° step buttons, North), quality (High, Balanced, Fast), zoom (Auto, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, and a slider up to 48), switches (Wall shadows, Moving light, Smoothed ground), `Lose device (debug)`, and a readout of the backend (WebGPU or WebGL2 fallback), the zoom and the render size. F3 opens the frame times. The address bar follows every change; URL parameters: `pitch`, `yaw` (degrees), `quality=high|balanced|fast`, `zoom=1..48`, `backend=webgl` (force the fallback), `shadows=0`, `orb=0`, `smooth=0`, `stats=1`. The defaults are pitch 48.6° (the user's pick: a tile is 12 whole screen rows), turn 0°, Balanced, everything on except the frame times. Other keys of the address are kept. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose]` loads it headless, prints the backend and any browser errors and saves `r3d.png`; `mode` is `webgl` (default) or `webgpu` (with `HEADED=1` a visible window on the real GPU), and `lose` forces a device loss and saves `r3d-after-loss.png`. |
| `?r3d` in a debug account's run URL | Every run is drawn in the 3D view (§9 "World geometry and the 3D run"); on a debug account (`Welcome.debug`) a `?r3d` in the URL also starts the host in its `run` mode (`startRun3d` in `render3d/boot.ts`), with the dev panel and the dev URL keys of `render3d/params.ts`. Without `?r3d` a debug run looks like a player's run (host mode `play`: `DEFAULTS` plus the stored quality, no panel, the address bar untouched), so the look can be judged without dev rows over the picture; on other accounts `?r3d` with no page is ignored. `lag`, `jitter`, `loss` and `backend=webgl` work for every account. In a `run` host the panel sits at the bottom left and adds a zoom row, the switches Game light, Dark fade and Free camera and the sliders Ambient, Lights and Casters (URL `light=work`, `dark=fade`, `amb`, `lcap`, `ccap`, `free=1`, `see=off`, `seeR`, `seeKeep`, `seeEdge` for the See through row, `quality=high|balanced|fast`, which wins over the stored quality at the start of the run; other keys of the address, such as `lag`, are kept). The hero walks (W A S D, arrows), Esc the menu, Q / E watch teammates after death, F the free camera (movement keys pan it, Shift faster), Z, X, C and the middle mouse button turn the camera, F3 the HUD's panel, F4, F7, F8 and F9 the debug keys (#315). `node tools/e2e/r3d-run.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [level]` (level `dungeon` or `woodland`) registers an `e2e_*` debug account, starts a run with `?r3d&backend=webgl`, walks, toggles the free camera, checks culling on the dungeon, presses F4 twice (the F3 block's `darkvision 50%`, then `100%`) and F9 (`DEBUG · MORTAL`, then `DEBUG · immortal`), with `LOG=1` also L and F8 (a rules-log row within 10 s), prints the number of figures drawn (it must be at least 1 once the run has started; `window.__r3d.figures`), saves `r3d-run-<level>.png` and `-free.png`, deletes the account and prints "no browser errors". |
| `?r3d=lights` | The dungeon's light (#304, §9 "The dungeon's light"), no server and no login: the game's lights in the 3D dungeon, seven torches (one blue, switching), a lava pool, a fireball, a frost bolt, an explosion flash and a knight walking past pillars. V cycles the darkvision 0 / 50 / 100; the light parameters `amb`, `lcap`, `ccap`, `dv`, `light`, `dark`, `shadows` and the 3D keys apply; F3 shows the light and caster counts. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose] lights` shoots it. |
| `?r3d=gallery` | The figures (#303, §9 "The 3D view"), no server and no login: every figure kind cycling through its states, the status-effect looks, chests, barrels, mimics, torches and shots, and a corridor behind a full-height wall for the see-through. The 3D panel applies (pitch, turn, quality, zoom, See through with Off, Radius, Keep and Edge). URL: `state=<name>` pins one state (idle, move, melee, shoot, cast, windup, dash, channel, breath, tail, bite, takeoff, land), `slow=<n>` slows the clock, `focus=corridor\|effects\|objects` starts the camera there, `t=<s>` starts the clock there, `zoom` (default 2), and the 3D keys `pitch`, `yaw`, `quality`, `backend=webgl`, `see=off`, `seeR` (0.5 to 3), `seeKeep` (0 to 1), `seeEdge` (0 to 1). `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose] gallery` shoots it. |
| `?r3d=woodland` | The woodland's light and trees in the 3D view (#306, #329, §9 "Woodland light, shadows, wind and time of day in 3D"), no server and no login: a forest road under the sun and moon of the time of day, with the sun's shadows, cloud shadows, grown trees (the forest edge and the line-ups of leaf amounts, shapes, a dead tree and a willow) that sway and shed leaves, bees and butterflies by day, fireflies and a pixie at night, and a knight to walk with WASD. The 3D panel applies, plus a Time of day slider, the switches Time runs, Wind and Clouds, a Leaves slider with "Own" and a "Plain trees" switch. URL: `hour` (0 to 24, default 9.5), `hourRate` (times 1 game hour per 720 s; 0 stops time), `wind=0`, `clouds=0`, `shadows=0`, `leaf` (0 to 100: forces the leaf amount), `plain=1`, `leafRate` (1 to 1000), `at=x,z` (start tile) and the 3D keys. F3 shows the `sun` and `plants <built> of <total>` lines. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose] woodland` shoots it (`WAIT_MS=3000` lets the trees grow). |
| `?r3d=mood` | The dungeon air (#307, §9 "The air in 3D (#307)"), no server and no login, zoom 2 unless the URL picks one: a small room (`game/moodDemoScript.ts`) with torch smoke, lava smoke and sparks, dust, haze, pool drips, a knight on its loop (dashing every 6 s), an arrow every 3 s and a fireball every 4 s that explodes at the east wall; the knight, arrow and fireball stir the air. The panel shows Stir and Draft next to the Air switches. URL: `dust`, `smoke`, `embers`, `haze`, `drips`, `stir`, `draft` (`=0` switches off), `respawn=<seconds>`, `t`, `slow` and the 3D keys (the `wind` key is the woodland's sway). WASD pans. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose] mood` shoots it. |
| `?r3d=sunlight` | Sunlight through a ceiling crack (#307, §9 "The air in 3D (#307)"), no server and no login, zoom 2 unless the URL picks one: sunlight through a ceiling crack at `SUN` (floor patch, light shaft, dust in the beam), a knight in the patch and one beside it, the alcove's lit torch. Keys (and panel buttons): **2** Second crack, **R** Reroll (new ids, new looks), **V** Vary on/off. URL: `second=1`, `vary=0` and, with `vary=0`, `shape`, `patch`, `slant`, `from`, `clouds`, `often`, `height`, `style`, `rays`, `glow`, `shafts=0`, `dust=0` and the 3D keys. WASD pans. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs ... sunlight` shoots it. |
| `?r3d=cove` | The nymph's cove in the 3D view (#329, §9 "Woodland light, shadows, wind and time of day in 3D", "The cove"), no server and no login, zoom 2 unless the URL picks one: a dark stone room of the real cove's size (26 x 20 tiles, `COVE_STAGE`) with the trunk tile at its middle, the cove's willow and bushes as grown trees, its tufts and ferns as atlas cards, its seven pixies (three above the crown, four round it outside the curtains), two stone pillars with a wall torch each, and a knight to walk with WASD, with the sight on. No sun: the trees are lit by the torches, the pixies and the base light. The 3D keys apply, plus `at=x,z`, `leaf`, `plain=1` and `leafRate` from the URL (the panel's tree rows belong to the sky pages and stay hidden here). `window.__r3d.plants` is `{ built, total }`. |
| `?r3d=fx` | The 3D effects (#308, §9 "Effects in 3D (#308)"), no server and no login, zoom 4 unless the URL picks one: every effect of the design in its own small room (a still wizard caster, three skeleton targets, two pillars, two torches), a group of rows side by side, looping. `&fx=<group>` (`spells` default, `booms`, `shots`, `figures`, `bosses`, `hazards`, `status`, `objects`), `&t=<s>` pins the clock, `&slow=<n>`, `&at=<slot>` (camera over that room), `&torches=0`; panel buttons: the groups, Slow, Cast now, Torches. `window.__r3d` shows the effects' numbers. Check shot: `node r3d-shot.mjs http://localhost:5173/ shots "&fx=booms&t=0.25" webgl "" fx`. |
| `?r3d=liquids` | Sunken water, lava and chasm (#324, #345, #344; §9 "Sunken terrain in 3D (#324)", "Water in 3D (#345)", "Lava in 3D (#344)"), no server and no login, zoom 2 unless the URL picks one: one room with a water pool (shallow rim, deep centre), a lava pool and a chasm, with wading, the lava sink and pushed figures. Keys 1, 2 and 3 push an imp into the chasm, an imp into the deep water and a barrel into the chasm, or the panel's buttons; `&push=chasm\|water\|barrel` starts the push, `&t=<s>` pins the clock, `&refl=0` turns the water's reflections off and `&heat=0` the lava's heat shimmer (quality Fast does both). `window.__r3d.liquids` shows the clock, the things falling, the live ripples and bubbles. Check shot: `node r3d-shot.mjs http://localhost:5173/ shots "&push=chasm&t=1.6" webgl "" liquids`. |
| `?lag=150&jitter=40&loss=2` | Network simulator (every account). |

**3D tools (`tools/e2e/`).** `gpu.mjs` is the browser start line for the real graphics card on the dev notebook (NVIDIA driver 595): with `GPU=1`, `r3d-shot.mjs`, `r3d-run.mjs` and `r3d-trees.mjs` launch a **visible** window (`headless: false`; on a locked screen it presents about one frame a second, so unlock it first) with `--use-angle=vulkan`, `--enable-features=Vulkan,DefaultANGLEVulkan,VulkanFromANGLE`, `--enable-unsafe-webgpu`, `--ozone-platform=x11`, flags that keep an occluded window drawing at full rate, and the environment `__VK_LAYER_NV_optimus=NVIDIA_only`; `GPU=uncapped` also lifts the frame limit (`--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit`), for frame-rate figures. After the first frames each script prints `gpu: WebGL2 <renderer>; WebGPU <adapter>` (`printGpu`), the way to check which card really draws. Without `GPU` the scripts keep their headless SwiftShader launch. Under `GPU`, `r3d-shot.mjs` drops the browser flags its `mode` would pick; the backend is then the page's (WebGPU unless the extra query says `backend=webgl`), and its last argument also takes `cove` (`?r3d=cove`). **`r3d-trees.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [view ...]`** takes one picture per named view of `?r3d=woodland` (WebGL2, `quality=high`, the panel hidden, default output folder `shots/trees-329`): `leaf` (the five leaf amounts), `shapes` (round, wide and tall), `plain` (the same as flat silhouettes), `dead`, `willow`, `forest08` and `forest13` (the forest at 08:00 and 13:00); each view is a start tile (`at`), a zoom and parameters. It waits until `window.__r3d.plants` says every tree is built (`WAIT_MS`, default 120000) and prints `<view>: <built> of <total> trees` and "no browser errors". Env: `GPU`, `BROWSER`, `R3D_W` / `R3D_H` (1600 x 900), `R3D_BASE_QUERY` (extra parameters for every view). **`r3d-run.mjs`** on the woodland waits for the F3 line `plants <built> of <total>`: all trees on the GPU, but at least `min(40, total)` in software (about two frames a second there; the wait is only pacing, and the walk, the turn and the click get four times their time), prints `trees built x of y` and the forest's build time with its longest frame, and `BACKEND=webgpu` picks WebGPU (default WebGL2), `R3D_W` / `R3D_H` the window (1280 x 768), `R3D_QUERY` more page parameters (e.g. `"&quality=high"`), `WAIT_MS` the time allowed for the forest. `FPS_S=10` samples every animation frame for that many seconds and prints the median frame, the frames over 200 ms, the mean fps, the 1 % low, the longest frame, and the draw calls and triangles per frame (all passes; it hooks WebGL2 and WebGPU draws), then the F3 block and `r3d-run-<level>-fps.png`; `FPS_ONLY=1` ends the run after that. The frame-rate command: `cd tools/e2e && GPU=uncapped BROWSER=/usr/bin/brave-browser FPS_S=10 FPS_ONLY=1 BACKEND=webgpu R3D_W=2560 R3D_H=1280 R3D_QUERY="&quality=high" node r3d-run.mjs http://localhost:8080/ shots/trees woodland` (and `BACKEND=webgl`).

**The F3 panel** (#291, #314). F3, or Network stats in the Esc menu's Settings, opens it for every account. The frame lines come from `FrameTimer` (`game/frameTimer.ts`), which runs only while the panel is open: closed, its `lap` returns at once and nothing is counted. Every time is an exponential average (0.9 old, 0.1 new; the first frame after opening seeds it) of main-thread CPU time; the GPU works asynchronously, so its own cost shows only where the CPU waits for it (a pixel readback, a texture upload).
- `frame`: the whole host frame, `stepStart` to `renderEnd` (`render3d/host.ts`: the stage's step and the draw).
- `render`: `renderStart` to `renderEnd`: the scene pass and the copy to the canvas.
- `update`: `RunSession.frame` alone (the fixed steps, the view's frame, the HUD call).
- The indented lines are the laps of the frame (`timer.lap(name)` books the time since the previous lap; `R3D_SYSTEMS` in `render3d/stats.ts`): `sim` (the session's step up to the view: fixed steps, interpolation, prediction, the own shots' flight, the use hint's pick; in the dev pages the stage's own step), `cards` (the per-frame card, flame and glow quads), `plants` (the trees' build and meshes), `lights` (the light list), `sight` (the mask's refill), `air` (the air's step and quads), `sun` (the sun's pass), `shafts`, `mirror` (the water's mirror pass), `upload` (the render plan, the readout line, and the buffer and uniform updates before the scene pass), `models` (the figure models), `scene` (the scene pass alone), `present` (the copy to the canvas; 0 on High) and `hud` (`Hud.frame`). The session books `sim` and `hud`, the stage and `RunView3d.frame` the rest. A new system gets a name in `R3D_SYSTEMS` and a lap after its code.
- After the laps come the lines the run adds (`lights <list> of <inView> (cap <lcap>)`, `shadows <casters> cards (cap <ccap>)`, in a woodland run `sun <hour> <px>` and `plants <built> of <total>`, and `fx <records> rec, <tris> tris (<dropped> dropped total)`), then `objects` (three's scene objects, `View.meshes`), `draw calls` (three's `info.render.drawCalls` of the last frame, scene and copy), `canvas` (the canvas's pixel size) and `render <w>x<h> (<quality>)`, `zoom` and `backend` (#314), then, in a run, ping, jitter, interpolation delay, snapshot bytes, prediction correction, server ms per tick, entities and darkvision.
